## 1. MEASURED (probe, page 1; log: session scratchpad `probe_p1.log`, script `probe.py`)
- PDF is vector, 0 raster images, text embedded (115 words on p1: grid A–E / 1–5, D1–D3, W1–W4, G1–G10, SWS, CR, CT, CP, FG, MR, scale 1:100, sheet WD-1/01).
- Geometry is NOT entity-level: 37,907 paths / 109,766 segments; 97% < 3 pt; median 0.5 pt; only 287 segments ≥ 30 pt.
  Curves/arcs/hatch are flattened polylines. No layers, no blocks. Strokes: widths 1/6 (+3,5).
- Long H/V lines are fragmented into 2–4 parallel/collinear pieces (wall = pair of lines + hatch, not one entity).
- Room names are NOT on p1 (seen on plumbing sheet p7: BILIK MANDI, RUANG BASUH).
- Ground-truth candidates in repo (NOT yet checked against this PDF): `examples/tb_lktn_*.dsl`, `database/TB_LKTN_2S.db`
  (name says 2-storey; the PDF may be single-storey — reconcile before using as oracle).
- Prior attempts (user, 2026-11-03): "bad in OCR and shape recognition". Consequence for this spec: NO OCR (text is embedded and read as words+bbox) and NO learned/raster shape recognition (shapes = deterministic geometry on vector paths). A raster-only PNG input would reintroduce both failure modes and stays out of scope.

## 2. Witness claims (written BEFORE code; each names the issue it proves/disproves)
| ID | Claim | Issue it exposes | Must be able to say |
|----|-------|------------------|---------------------|
| W-P1 | Text words + bboxes extracted per page equal `pdftotext -bbox` count | extraction drops/duplicates text | VACUOUS if 0 words |
| W-P2 | Collinear-merge reduces segments; total drawn length conserved ±0.1% | merge loses/invents ink | NO-OP if count unchanged |
| W-P3 | Hatch regions (dense sw6 micro-segments) removed; removed-ink % logged per region | hatch mistaken for wall | INCONCLUSIVE if no region found |
| W-P4 | Grid lines located; letter/number bubbles bound to them by text position; spacing in pt → mm via scale label 1:100 and A4 pt→mm | scale/units unverified | WRONG if spacing ≠ any value in `tb_lktn_grid.dsl` |
| W-P5 | Wall candidates = parallel-line pairs at one thickness; thickness histogram printed | pairing invents walls | scope-blind: log unpaired long lines |
| W-P6 | Openings: D/W tag text → nearest gap/arc; count per tag equals schedule on p8 | tag binding wrong | WRONG if count ≠ schedule |
| W-P7 | Result vs oracle: wall length, opening counts, grid spacing deltas | whole chain | PASS only with delta table |

## 3. Method (deterministic, no ML)
`pdftocairo -svg` per page (poppler present; PyMuPDF/pdfplumber NOT installed) → flatten transforms → merge → de-hatch → grid → wall pairing → opening binding → emit DXF-equivalent entities for the existing DXF2BIM lane (`~/bim-ootb/prompts/DXF2BIM_ARCHITECT_REVIEW.md`, edit via /tmp worktree only).
Logging: `§PDF_*` lines every stage (counts in/out, ink length, thresholds used). Thresholds come from measured histograms, never hand-picked.

## 4. Out of scope until W-P1..W-P7 run on p1
PNG/raster input (needs a separate raster path); other sheets; Text2BIM/LLM approaches (project positioning: no AI inside).

## 7. BUILDING TYPE = A PRIOR THAT NARROWS SHAPE + LAYER GUESSING (user, 2026-11-03)
> *"What I mean by type is that it narrows down the guess work of identifying its shape and layers."*
- Type is therefore a **constraint table the recogniser consults**, not a label. For `LowCostHouse` (single storey, JKR TB-LKTN family) it would list what MAY appear, so everything else is rejected or flagged instead of guessed.
- **Existing mechanism (read 2026-11-03):** `~/bim-ootb/modeller/dxf2library_types.js` (`offered(rows,type)`, spec DXF2BIM_MODELLER_LANE.md §L23) already narrows LIBRARY records by `building_types` (typed first, then General). Layer guessing for Shed is manual `@LAYER=Rod,fan` (DXF2BIM_FOUNDATION_LANE.md l.171). So today's type narrows candidates only; the shape/layer prior for a PDF is NEW and must reuse that tagging style, not a second engine.
- **Every prior must be EXTRACTED, never recalled** (Prime Rule). Sources, in order: (a) this PDF's own p8 schedule/legend (D1–D3 door types, W1–W4, G1–G10, finish codes FFL/SWS/CR/CT/CP/FG/MR as printed); (b) `examples/tb_lktn_house.dsl` once checked; (c) measured histograms from p1 (stroke widths, wall thickness).
- **Where the prior enters (each is a witness, not an assumption):**
  | Stage | Prior supplied by type | Replaces which guess |
  |---|---|---|
  | W-P3 hatch | stroke-width class that is wall fill (sw 6 vs 1), measured | density-only Otsu |
  | W-P4 grid | expected axis set A–E x 1–5 (printed on sheet) | number of grid lines |
  | W-P5 walls | wall thickness from the pair histogram, not a constant | parallel-pair tolerance |
  | W-P6 openings | tag vocabulary D1–D3 / W1–W4 + counts from p8 | arc/gap shape matching |
  | layers | stroke width -> pseudo-layer table (sw -> role), printed and checked | no layers in PDF |
- **Failure rule (the earlier-attempt lesson):** a prior may REJECT an unmatched shape; it must never fabricate one. Unmatched ink is logged as `§PDF_UNCLASSIFIED n= ink=` so a type that is too narrow shows up as a number, not as silent loss.

## 8. LESSONS FROM THE EARLIER ATTEMPT (read 2026-11-03: `docs/archive/tb-lktn-vocabulary-extraction.md`, Google Vision OCR of these same 8 sheets, 2026-01-30)
What it was: OCR → vocabulary tables (room names, grid, D/W schedule, MEP points, finish codes). Also `docs/internal/PDF_TERRAIN.md` (survey PDF/PNG → Vision OCR → 689 elevation points): OCR of isolated numbers on a survey worked as a POC; OCR as the source for a building's dimensions did not.
**Measured against this PDF's embedded text layer (p8 schedule, pdftotext -layout):**
| Item | OCR doc (Jan) | PDF text layer (exact) |
|---|---|---|
| D1 / D2 / D3 size | 900x2100 / 800x2100 / 700x2100 | 900x2100 / 900x2100 / **750**x2100 |
| D1 / D2 / D3 units | not stated | 2 / 3 / 2 NOS (locations: Ruang Tamu+Dapur / Bilik Utama,2,3 / Bilik Mandi+Tandas) |
| W1 / W2 / W3 size | 1200x1200 / 1800x1200 / 600x600 | **1800x1000** / **1200x1000** / **600x500** (W1,W2 SWAPPED vs OCR) |
| W1 / W2 / W3 units | not stated | 1 / 4 / 2 NOS |
| W4 | 600x900 louvre | NOT in the p8 schedule text (tag appears on p1; its schedule row is elsewhere or absent — unresolved) |
| Grid spacing X | 750,2300,3100,3100,750 (sum 10000) but "length 9900" | internally inconsistent by 100 |
| Grid spacing Y | 750,3100,3100,3700,750 (sum 11400) but "width 8500" | internally inconsistent by 2900 |
Lessons (each becomes a rule of this lane):
1. **OCR-derived numbers are rejected as priors.** Only the PDF text layer (exact) or a witness-measured vector value qualifies. The OCR doc stays a hint list of WHICH terms exist (room names, abbreviations, MEP legend), never of values.
2. **The vocabulary is reusable and is already the type's prior skeleton:** MASTER_BEDROOM/BEDROOM/BATHROOM/LIVING/DINING/KITCHEN/WET_KITCHEN/CAR_PORCH/VERANDAH (Malay→SpaceType), `Malaysian_Residential` profile, validation rules. W-P6 oracle for openings = the PDF's own schedule: **doors 2+3+2 = 7, windows 1+4+2 = 7 (+W4 unresolved)**.
3. **Dimension numbers are not in the text layer of p1** (only "100" found as a number on p1/p3): the plan's dimension digits are not readable as text. Grid spacing must therefore be MEASURED from vector grid-line positions (pt → mm via 1:100), never read, and checked for self-consistency (sum of spacings == overall) — the check the OCR doc failed.
4. **Self-consistency is a witness:** every extracted table must pass an arithmetic check (sums, counts vs schedule). A table that cannot be cross-checked is INCONCLUSIVE, not data.

## 9. RESULTS 2026-11-03 (continued): W-P4 grid on page 1 (`extract_p1.py`, log `extract_p1.log` in session scratchpad)
- **Role table (first row, MEASURED):** stroke width **3** = long cut-wall lines (41 raw segments on p1) — NOT thin grid centrelines. They come in PAIRS 2.8 pt apart = **99 mm @1:100 (wall thickness ≈ 100 mm)**: X pairs (161.8,164.6) (351.4,354.2) (437.8,440.6) + single 248.2; Y pairs (174.6,177.3) (216.3,219.1) (261.0,263.7) (347.4,350.1). This is W-P5 evidence arriving early: wall thickness comes from the pair gap, as §7 required.
- **Label binding:** bubble labels sit on the CENTRE of the wall pair (A: label 163.2 = pair centre 163.2). Bound within 6 pt: A C D E, 2 3 4 5. **UNBOUND, reported not guessed:** B (no sw3 line near x≈199.5), 1 (label y 414.3, nearest line 64 pt away). So the type prior "A–E x 1–5 all present" is FALSE for the sw3 class — B and 1 are drawn with another stroke class or not at all. Open: find them (W-P4c).
- **Overall A..E = 9835 mm @1:100** (pitch 1 pt = 35.28 mm); the OCR doc's "length 9900" is within 0.7 % — plausible, but the OCR figure stays unverified (§8 rule 1).
- **My own check was VACUOUS:** "sum of spacings == overall" is a tautology when spacings are differences of the same lines. Logged `self_consistent True` proves nothing; the real consistency test must compare against an independent number (printed dimension chain or schedule).
- **Oracle FAILS:** `examples/tb_lktn_grid.dsl` spacing X 2.3+5.0+3.1+3.1 = 13.5 m and Y 3.1+3.1+4.4+3.2 = 13.8 m vs measured 9.84 m (A–E) and 6.10 m (rows 5..2). Ratios 1.37 and 2.26 — not one scale factor, so it is not a scaling problem. `tb_lktn_grid.dsl` is NOT usable as oracle for this PDF (header says "extracted from PDF dimensions", i.e. OCR-era). Oracle for W-P4/W-P7 must now be the PDF itself (printed dimension chain, if readable as vectors) — Open Q4.
- **Open Q4:** are the printed dimension digits drawn as vector glyph paths (the 393 `<use>` glyph references) rather than text? If yes they can be matched by glyph identity (exact, no OCR) to read the dimension chain — the deterministic route that avoids the January failure.

## 10. Q4 ANSWERED (2026-11-03, `scripts/pdf2bim/glyphprobe.py`, p1): the plan's inner text is STROKED VECTOR TEXT, not font text
- The text layer holds only schedule/legend/title text (font glyphs, 393 `<use>`). Room names, areas and dimension digits inside the plan are **drawn as polylines** (single-stroke CAD font flattened by the PDF export). That is exactly why OCR was needed in January and why it misread.
- Measured: 3,141 small multi-point paths (bbox 1.5–9 pt, >=4 pts; sw6 1,702 / sw1 1,371 / sw5 68). **106 character-row clusters** (3+ glyph paths, height 2–7 pt, width >= 6 pt), e.g. a 29.4 x 4.2 pt row of 44 paths at (231,174) and again at (282,347) — identical width/height, i.e. the same string twice. Several rows repeat at 6.6 x 2.0 pt (13 paths each) at 5+ places.
- Glyph IDENTITY is therefore usable: 1,041 distinct path shapes among 3,141, with the top shapes repeating 157/130/110/100/87... times (translation-only signature, no scaling). **Not yet done: decoding** — the alphabet is unlabelled.
- **Labelling anchor proposed (no OCR, no external table): geometry labels the digits.** A dimension's printed value ~= the measured distance between its extension lines (e.g. wall-pair centres, §9). Take each chain segment whose measured length (pt x 35.28 mm) is known, take the glyph row at its midpoint, and assign that row's glyph classes the digits of the nearest multiple of 10 mm... verified by requiring the SAME class to receive the SAME digit across >= 3 independent dimensions (else `INCONCLUSIVE`). Room names are then decoded by the same alphabet extended with letters from the p7/p8 text layer (known strings at known places).
- Witness to add (W-P8): decoded dimension chain sums to the decoded overall dimension AND equals the measured overall within 1 % — the independent consistency check §9 said was missing.

## 11. CORRECTION to §10 (2026-11-03, `rowprobe.py` over the cluster at (231,174)) — §10 OVERSTATED
- The "29.4 x 4.2 pt row of 44 paths" (and its twin at (282,347)) is NOT shown to be text. Its parts: 74-point paths repeated at 0.36 pt y-offsets (an overdrawn/hatched circle or fill) and CONCENTRIC 5-point squares 2.76/2.52/2.28 pt (a hatched square) — i.e. a hatched SYMBOL, drawn by stacking offset outlines. Same for the many 6.6 x 2.0 rows (unexamined).
- So "106 character-row clusters" = rows of small paths, text OR symbol. §10's claim "inner text is stroked vector text" is **UNPROVEN for room names and dimension digits**; the only fact held is that those strings are absent from the text layer.
- Also withdrawn: "1,041 distinct shapes / top shapes repeat 157x" as evidence of a character alphabet; much of the repetition is hatch/overdraw, not letters.
- NEXT must find a place where text is certain before decoding: (a) the dimension chain — locate dimension lines/ticks by geometry (sw class, parallel to grid, outside the plan), look only there; (b) the title-block "scale 1:100" / sheet number, whose characters are KNOWN strings, as a calibration sample for what a stroked character looks like in this export.

## 12. TEMPLATE-MATCHER STRATEGY (owner, 2026-11-03: "a template matcher strategy which I always propose from those early days … a certain set of info must be present. The unclear ones can be yellow marked")
Owner also states: the room names / dimensions / areas ARE on the sheet (drawn, not in the text layer). §11's doubt was about my cluster, not about their existence.
**Method:** the type (§7) supplies the set of things that MUST be present; the recogniser only tries to FIND those (match-against-expected), never to read freely. Outcome per expected item: **GREEN** (matched, count/identity agree), **YELLOW** (found but unclear, or count/identity disagree, or expected-but-absent, or present-but-unexpected), never silently dropped, never invented. Yellow reuses the DXF2BIM yellow-list/amber-editable convention (`~/bim-ootb/prompts/FILM_DXF2BIM_YELLOW_MARKINGS.md`) so the user resolves it in the existing UI; a resolved yellow becomes a stored template of the type (teach once, reuse).
**Step 0 DONE (text-layer tokens): `scripts/pdf2bim/type_checklist.py`, log `checklist.log`.** Expected set = the PDF's own p8 schedule (exact); found set = tags on p1 with the legend column removed (column of >=4 vocabulary words sharing x±3 pt — derived, not hard-coded):
| tag | schedule | plan p1 | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 / D2 / D3 | 2 / 3 / 2 | 2 / 3 / 2 | GREEN x3 — doors 7 = 7 |
| W1 / W2 | 1 / 4 | 1 / 4 | GREEN x2 |
| W3 | 2 | **4** (+1 legend) | **YELLOW count_mismatch** (unexplained; not guessed) |
| W4 | absent | 0 on plan, 1 legend | **YELLOW not_in_schedule** (also OCR doc's W4 row unconfirmed) |
Windows: schedule 7, plan 9 (the W3 excess is the whole difference). W-P6 therefore reports GREEN on doors, YELLOW on windows — an honest result the earlier OCR could not produce.
**Step 1 (next, not built): same match-against-expected for the DRAWN strings.** Expected list from the type vocabulary (Malay room names, `m²`, dimension values) + measured geometry (§9: wall-pair centre distances predict the dimension values). Candidate regions come from the stroked small-path clusters (§10/§11, symbols excluded by a shape test); matching = normalised ink-bitmap template distance; a candidate that beats a margin against exactly one expected item is GREEN, a close call or no match is YELLOW. Templates are seeded where truth is certain (known title-block strings, geometry-predicted dimensions confirmed across >=3 instances, then user-taught yellows) — never from a free-reading recogniser.
**Witness claims added:** W-P9 every expected item has a verdict (no silent absence); W-P10 template match never GREEN on an item with no ink candidate (NO-OP/VACUOUS guard); W-P11 unexpected ink clusters are counted as `§PDF_UNCLASSIFIED`, so a type that is too narrow shows as a number.

- Done: `scripts/pdf2bim/types/low_cost_house.json` — type, storeys, sheet scale, tag patterns -> IFC class (`D\d+`=IfcDoor, `W\d+`=IfcWindow, both marked `source: owner 2026-11-03`), schedule labels (REFERENCES / UNITS / NOS), legend-detection parameters, and `vocabulary_unclassified` (G#, SWS, MR, FG, CP, GB, CR, CT, TB, FP#: seen but MEANING NOT EXTRACTED, so no class is assigned — they only help find legend columns).

Witness for the refactor: `diff` of SCHEDULE/PLAN lines printed `SAME_AS_HARDCODED`.

## 17. ROSETTA DICTIONARY (owner, 2026-11-03: *"Thus it is a Rosetta Stone dictionary you can infer in hindsight"*)
This PDF is a SOLVED example of its type: authored once, in hindsight, by reading the sheets (multimodal read, owner-accepted §16). The product is a dictionary of drawing marks -> meaning, **`scripts/pdf2bim/types/low_cost_house_rosetta.json`** (companion of the §13 marking template `low_cost_house.json`), each entry naming the numeric witness that corroborates it. It is the same pattern as the repo's Rosetta Stone gates (known answer -> mined rules), applied to 2D drawings.
**Dictionary content read (all 8 sheets viewed at 170 dpi; p1 plan at 330/450 dpi):** sheet/title-block fields; grid A..E = 0/1300/3100/6800/9900, rows 5..1 = 0/1500/3100/6200/8500 (overall 9900 x 8500; dimension chains 1300,3100,3700,3100,9900 and 1500,1600,3100,3100,2300); **10 rooms** with class, grid rectangle, FFL-from-AGL, floor/wall finish codes, printed area; room-tag format (2x2: FFL|area over floor|wall finish); plan-symbol table (sink 1, basin 1, WC 1, shower 1, floor trap 3, tap 4, gully trap 2); **notes G1-G10** (triangle tags keyed to the p3 table: G1 RC beams/slabs/footing, G2 soil pipe to IST, G3 waste pipe, G4 manhole, G5 230 mm dia half-p.c. PERIMETER DRAIN, G6 250 gal water tank, G7 waterproofing 1500, G8 damp-proof membrane, G9 50 dia vent pipe, G10 r.c. working counter top); roof (metal, 25 deg, 700 mm eaves, E-W ridge on grid 3 + N-S porch gable); perimeter drain (G5; nested outline round the L-shaped footprint incl. porch, DISCHARGE arrows mid-side, MH1 manhole NW).
**Witness results (`rosetta_verify.py`, log `rosetta_verify.log`):**
- **G1 PASS:** the text-layer positions of the grid bubble labels reproduce my READ dimension chains. X: fit 35.870 mm/pt, interior residuals B 0.00, C 0.01, D 0.08 pt; Y: 35.847 mm/pt, interior residuals 2 0.04, 3 -0.04, 4 -0.04 pt; X and Y scales agree (0.06 %). (End points are the fit anchors, so only interior residuals are evidence.) This also resolves §9: grid lines B and 1 exist (not sw3); their positions are predicted, not unbound. Earlier "1:100 => 35.28 mm/pt" was 1.7 % off: the sheet is printed at 35.87.
- **R1 MATCH 8 of 8 readable:** grid-cell area (dx*dy) equals the printed room area for TANDAS 1.95, RUANG BASUH 2.88, DAPUR 8.25, RUANG MAKAN 5.92, BILIK 3/2/UTAMA 9.61, RUANG TAMU 11.47. BILIK MANDI (computed 2.08) and ANJUNG (7.36) printed values were not legible in my read -> `NO_PRINTED_READ`, not asserted. Coincidence is implausible: eight independent products matched two-decimal values.
- Corrects the January OCR doc: its X/Y spacing lists were garbled but its TOTALS (9900 x 8500) were right.
**Discerned, NOT yet witnessed (YELLOW):** roof tags MR/HC/BC/VT meaning beyond MR; ridge/valley layout; perimeter drain: the nested outline gap measured 276 / 176 mm, not the stated 230 mm dia (a half-pipe cross-section may not equal the line gap) — unresolved; ANJUNG FFL; p7 tags the tank G10 while p3 says G6 (drafter inconsistency).
**Consequence for the plan:** step 1 (drawn-text matcher) is mostly unnecessary for THIS sheet: the dictionary + grid fit already predict every label/dimension/area, so the job becomes VERIFY (find the mark at its predicted place) instead of READ. The matcher is for new drawings of the same type, where the dictionary supplies the expected strings and the yellow list shows departures.

## 18. WALLS + OPENINGS on p1 (`scripts/pdf2bim/walls_p1.py`, log `walls.log`) — PARTIAL
- **W-P5 PASS for what sw3 holds:** 41 sw3 segments -> 18 distinct lines -> **19 wall pairs, ALL 100 mm thick** (histogram {100: 19}; gap 2.8 pt x 35.87 mm/pt; the old 99 mm used the nominal scale). **19 of 19 pairs sit within 1.5 pt of a grid axis**: X axes A, D, E offset 0.00/-0.03/-0.02 pt; Y axes 2,3,4,5 offset -1.20..-1.27 pt (one consistent bias: the Y bubble-label text centre sits ~1.2 pt (~43 mm) above its axis — a label-placement fact, not a geometry error; X labels have no such bias).
- **Coverage is NOT complete (honest NO):** sw3 pairs exist only on axes A, D, E and 2, 3, 4, 5. NO sw3 pair on B, C or row 1; interior partitions and the porch walls are drawn another way (the plan shows ladder/tick hatch strips, i.e. sw6 hatch pairs). Total sw3 pair length 38.84 m is therefore a LOWER bound.
- **W-P6b FAIL:** gaps >= 300 mm along sw3 wall pairs = **4** (H2 997 mm, H3 4799 mm, H5 1550 mm, VD 1919 mm) vs 14 expected openings (7 doors + 7 windows). Windows/doors sit in walls whose openings are not gaps in the sw3 outline (they are drawn by symbols/hatch, e.g. the ladder strips and swing arcs). Reported as `NOT EQUAL`, not forced.
- **Next (this is where the product work is):** classify the sw6 ladder/tick hatch strips as walls (pairs of parallel long lines with periodic ticks at the same 100 mm thickness), add the door swing arcs (quarter-circle 'C' paths, radius = door width) and window ladders as opening evidence, then re-run W-P6b with the expected 7 + 7 (+2 columns) from the checklist. The grid and the dictionary now predict where each should be, so each missing one is a named yellow (e.g. "D2 near BILIK 3 expected at C-D x 4-3 row; no arc found").

## 19. OPENINGS FROM GEOMETRY — W-P6 PASS on p1 (`scripts/pdf2bim/openings_p1.py`, log `openings.log`) — supersedes §18's FAIL
- **How the plan draws them (measured, now in the dictionary `opening_drawing_conventions`):** walls are chains of 100 x 100 mm closed squares (379 cells at 100 mm, 12 at 90 mm); **a window is a chain of 100 x 500 mm closed rectangles at 600 mm pitch along a wall axis** (13 modules); **a door is a quarter-circle swing arc** drawn as cubic Bezier(s) (the earlier arc test failed because my parser kept only Bezier endpoints and measured the chord as arc length; fixed to test chord = r*sqrt2 and r x r span).
- **Windows, from geometry alone (tags not used):** chains 1 / 2 / 3 modules = 600 / 1200 / 1800 mm = **W3 x2, W2 x4, W1 x1 = schedule 2 / 4 / 1 exactly (MATCH)**; centres land on the plan's W tags (e.g. W1 chain centre 284.5 pt vs tag 284; W3 chain on axis A at y 238.6 vs tag y 238). So the 2 extra W3 tags = columns (§15) is now confirmed from the other side.
- **Doors:** 8 arcs. Parameter-free one-to-one assignment (closest tag/arc pairs first) binds **D1 x2 (r 820 mm), D2 x3 (r 820 mm), D3 x2 (r 669 mm) = schedule 2 / 3 / 2 exactly (MATCH)**, distances 4.3-7.0 pt (D1/D2) and 12.0 pt (D3). The arc radius is the nominal width minus a constant frame offset: **D1 80, D2 80, D3 81 mm** (900 -> 820, 750 -> 669) — one consistent number across both width classes, i.e. a real drawing convention.
- **Left over, named YELLOW:** one arc r 559 mm at (172.5,227.0) sw1 — thin-line, not within reach of any schedule door tag (34.7 pt from the nearest) = a fixture swing in BILIK MANDI (shower screen?), not a door. Not forced into the schedule.
- **Note on the 12.0 pt D3 distance:** an earlier fixed 12 pt cut-off dropped one D3 on a float edge (6 of 7 bound); replaced by the assignment above rather than raising a threshold.
- **Status of the wall/opening lane on p1:** openings DONE (14 of 14 from geometry, + 2 columns, + 1 named non-door arc). Walls: sw3 pairs DONE for axes A, D, E, 2-5 (§18); the 100 mm CELL CHAINS give the remaining walls (axes B, C, row 1, partitions, porch) — **next: group the 379 cells into wall runs, snap to the grid, add them to the 19 pairs, then emit wall + opening entities** (the DXF-equivalent output for the DXF2BIM lane).

Run: `node ~/bim-ootb/modeller/dxf2bim_cli.js --sheets p1_marked.dxf --out <scratch> --accept-suggestions --set LowCostHouse --level 0 --layer plan:PDF-WALL=wall --layer plan:PDF-WINDOW=opening --layer plan:PDF-COLUMN=ignore --layer plan:PDF-DOOR-SWING=ignore --layer plan:PDF-GRID=ignore --layer plan:PDF-TAG=ignore` (logs `d2b.log`, `d2b2.log`; run only against bim-ootb read-only, output in the session scratchpad).
- **Reads our DXF cleanly:** `§ACCOUNT entities=133 dropped=none` (122 drawing + 11 mark texts); `@UNITS=mm`, `@SET`, `@LEVEL=0` accepted; all 6 layers typed; the sheet is a PLAN once it carries markings: `@VIEW=PLAN` (TEXT on layer `DXF2BIM-MARK`, format copied from the shed's marked sheet) **and `@GRIDX=A..E` / `@GRIDY=5..1`** at the axis coordinates from the Rosetta dictionary (without >=2 of each: `refused ... missing GRIDX(>=2),GRIDY(>=2)` — the first refusal, fixed by the dictionary's grid).
- **Refusal 1 — `no elevation (@VIEW=ELEV + @FACE)`:** the lane builds 3D from plan + elevations; a plan alone has no heights. The PDF has them (p4, p5 elevations, p6 section). This is the real next work: the same Rosetta approach on p4/p5/p6 (level markers FFL, sill, head, ceiling, ridge; opening heights; roof pitch 25 deg, eaves 700 mm) -> elevation DXF sheets with `@VIEW=ELEV @FACE=...`.
- **Refusal 2 — `mixed sets are not supported yet: body kinds and wall/slab/opening/roof layers are typed together`:** raised by `dxf2structure.js judge()` whenever any layer typed wall/opening/glazing... exists (even with columns and door swings set to `ignore`). The CLI is the STRUCTURE chain (its own header says so); the wall/slab/opening pipeline is the Modeller page path (`dxf2bim_import.js`, variant A). Whether the CLI can be driven down that path with a plan + elevations set (the shed/duplex recipe) is NOT yet established; to read, not guess: `dxf2bim_import.js` gate vs `dxf2structure.js` judge dispatch. Not resolved this turn.
- **Also not yet emitted** (items the lane wants): door OPEN-leaf line (else `GAP:door_closed_unknown`), door/window heights (elevations), column typing for the 3 rectangles (2 real W3 columns + 1 unexplained).
- **State:** plan side of the pipeline is DONE and witnessed (grid, walls 46.12 m, 7 windows, 7 doors, 2 columns, round trip 122/122). The lane gate is blocked on elevations, not on plan quality.

## 27. DOOR FAMILIES BECOME BINDABLE (owner 2026-11-04 "yes"; spec BEFORE code) — migration `CL006_opening_family_size_pair.sql`
- **Issue (measured §25/§28):** the library already holds the exact-size doors (Terminal `FD1:(PTp01b) 900 x 2100 @PT02` x7 and `750 x 2100` x15, with meshes) but the importer cannot bind them: `familyToRef` (dxf2shell.js l.533) needs a `W x H` pair in the family DESCRIPTION whose key equals the geometry row's element_ref pair key AND whose leading token equals the element_ref prefix before ':'. `INST_FIRE_DOOR_900` ("FD1 fire-rated single leaf 900mm (thinner frame)") and `INST_FIRE_DOOR_750` have no pair -> `GAP:no_lod400_geometry` hard fail.
- **Fix = data, not code, not a binary DB commit (CLAUDE.md DB rule):** append-only migration updating exactly those two descriptions, guarded by the old text (idempotent): `FD1 900×2100mm fire-rated single leaf (thinner frame)` and `FD1 750×2100mm fire-rated single leaf for escape routes`. Token `FD1`, key `900x2100` / `750x2100`.
- **Witness claims (each must be able to say NO-OP / WRONG):** W-DOOR1 `lod400` hard-fail rows for doors: 0 (before: 2+). W-DOOR2 every door fill reports no `fill_size_mismatch`, or `delta_w = delta_h = 0` (before: -4%/-3% and +1.6%/-3%). W-DOOR3 element counts do not drop (53 or more; 5 doors still placed). W-DOOR4 the migration is idempotent (second run changes 0 rows) and touches exactly 2 rows. Sandbox first on a patched COPY of the exported library index (scratchpad), then the migration on a COPY of `library/component_library.db`, never the original.
- **Binding change:** `types/low_cost_house_rosetta.json -> library_binding`: D1 and D2 -> `INST_FIRE_DOOR_900` (exact 900x2100), D3 -> `INST_FIRE_DOOR_750` (exact 750x2100). Semantics stay YELLOW: the library's fire-rated institutional door stands in for an unrated house door; the size is exact, the type is not.

## 27b. RESULT of §27 (2026-11-04)
- **Migration `migration/CL006_opening_family_size_pair.sql`**: 2 rows changed on the first apply, 0 on the second (idempotent), `ad_opening_family` count unchanged (68). Applied to a COPY of `library/component_library.db` only (`run_p1.sh` step `10a_library_migrate`: `before 0 after 2`); the export from the copy gives the SAME lane result as the patched-index sandbox (58 elements both ways).
- **W-DOOR1 PASS:** no door `GAP:no_lod400_geometry` hard fail (INST_FIRE_DOOR_900/750 now bind to `FD1:(PTp01b) 900 x 2100 @PT02` / `750 x 2100`).
- **W-DOOR2 PARTIAL (honest):** of the 7 schedule doors: 2 exterior D1 EXACT (no mismatch row, height 2.1 m from the elevation); 1 D3 exact width (dw 0, dh -0.076 = the lane's head-frame convention); 2 D2 on grid line D dw +0.080 (the PDF's clear-opening radius 820 mm is kept there: the drawn wall gap only fits it; the schedule width 900 would overshoot the wall piece); 1 D2 in the 4.7 m open-plan gap (dw -3.79: the gap is door + open plan, not a door); 1 D3 `fill_no_void`. Before: 0 exact.
- **W-DOOR3 PASS:** elements 53 -> **58** (44 wall, 6 door, 7 window, 1 roof); doors 5 -> 6. **W-DOOR4 PASS** (idempotent, 2 rows).
- **Emission changes this needed (all generic, data from the dictionary):** the importer wants leaf width = arc radius = wall gap; the PDF draws the CLEAR-opening radius (schedule width - ~80 mm, measured 80/80/81, §19) and two gap conventions. So `emit_dxf_p1.py` tries the SCHEDULE width (p8 text layer) first and keeps the drawn radius where only that fits (`E2r`); jambs: the wall piece on the hinge side is extended to the hinge (`E2j`, <=150 mm), or a pier is added where the hinge side is a perpendicular wall (`E2k`, T junctions: 92.1 / 87.7 / 140.2 mm). `to_std.py` tags doors whose radius is a schedule width OR the clear radius.
- **Binding:** D1, D2 -> INST_FIRE_DOOR_900 (exact 900x2100), D3 -> INST_FIRE_DOOR_750 (exact 750x2100). Semantics YELLOW: a fire-rated institutional door standing in for an unrated house door.

## 29. LEVEL C GROUP (1): THE EMISSION-FIXABLE ROWS (session 2, 2026-11-04; spec BEFORE code; session-2 prompt option B)
**Baseline reproduced first (`run_p1.sh`, bim-ootb main @ 286f2ca8):** `§LIB els 58` = 44 wall + 6 door + 7 window + 1 roof; `§LIB blocking 82`; DIRECT wall bbox x -0.05..9.95, y -6.25..0.05, z 0.15..3.15. IFC validate now reports 45 errors (44 walls + 1), not 41: same `PredefinedType` defect, the old number predates the 4 extra walls of §27b.
**Measured causes, each read from the owning code (not guessed):**
- **Row 90 (`no_elevation_height`, `no_host_cut`) = the 559 mm arc.** `emit_dxf_p1.py` writes every arc of 15-30 pt to the door layer. Not a schedule width (§19, §25). It moves to its own non-door layer, stays in the file, stays YELLOW: extracted, not a door, not dropped.
- **Row 89 (`fill_no_void`, `opening_gap_unbounded` before=null, `no_host_cut`) = D3 at the T junction B/3, not the shared-gap doors §25 blamed.** Hinge (1250, -2910.6), wall 25 (x -50..2059.7, top face y -3050) is 139.4 mm away, and its opposite D3 got the same pier at 140.2 mm. The E2k pier was refused by its own acceptance test, `w0-60 <= wall centre x <= w2+60+2000`: wall 25's CENTRE (1004.9) lies outside even though the wall spans the strip. The centre is the wrong measure; the rule is "the perpendicular wall spans the door's strip". `dxf2shell.js` then bounds the gap and `placeFills` gets its void.
- **`QF:grid_unlabelled` x10.** `dxf2qualify.js gridLines` (l.95-125): a label is non-MARK TEXT inside a CIRCLE on the grid layer whose centre lies on the line, within the line's extent + radius. We emit no circles. The PDF draws them: p1 strokes 10 circles r 10.14 pt (364 mm), each holding its grid word in the text layer. Read them, do not synthesise them.
- **PREDICTED CONSEQUENCE, stated before running: these 10 rows do NOT go away, they become `QF:grid_label_series` x10.** `dxf2qualify.js` l.309-310 (G2) demands numbers on constant-X lines and letters on constant-Y lines, with no setting; JKR draws the opposite (A..E across, 5..1 down, on all sheets). Relabelling to satisfy G2 would falsify the drawing (Prime Rule) and is refused. Net rows 0; the row changes from an emission defect to a TRUE FINDING about the drawing's convention, for the owner or the DXF2BIM lane.
- **`QF:grid_off_axis` x1 (grid 1, y -8500, the open porch).** `dxf2qualify.js` L2.9 accepts column centres as grid evidence, but only closed LWPOLYLINEs on a column layer. We emit columns as 4 LINEs, so the evidence is unread. The two W3 porch columns' centres are at y -8499.9 (§15). Same geometry, right entity. Not in the session-2 list of 16, closed because the same read shows the cause.
- **`slab_no_underside` x1.** `dxf2shell.js slabZ`: a single storey has no lower storey; the only stated source is a SECTION sheet (`sectionSlabs`, closed rect on `S-SLAB` whose top = the level). **p6 draws no slab outline and no slab dimension, and note G1 says ground slabs are "to Engr's detail": the structural thickness is NOT on these sheets.** What p6 DOES draw: under the floor of both sections, the sheet's only chromatic line (G8 = damp-proof membrane) 4.3 pt below FFL = 154 mm, against the drawn 150 dimension (level line within 0.1 pt). So what is extracted is the **floor build-up FFL -> membrane = 150 mm**, YELLOW in the dictionary as `floor_buildup`, never called the structural slab. The cut comes from the plan's own section markers: bubbles "A" at (48.6, 243.5) and (510.5, 243.5) pt, Y = -2424 mm.
**Witness claims (`scripts/pdf2bim/witness_level_c.py`, reads the run's logs and sheets; each can say NO-OP, VACUOUS or WRONG):**
- W-C1 door arcs on `A-DOOR` = 7 = the p8 schedule; 1 arc on the non-door layer; total 8 (WRONG if any arc was dropped); `no_elevation_height` 0.
- W-C2 `fill_no_void` 0, `opening_gap_unbounded` 0, `no_host_cut` 0, IfcDoor 7; piers include 139-140 mm at B/3 and the old 92.1 / 87.7 / 140.2 are unchanged.
- W-C3 10 bubbles read, each label equal to its dictionary axis (VACUOUS if 0); `grid_unlabelled` 0; `grid_label_series` exactly 10 as predicted (WRONG if any other grid row appears).
- W-C4 `grid_off_axis` 0 and the two column centres on the grid 1 line within the qualifier's 10 mm.
- W-C5 membrane depth 150 +- 10 mm on BOTH sections independently (WRONG otherwise, and then no section sheet is written); `slab_no_underside` 0; exactly 1 IfcSlab, DIRECT z 0..0.15.
- W-C6 walls keep the DIRECT bbox above; blocking total falls (NO-OP if not). Predicted: 82 - 1 - 2 - 1 - 1 - 1 - 1 + 2 (D3 now states its own size rows) = 77, +- any row a new sheet raises, each named.
**First run corrected the spec (witness W-C2, W-C3, W-C5 said WRONG; blocking rose to 95, kept here on purpose):** (a) **`QF:elev_grid_missing` x18, not predicted:** once the plan's labels are readable, `dxf2qualify.js` l.414-419 demands the same labelled lines on every face (5 + 5 + 4 + 4; grid 1 lies outside the side faces' envelope). The elevations draw them, so they are read per view the same way (`elev_emit.py`, residual logged; REAR against its drawn order, §22). (b) **A second pier, 90.3 mm, not predicted:** the bad centre test had also refused D2's jamb at the C/3 T junction. This one is a gain: that door was §27b's "4.7 m open-plan gap" (dw -3.79), now dw 0.000002. W-C2 now expects 5 piers. (c) **B-B read 89.7 mm: selector bug, not a drawing fact.** Choosing "the nearest row with >= 50 dashes above the membrane" took y 510.6, a tile-hatch row inside the toilet walls (x 337-378), instead of the FFL line at y 509.0 (x 152.6-560.3). A level line now must run past the outer grid bubbles. The 10 mm tolerance is unchanged.
**RESULT (2026-11-04, `scripts/pdf2bim/witness_level_c.py`, `logs/13_witness_level_c.log`, clean-dir run):** `§WC RESULT fails 0`.
| Witness | Verdict | Measured |
|---|---|---|

| W-C2 doors | PASS | `fill_no_void` / `opening_gap_unbounded` / `no_host_cut` 0 / 0 / 0, IfcDoor 7, piers 92.1, 87.7, 90.3, 140.2, 139.4 |

- **Falsified on doctored copies of the evidence:** a door-layer 559 arc -> W-C1 WRONG; no bubble line -> W-C3 VACUOUS; no slab -> W-C5 WRONG; blocking back at 82 -> W-C6 NO-OP.

## 31. HOW IT REACHES THE MODELLER, AND IS THE HOUSE COHERENT (owner 2026-11-04: *"we consolidate on this low cost single storey house first. How will it be imported? As a 2D image within the Modeller? Can this produce a coherent house?"* ... *"If it goes to DXF, then we have good flow and separation"*)
- **The flow is PDF -> DXF -> IFC, not a 2D image.** `dxf_import_ui.js` offers Open > "Open 2D drawings (.dxf)", reads the sheets by their `DXF2BIM-MARK` markings, draws them, then Convert. No PDF route and no image underlay exist in the Modeller's js (grep, bim-ootb @ 286f2ca8). The seam is the 7 typed DXF sheets. One leak across it: `to_std.py` writes library family ids into the sheets as `@TAG`.
- **PDF -> DXF is a developer command today** (`run_p1.sh`, needs poppler and Python), with sheet-specific constants (§30). No user-facing step exists. It reads VECTOR PDFs only; a scanned image has nothing to read, by ruling.
- **The Modeller would refuse this set, measured from the code:** `dxf_import_ui.js` l.980 refuses unless `verdict === 'qualifies'`, and `convert()` stops on `!state.gate.ok`; there is no override. Same reader headless: `§VER verdict blocked blocking 76`. **The IFC of §26-§30 came from the `lib_probe.js` driver, which does not pass that gate. The owner's §30 ruling is recorded here and does not reach the Modeller.**
- **The live library lacks CL006:** OCI `dxf2bim/library/latest.json` -> index `7e5df6e10027`: `INST_FIRE_DOOR_900` / `750` still have the old description with no size pair, which §27 measured as a `GAP:no_lod400_geometry` hard fail. Doors would hard-fail in the Modeller until the library is re-published. Migration without a published library is half the deliverable (CLAUDE.md DB rule).
- **Coherence, measured from the IFC triangles (direct, no engine):** the shell holds: closed ring, 40 of 47 wall pieces reach 3.15 and the rest are the pieces over and under openings, doors and windows on the drawing's positions, slab under the walls. **The house does not:** (a) roof = solid block; (b) the porch is absent (no floor, no columns, no gable, so D1 opens onto nothing and the elevation's main feature is missing); (c) window stand-ins do not fit: W2 is 819 x 759 in a 1200 x 1000 hole, leaving open gaps a person can see through; W3 is 819 wide in a 600 hole, so it runs into the wall, and its top 2.78 is above the 2.65 head; all are 352-417 mm thick in a 100 mm wall; (d) 5 interior doors sit 38 mm above the floor with a 38 mm gap at the head (mesh 2.024 in a 2.176 void).
- **The owner's acceptance of stand-ins (§30) was given before this picture.** Picture, CPU render of the file's triangles, no GPU: `~/Pictures/Screenshots/LowCostHouse_model_vs_drawing.png`. It is an illustration for the owner, not evidence; the numbers above are.

## 32. THE ROOF AND THE PORCH (owner 2026-11-04: *"proceed"*; spec BEFORE code; importer side `bim-ootb prompts/DXF2BIM_MODELLER_LANE.md` §L36)
**Issue:** the IfcRoof is a solid block, the porch is absent (§31). The sheets state both.
**Read from the sheets (probe, 2026-11-04):**
- Main roof: sheets end 751 / 745 / 751 / 747 from the axes (W-S3) -> axis +- 750. Ridge on grid 3, z 4.872, 25 deg. Predicted eave 3.077 m; p4 draws its top at 2910 above FFL = 3.060 m (17 mm).
- Porch roof, footprint from p3: ribs x 2976..6919, front edge y 9144, ridge x 4947. Height from p4: apex 4032 above FFL = z 4.182, slopes measured 25.0 deg.
- Porch floor: p4 line 100 below FFL, x 3073..6822; p6 B-B line at -99 from the wall to y 8347; a line at -250 below it = the same 150 mm build-up. So top z 0.05, thickness 0.15.
- Porch columns: the 2 W3 rectangles (§15), floor to beam: p4 beam line at 3000 above FFL, so z 0.05..3.15. The third rectangle (228 x 297, unexplained since §19) is NOT a column: it moves off A-COLS, YELLOW.
**Rule for a disagreement: the plan gives footprints, the elevation gives heights** (how the importer reads everything else). **Two FINDINGS, kept, not reconciled:** (a) p4 draws the porch gable x 2547..7347, 4800 wide; p3 draws its roof 3948 wide. p3 is used. (b) The plan's valleys reach the ridge at y 4865; the rule "highest plane" with p4's height reaches it at y 4580: 285 mm. Heights are not adjusted to fit.
**Gable ends:** p5 (both sides) and p4 (the porch) draw the triangle above the beam closed, so they become 3 walls rising to the roof's underside: x 0 and x 9900 on the plan's wall lines, the porch's on the column line between the columns' outer faces. Thickness = the plan's 100 mm walls; the porch one's is that same value by analogy, YELLOW. W-R9.
**Not drawn or not built, stated:** the porch beam (the porch gable wall stands in its place), the slot between the long walls' 3.15 top and the roof's underside (hidden by the eaves), the roof's 25.4 mm thickness (the registry's default, advisory), the 50 mm wet-room floor step.
**Witness claims (`scripts/pdf2bim/witness_house_roof.py`, reads the IFC directly):** W-R1 IfcRoof 4 bodies, none a box; top z 4.872. W-R2 roof plan extent x -0.75..10.65, y 0.75..-9.144. W-R5 no wall top pokes through the roof: at every wall corner the roof's underside is at or above the wall top. W-R6 IfcColumn 2, z 0.05..3.15, under the porch roof. W-R7 IfcSlab 2, porch top 0.05. Each says WRONG on failure, VACUOUS if the class is absent. **Falsifier:** without `@PITCH` the same set gives 1 box roof, and W-R1 says WRONG.
**RESULT (2026-11-04, `16_witness_roof.log`, bim-ootb #2631):** `§R RESULT fails 0`: W-R1 4 planes, none a box, top z 4.872. W-R2 x -0.75..10.65, y -9.145..0.75. ## 33. THE RULER TEMPLATE: 'Low cost single storey unit' (owner 2026-11-04; spec BEFORE code)
**Owner's words:** *"i like the code to infer with own default template reference for this building type: 'Low cost single storey unit'"* ... *"Element types from custom template. Meshes are from a standard library."* ... *"Thus it is accepting that it be hard for code to recognize marking unless it is given a ruler template to refer to"* ... *"The template is preset to have single storey, with such a roof and room layout and envelope with openings system"* ... *"it shall look for a narrower range of markings to fill in the blanks ie. dims, opening positions. Orientation are all presumed"* ... *"It is like a preset prefab factory with preset design where the user merely sets certain dims and options by way of its 2D image"* ... *"we could give an input method by way of chooser but that be overstepping the 2D layout on paper discipline"*.
**Issue:** the chain is deterministic but not all extracted: numbers measured by hand are typed in the code (RESUME block). On a drawing moved on its page it crashes (measured below).
**Ruling, do not re-litigate:** code does not recognise a drawing from nothing. The type's template is a form: the STRUCTURE is preset (one storey, main gable + porch gable, the room layout, the envelope with its openings system); ORIENTATION is presumed (which view is which face, its direction, the rear view's mirroring: never inferred, never checked by content beyond a stated witness); the code looks only for a narrow set of markings to FILL THE BLANKS (dimensions, opening positions). It is a prefab factory: the design is preset and the 2D drawing is the only input. **No chooser or form: paper stays the input (owner's discipline).**
**File:** `scripts/pdf2bim/types/low_cost_single_storey_unit.json`. It names the marking template and the Rosetta dictionary, and holds: `sheets` (page roles), `views` (presumed orientation), `preset`, `blanks` (nominal + tolerance per dimension), `ruler` (the pen and the plan frame the code looks within). Editable.
**The fill rule, one function (`type_template.py decide`), every blank logs a `§INFER` line:**
| Sheet says | Status | Value used |
|---|---|---|
| measured within the blank's tolerance | `READ` | the nominal (the drawing's module; the delta is logged) |
| measured outside the tolerance | `FINDING` | the MEASURED value: the sheet wins over the template, never adjusted to fit |
| nothing measurable | `DEFAULT` | the nominal, stated YELLOW, never silent |
**Stages:** new `03b_infer_type` (`infer_type.py`) writes `<out>/inferred.json`; `elev_emit`, `to_std`, `section_emit`, `roof_emit` read it. The high-window sill and head are decided in `elev_emit`, where they are measured.
**Witness claims (`witness_template.py` in the chain; `witness_ruler.sh`, `witness_sheets_same.py` as separate runs):**
- W-T1 every blank in the template has at least one `§INFER` record with a status (a high window has one per window); VACUOUS if there are no records, WRONG if a blank has none.
- W-T2 the typed numbers are gone: a static scan of the emit scripts for the literals named in the RESUME block; NO-OP if none of the scanned scripts exists, WRONG if a literal is found.
- W-T3 nothing changed: typed sheets byte-identical to a reference run of the old code, and the same geometry hash (`geom_hash.py`, committed now). A difference must be explained, not hidden.
- W-T4 positions are read, not typed: the whole PDF moved on an enlarged page (+60 pt, +125 pt, via Ghostscript) gives the same geometry hash and the same entities: everything but annotation text within 0.3 mm, annotation text within 30 mm (Ghostscript re-embeds the fonts, so text boxes move against the lines: measured below), and no chain witness says WRONG. **Falsifier measured BEFORE the change (old code on the moved PDF): `W-P5 wall pairs 0`, `IndexError` in walls, emit and elevations, no plan.dxf, no IFC.**
- W-T5 the ruler is consulted, not obeyed: a template stating beam height 3100 and eaves 900 must give `FINDING` and the sheet's values, not 3100 and 900. A sheet with its diagonals stripped must give `DEFAULT` for the ridge. A witness that passes on an untouched run proves nothing, so each run names what it altered.
- W-T6 presumed orientation has one check: FRONT and REAR swapped in the template must make slat windows lose their plan chains; the true template has none lost.
**Not in scope here (stated):** the marking recognisers' own tolerances (100 x 500 window modules, door arc radii) stay in the code and the dictionary; the grid dimensions, room list and K stay in the Rosetta dictionary until a second drawing exists to test them; witness-only sheet filters (`other_sheets.py` QTY column).
**RESULT (2026-11-04, bim-ootb 9b879d2d, modeller/ unchanged since c80b286f; logs `03b_infer_type`, `17_witness_template`, `18_geom_hash`, `witness_ruler.sh` output):**
| Blank | Measured on the sheets | Used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| beam above FFL | 3002.3 (4 views) | 3000 | READ |
| FFL above ground | 150.7 (4 views) | 150 | READ |
| ridge above beam | 1720.3 (2 side views) | 1722 | READ |
| eaves from axis | 747.5 (p3 ribs 750, 745) | 750 | READ |
| wall thickness | 100.4 (7 paired lines) | 100 | READ |
| porch step below FFL | 100 | 100 | READ |
| high window sill / head | 2000, 1996 / 2499, 2499 | 2000 / 2500 | READ |
Also read now, no longer typed: each view's beam and first-label x (all 8 values come out equal to the old typed ones), the porch gable's x (plan's A-COLS), the valley's end on the plan (y 4866, was typed 4865, so §32's finding reads 286 mm, not 285), QTY column, section band, plan frame (grid labels + 300 mm), the wall pen, page roles.
- `§T W-T1 PASS` 8 blanks, 10 records, all READ. `§T W-T1b PASS` G1 clean. `§T W-T2 PASS` 7 scripts, 51 literals, none found.
- **W-T3:** clean double run: 7 sheets byte-identical run to run, geometry hash `55b53362b2493a6f` twice. Against the OLD code: the same hash (`geom_hash.py` run on the old code's IFC), 6 sheets byte-identical, `plan.dxf` +28 lines and 0 removed = exactly 2 added annotation tags, explained next. `§LIB els 72`, `§LIB blocking 76`, `§WC` and `§R` fails 0, `§GAP-TOTAL` 19, all unchanged. The old hash a4c986f267f69a56 came from a method never committed and not reproduced (tried 3 serialisations x 5 roundings x 3 digests): retired.
- **A defect the typed box was hiding:** the old region (x 160..445, y 170..420) cut the two REAL W3 window tags by 3-10 pt ((182,170), (157,238)); the count looked right at 14 only because the two column W3 tags were inside. Any margin from 272 to 2218 mm gives the same 16 tags, so 300 is stable. Annotation only: no geometry moved.

(b) "Topmost B" picked a section marker at y 69 and G1 said WRONG only on the moved run: labels are now chosen as a whole row, and W-T1b makes G1 mandatory. (c) My first margin (200) dropped a window tag; the geometry hash did not notice, the byte comparison did. So the hash alone is not enough proof for annotation.

**Not closed, stated:**
- Still from the Rosetta dictionary, not filled from the sheet: grid dimensions, K, room list, schedule sizes. These are the next blanks.

- Marking recognisers' tolerances, selector windows in mm (`roof_emit.py` rib and front-edge bounds) stay in code: they bound a search and are not house dimensions.
- W-T5a's measured values are unsnapped (3002, 748): correct under the rule, since a sheet that disagrees is not rounded to a guess.
- `12_ifc_check` bbox lines differ run to run (window z-min 1.046 vs 0.0, roof y -9.145 vs -7.98e27): **our own use-after-free, retracted in §38; then blamed on the engine.** `geom_hash.py` reads the triangles directly.
**What the owner's public wording rests on (measured from `11_lib_probe.log`, `§LIB families`, `fills.elements 14`):** *"Element types from custom template"* holds: every element's class and binding comes from the JSON templates. *"Meshes are from a standard library"* holds for the 14 doors and windows (of 72), from 4 library families; the other 58 (walls, slabs, columns, roof) are built from the drawing's numbers. All 7 windows are nearest-size stand-ins and do not fit their holes (§31).

## 34. WINDOW FAMILIES FROM THE p8 SCHEDULE; THE HOUSE CONVERTS (owner 2026-11-04: *"Go with your hunches"* = windows authored from the schedule into the standard library, a recorded acceptance the Modeller honours, the library published, fold arrow kept; spec BEFORE code; importer side `bim-ootb prompts/DXF2BIM_MODELLER_LANE.md` §L37)
**Issue:** the 7 windows are stand-ins that do not fit their holes (§31: W2 819 x 759 in a 1200 x 1000 hole; W3 819 wide in a 600 hole), and the Modeller refuses the set.
**Read from p8 (text layer):** W1 "M.B alum. 3 panel frame adjustable window" 1800 x 1000; W2 "2 panel" 1200 x 1000; W3 "M.B alum. frame single panel, top hung adjustable" 600 x 500. Depth: the plan's window modules are 100 mm deep (§19), the wall's thickness.
**Generated, stated as generated:** `gen_window_families.py` writes `migration/CL007_low_cost_window_families.sql`: head, sill, 2 jambs, panel-count-minus-1 mullions, one pane per panel. Overall size and panel count are the schedule's; depth is the plan's. **Not in this drawing, so template defaults, YELLOW:** frame face 50 mm, pane 6 mm (`window_system`). The blades of an adjustable window are not modelled: the pane stands for them. `provenance='PARAMETRIC'`, `source` names the schedule. Conventions read from the library: centred mesh, metres, X width, Y depth, Z height, hash = sha256(vertices)[:16], faces int32 LE, normals NULL. **Each size gets an X-running and a Y-running mesh:** `placeFills` selects by the wall's direction, and one direction alone hard-fails `GAP:lod400_orientation` on grid A.
**Witness claims (`witness_windows.py`, reads the IFC):**
- W-W1 7 IfcWindow, each mesh's width and height equal its p8 size within 1 mm; WRONG if a stand-in size remains; VACUOUS if no window.
- W-W2 every window sits inside its hole: its extent along the wall and in z within the drawn opening +- 5 mm, and its depth within the wall's 100 mm. Issue: §31's open gaps and windows running into walls.
- W-W3 no other family moved: every old family's `familyToRef` result is the same with and without CL007 (a new pair key could make an old family ambiguous).
**Publish, one new version, reversible:** upload `<sha12>/` beside the live version, then regression, then flip `latest.json` last. The new export also carries `building_types` (General), which the live one lacks (§L20's tags, never republished). Regression: all old meshes byte-equal to the live shards; the published Duplex marked samples give the same verdict and fills on old and new.

## 35. A SIMILAR DRAWING WITH DIFFERENT MARKERS (owner 2026-11-04: *"as long as when someone submits a similar drawing type but with different markers such as bubbles, dims, the code can pick them out and adjust correctly. The code might tell where to look and infer as a blind person but with good rules of thumb"* ... *"I assume code OCR is weak thus such preset marking to compare with is like a weak sighted person going by what is expected to be made out"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** §33's W-T4 only proves position. Grid dimensions, K and schedule sizes still come from the dictionary, so another drawing of the type gets THIS drawing's numbers. **Ruling:** the code is a weak-sighted reader going by what is expected: the template says where to look and what to expect; it compares, and adjusts. Still no OCR.
**Rules of thumb (`fill_blanks.py`, the first stage; writes `<out>/rosetta.effective.json`, which every later stage reads; the §33 fill rule decides each value):**
- **Find the grid:** the row with the most distinct grid letters, the column with the most distinct grid numbers.
- **Find the scale (K):** a sheet's printed 1:100 is not its real scale (fitted print: 35.28 printed vs 35.87 real). The window modules are the ruler: closed 1:5 rectangles at a 600 mm pitch (`ruler.window_module_pitch_mm`): K0 = 600 / the measured pitch. Then the overall span, rounded to the module (`ruler.grid_module_mm` 100), gives K = span mm / span pt.
- **Read the grid dimensions:** label distance x K. Within tolerance of the dictionary's = READ; else FINDING, rounded to the module.
- **Read the schedule:** the p8 SIZE rows, by the REFERENCES row above them, left to right (text layer).
- **Bubble and section-marker sizes** move from typed points to millimetres in the ruler, so they follow the scale.
**Different-drawing witness (`variant_drawing.py`, `witness_variant.sh`):** no second PDF exists, so real ones are made by warping this PDF's own vector paths and text, written as a new PDF through a small writer; the chain sees a file it has never seen, with no template or dictionary change:
- **Z control:** no warp. Same geometry as the original within rounding. Without this a pass below could be the writer's fault or luck.
- **B wider end bay:** D-E 3100 -> 3700, at one seam in that bay free of doors and windows, on the plan, roof, front, rear and A-A. Expected: `grid_x_E` FINDING 10500, everything else READ; walls to x 10.55, roof to 11.25, windows still within 5 mm of their holes, verdict as the original's.
- **H taller wall:** 3000 -> 3300, at a seam above the high window's head, on the 4 elevations and A-A. Expected: `beam_above_ffl` FINDING 3300 or within a few mm; wall top 3.45; ridge 5.172; no wall through the roof.
- **S print scale:** the whole sheet at 90% (Ghostscript). Expected: K about 39.86, every dimension READ, the same model within rounding.
- **Falsifier:** run B against the dictionary's fixed grid (`PDF2BIM_NO_FILL=1`): it must NOT give the right house, or the witness proves nothing.
Rooms' printed areas are not warped: `R1` reports them as WRONG on B, correctly: the drawing now contradicts itself.
**Also rules of thumb:** a FINDING rounds to the blank's `module_mm` (a drawn 3302 is a 3300 wall). The wall pen moves to mm (`ruler.wall_pen_mm`). H warps the 4 elevations only: the section keeps no wall height the chain reads. Z's comparison allows 5 mm: the chain's own reading rounds to 0.1 pt = 3.6 mm.
**Stated limits, known now:** the grid labels keep their names; another bay count is outside the preset; a schedule size with no family in the library is refused by the Modeller, not invented (the family comes through `gen_window_families.py`).
**RESULT (2026-11-04; logs `00_fill_blanks`, `witness_variant.sh`, §34's `16b_witness_windows`; library f5a308656e73 live; geometry hash now dc47d2e37a86fc49, explained: only the 7 window meshes changed, triangles 7868 -> 7576, 72 elements, two clean runs identical):**
| Drawing the chain had never seen | What the code found | The model |
|---|---|---|
| Z control, no warp | nothing differs | the same house, 378 entities within the chain's own 0.1 pt rounding (worst 5 mm) |
| B end bay 3100 -> 3700 | `grid_x_E` 10500, only that | walls to 10.55, roof to 11.25, 72 elements, windows in their holes, qualifies |
| H wall 3000 -> 3300 | `beam_above_ffl` 3300 (drawn 3302), only that | wall top 3.45, ridge 5.172, no wall through the roof, qualifies |
| S print at 90% | K 39.86 (35.87/0.9 = 39.86), only that | the same house within 10 mm, qualifies |
| B with `PDF2BIM_NO_FILL` (falsifier) | nothing read | walls 3.15 high, no roof, verdict blocked: without the reader it is not B's house |
- **Instrument, checked:** the writer reproduced 25,371 points of p6 with 3 odd paths off by up to 0.25 pt. Some title-block and table words still re-split (p7 21, p8 18, p3 10); the schedule and grid words the chain reads survive. A seam at 7680 clipped two door arcs that reach 7700 and cost 2 doors: moved to 7725, before the window at 7748. That was the instrument, not the chain.
- **Typed points the S run forced into mm:** the 300 pt long-line threshold, the wall pen, bubble and section-marker radii. A larger print scale than tested may find more: the recognisers' other point bounds (for example `2<=b-a<=3.6`) are still in points.
- **Stated limits:** B and H were made from THIS drawing, so they prove dimensions and positions move correctly, not another drafter's style: a different pen, layout or label names are not tested; **only a real second PDF tests those (one question for the owner).** W-R1/R2/R4/R6/R9 hold this drawing's literal heights and extents, so they say WRONG on B or H; the §V checks judge those runs instead. Printed room areas are not re-read: `R1` says WRONG on B, correctly. Dimension strings are stroked lines with no text layer, so they are measured from geometry, not read: the owner's "weak sighted" comparison of drawn glyphs against expected values (§12) is not built, and geometry has not needed it so far.
**RESULT of §34 (2026-11-04):**
- W-W1 7 windows at their p8 sizes (1800/1200/600 x 1000/1000/500). W-W2 worst 1.3 mm from the drawn hole, 100 mm deep, in the wall. CL007 generator byte-stable. **All 14 window rows are gone: 76 -> 62 rows, all accepted, verdict qualifies.** The 9 size rows left are doors only.
- W-LIB-PUB, run BEFORE the flip (`witness_library_publish.js`): P1 331 of 331 old meshes byte-equal; P2 66 of 68 old families resolve as before, the 2 that moved are the doors CL006 makes bindable; P3 Duplex samples: qualifies, the same 36 fills, 158 elements on live and new; P4 added only 3 families + 6 meshes, lost nothing. W-W3 is covered by P2.
- **Published:** `dxf2bim/library/latest.json` -> f5a308656e73: 191 objects sha256-checked with gzip, JSON type and CORS; fetched back from the public URL. 7e5df6e10027 stays: rollback is one object. All 7 doors now bind in the Modeller.
- **Real page (bim-ootb §L37):** the house Converts. The IFC exported from the page has 14 IfcOpeningElement + 14 fills, 49 products (the wall pieces of a line become one body, 13 walls), and the roof and window witnesses pass on it. So the gap register's "openings as voids" is the `lib_probe.js` driver's writer, and the Modeller's own export closes it.

## 36. ROOMS AND PRINTED AREAS READ PER DRAWING (session 4, 2026-11-05; spec BEFORE code; RESUME next-item 1)
**Issue:** room areas come from the dictionary as literals, so another drawing of the type is compared against THIS drawing's numbers, and `R1` can only say MISMATCH on a drawing that differs. Two rows have no printed value at all.
**Measured first (probes, session scratchpad, on p1):**
- p1 holds exactly 10 room tags: closed 5-point hairline rectangles 19.32 x 8.9-9.0 pt (693 x 320 mm), pen 0.12 pt, each with a hairline vertical divider. None on any other sheet.
- The tag's top row is `FFL | area sqm`, drawn in a 0.72 pt pen as outlined strokes on a 0.12 pt grid, not in the text layer. **Exact shape hashing fails: 108 distinct shapes** across the 10 tags, from quantisation and overprinting (§10's alphabet idea, measured wrong here too).
- A vector ink distance works: each string placed glyph by glyph, template-to-ink plus ink-to-template, no raster. Tag left out of its own alphabet: **8 of 8 judgeable areas read right**, including `2.08` vs `2.88` under overprint (margin 0.011 pt). `11.47` and `7.36` are NOT judgeable that way: their `4` and `3` occur nowhere else on the sheet. In that state the best wrong candidates scored 0.146 and 0.092 pt, so an absolute limit alone does not prove a read.
- **FFL is NOT read: `100` vs `000` margins were 0.000-0.013 pt, with one wrong and one tied.** The ring-shaped `0` covers the `1`, and one glyph is diluted over six. FFL stays in the dictionary. No geometry depends on these tags.
- By the one-time hindsight read (§16's accepted method), the two empty rows are BILIK MANDI 2.08 and ANJUNG 7.36: both equal their cells (1300 x 1600, 3200 x 2300). ANJUNG's FFL tag reads 000.00; not entered, as nothing witnesses it.
**Ruling applied (§12, §35: match against what is expected, never read freely):** the code never reads a string from nothing. It holds a short list of candidates and asks which one is drawn here. **Candidates for a tag:** the area of every grid rectangle containing the tag on THIS drawing, plus every printed area in the dictionary. The glyph samples are the owner's "preset marking to compare with": generated once from this sheet by `gen_tag_glyphs.py` into `types/low_cost_house_tag_glyphs.json`, editable, in mm so they follow the print scale.
**Rule (`read_rooms.py`, a stage after the plan is emitted; writes the rooms into `rosetta.effective.json`):**
| Sheet says | Status | Printed value |
|---|---|---|
| best candidate within `read_max_mm`, beating the next by `margin_mm`, and equal to the room's cell | `READ` | that value |
| same test passes, but the value differs from the cell | `FINDING`: the drawing contradicts itself | that value; any other grid rectangle it fits is logged |
| no candidate passes (another font, overprint, no tag) | `DEFAULT`: stated YELLOW, "not made out" | null: **never the dictionary's number** |
- **Room cell:** the layout is preset (§33), so a room's cell is its preset grid names at THIS drawing's grid values. The tag must lie in it. A cell with no tag or two tags is a FINDING. The cell is never overridden by a read area: a fit elsewhere is reported, not obeyed.
- **ANJUNG's width:** the centre distance of the two A-COLS column rectangles this run read, against the dictionary's 3200 by the §33 fill rule (it has no grid line on one axis).
- `R1` moves from `rosetta_verify.py` to this stage and now compares two numbers read from this drawing. The dictionary's literals remain expected values only.
- Thresholds, in `ruler.room_tag`, set from the measurements above and not to be widened to pass: `read_max_mm` 5.0 (true reads 2.4-4.4 mm with the tag left out), `margin_mm` 0.25.
**Witness claims (`witness_rooms.py`; each can say NO-OP, VACUOUS or WRONG):**
- W-A1 the 10 tags are found, one in each preset cell. VACUOUS if no tag box is found; WRONG on a count or cell mismatch that was not reported as a FINDING.
- W-A2 every room has a status and a printed value or a stated null. On this drawing **10 of 10 READ and R1 MATCH**. WRONG if a READ value differs from its cell.
- W-A3 the alphabet is not circular: each tag is read again with its own samples removed. It must give the same value or say UNJUDGEABLE. WRONG if it reads a different value; NO-OP if nothing was judged. Predicted: 8 judged, 2 unjudgeable. So the 2 single-sample digits are stated as not independently proven.
- W-A4 the reader can refuse: each tag scored against all other tags' strings. The drawn string must win. WRONG otherwise; the smallest margin is logged.
**Different-drawing witness (`variant_drawing.py` modes + `witness_variant.py` V5):**
- Z, H, S: 10 READ. S proves the mm samples follow a 90% print.
- B (wider end bay; printed glyphs unchanged): **exactly BILIK 3 and BILIK 2 FINDING, printed 9.61 against cell 11.47**; the rest READ. This is the §35 "R1 says WRONG" case, now said properly.
- **New mode T:** the area ink of DAPUR and RUANG TAMU swapped. Predicted: exactly those two FINDING, printed 11.47 and 8.25, cells unchanged, geometry hash unchanged.
- **New mode G:** the tags' top-row ink sheared (x + 0.5 h), standing in for another font. Predicted: DEFAULT and null, **zero READ**. A READ here means the reader flatters itself.
**RESULT (2026-11-05, logs `06b_read_rooms`, `06c_witness_rooms`, `witness_variant.sh`):**
| Drawing | Rooms | Predicted? |
|---|---|---|
| this sheet, Z control, H taller wall, S 90% print | 10 READ | yes |
| B wider end bay | exactly BILIK 3 and BILIK 2 FINDING, printed 9.61 vs cell 11.47 | yes |
| T two areas swapped | exactly DAPUR printed 11.47 vs 8.25, RUANG TAMU 8.25 vs 11.47; same geometry hash as Z | yes |
| G sheared ink, another font's stand-in | 0 READ, 10 DEFAULT, every printed value null; best scores 7.1-8.8 mm against the 5.0 limit; W-A3 NO-OP | yes |
- W-A1 10 tags, 10 rooms. W-A2 10 READ, including BILIK MANDI and ANJUNG, empty before. W-A3 **8 judged, 8 same, UNJUDGEABLE RUANG TAMU 11.47 and ANJUNG 7.36: exactly as predicted. Those two reads are not independently proven: their `4` and `3` occur once on the sheet.** W-A4 smallest margin 0.37 mm. True scores 1.8-4.7 mm; RUANG MAKAN, under a table's linework, is the closest to the limit at 4.7.
- Geometry untouched: two clean runs, 7 typed sheets byte-identical to the run before this change, hash dc47d2e37a86fc49. ANJUNG's width reads 3200.3 from the columns: READ 3200.
- **A false read the first run produced, and the fix:** BILIK MANDI read `2.88`, FINDING, while W-A2 said PASS. Taking samples from the area cells only left the alphabet with no `0`, so `2.08` could not be scored, was dropped, and the next best won. Fixed three ways: samples also from the FFL cells; **any unjudgeable candidate now blocks the read**; W-A2 says PASS only when every room is READ, and V5 demands exact sets.
**Stated limits, known now:** room NAMES are not read (stroked, preset by cell); FFL is not read; finishes are not read; a different drafter's real font gives DEFAULT everywhere until samples are added to the JSON: a safe failure, not a working read. All samples come from one sheet.

(c) **Falsifier:** the same IFC judged against a copy of `inferred.json` with beam 3100 must make W-R1, W-R6 and W-R9 say WRONG: otherwise the witness only agrees with itself. **RESULT (2026-11-05):** (a) reference: 9 PASS, same measurements; eave now judged against 3.062 where the typed value was 3.060. (b) B: all 9 PASS with x to 11.250, walls 50. H: all 9 PASS with top 5.172, columns and gable bases at 3.450, eave 3.377 vs 3.363. ## 38. HOUSE DOORS FROM THE p8 SCHEDULE; THE FLOAT CLOSED (session 4, 2026-11-05; spec BEFORE code; RESUME next-item 5, taken before 3 and 4 because the owner said consolidate on this house first; importer side `bim-ootb prompts/DXF2BIM_MODELLER_LANE.md` §L38)
**Issue:** the 7 doors are fire-rated institutional FD1 meshes standing in for a house door (p8: D1 "metal frame, single leaf solid timber", D2/D3 "flush door"), and 5 interior doors float.
**Measured first (`12` IFC triangles, p4 lines, importer source). §31's account was incomplete and one earlier claim was circular:**
- Mesh 0.9 / 0.75 x 2.100 is the right size. **It is 150 mm deep in a 100 mm wall**, standing 25 mm proud on each face: not reported before.
- The float's cause: the importer cuts an interior door's void at schedule height + `CASING` 0.076 = 2.176 (`dxf2elev.js` l.221, §L2.7 "the plan and the schedule do not draw the head frame", a rule measured on Duplex), then centres the mesh in it (`placeFills` l.638): 38 mm under, 38 mm over.
- **Circular, corrected:** §27b's "2 exterior D1 exact, height 2.1 from the elevation" proves nothing: `elev_emit.py` l.137 draws that rectangle at the SCHEDULE's height, so the importer was reading our own typed number back.
- **What the sheet really draws (p4, both elevations, measured):** an outer frame x 7399..8299 = 900 wide, z 0..2100; an inner frame line 52 mm in, z 0..2055; a panel to 1905. So here the schedule's 900 x 2100 IS the frame's outside. **This drawing's head frame is inside the schedule's height; adding 76 mm contradicts the sheet.** Frame face 50 mm is read here, not a template default as the windows' is.
**Plan:**
1. **`elev_emit.py` reads the door head** from the door's outer verticals (a §33 blank `door_head_above_ffl`, nominal 2100, tol 15, one record per view). The rectangle is drawn at what was read. Falsifier below.
2. **A new importer mark, `@HEADFRAME=<mm>`, single, on the plan (bim-ootb §L38):** the head frame this set's door schedule leaves out. Absent = today's `CASING`, byte for byte. `to_std.py` writes `@HEADFRAME=0` only when every read door head equals its schedule height within tolerance; otherwise it writes no mark. Paper stays the input.
3. **Families from p8, migration `CL008_low_cost_door_families.sql`,** generated like CL007, with `LCSSU_D1_900x2100`, `LCSSU_D2_900x2100`, `LCSSU_D3_750x2100`: head + 2 jambs of the frame face read above, leaf between them, closed. Size from the schedule; depth = the plan's 100 mm wall. **Default, YELLOW:** leaf 40 mm (not drawn at a readable scale). Not modelled, stated: knob, lock, the swing (the leaf is shut), D1's timber vs D2's flush finish (the same mesh, by size). `is_fire_rated` 0: the sheet states no rating. `gen_door_families.py` is new; it and `gen_window_families.py` share `family_mesh.py`. CL007's header names its generator, so that script stays, and **its output must stay byte-identical to the committed CL007**.
4. **`library_binding` D1..D3** move to the new families. Old FD1 families are untouched.
**Witness claims (`witness_doors.py`, reads the IFC and the p4 values):**
- W-D1 7 IfcDoor at their p8 sizes within 1 mm; VACUOUS if none; WRONG if an FD1 size or a 150 mm depth remains.
- W-D2 **every door stands on its floor: z-min = FFL within 1 mm, and the head at FFL + schedule height.** This is the float. WRONG on 38 mm.
- W-D3 every door lies inside its wall: depth <= 100.5 mm.
- W-D4 the door's width against its drawn gap: 5 within 5 mm. **Predicted FINDING, kept: the 2 D2 on grid D are 900 in an 820 gap** (the drawn clear radius, §27b). The drawing's own disagreement: not fitted, not widened.
- W-D5 elevation door heads were READ from p4: a `§INFER door_head_above_ffl` per view. VACUOUS if none. **Falsifier:** a template stating 2400: FINDING and the sheet's 2100, not 2400.
- W-D6 (importer) `witness_dxf_headframe.js`: no mark = the same voids as today; `@HEADFRAME=0` = void 2.100, leaf 2.100; malformed = `MARK:malformed`.
**Predicted effect:** `§LIB` accepted falls from 62 to about 55: all `dh` rows and the exact-width doors leave, the 2 D2 rows stay and remain accepted. Every row number is read from the log, not this estimate.
**Regression:** W-LIB-PUB P1..P4 on a new export (old meshes byte-equal, old families resolve as before, Duplex samples unchanged). Every Convert-clicking witness (§L36's rule); the not-runnable ones are named.
**RESULT (2026-11-05, logs `08_elev_emit`, `09_to_std`, `11_lib_probe`, `16c_witness_doors`; final clean runs, 7 sheets byte-identical, hash 03a3dfef2a0b4342, triangles 7576 -> 1808):**
