## 39. A SCHEDULE SIZE THE LIBRARY LACKS (session 4, 2026-11-05; spec BEFORE code; RESUME next-item 3)
**Issue:** `fill_blanks.py` reads a new size from p8, yet `library_binding` still names the dictionary's family, so another drawing's D3 at 800 gets a 750 mesh (a size row, silently acceptable), or would be refused. §35 stated the limit; this closes it inside the chain.
**Rule, no owner choice, paper stays the input:**
- The family's id follows the size the sheet states: `LCSSU_<tag>_<w>x<h>`, in `fill_blanks.py`, for every scheduled door and window. On this drawing that is the same ids as today: no change in any byte.
- Stage `10a` runs `gen_door_families.py` and `gen_window_families.py` on THIS run's effective dictionary, and applies them to the COPY of the library (`INSERT OR IGNORE`). A family the library holds changes nothing. One it lacks is generated, with the run's sizes.
- If the export in use lacks a family, the run re-exports; a run sharing another run's export replaces its link with its own.
- `§FAM <tag> <family> LIBRARY|GENERATED`, one line per tag. **A family this run generated is written to `<out>/families_new.sql`, ready for a publish decision, and is stated as not on the live library:** the published Modeller refuses it with `GAP:no_lod400_geometry`. Generating is not publishing: nothing here touches OCI.
**Different-drawing witness, new mode D (`variant_drawing.py`, `witness_variant.py`):** p8's D3 text changes 750 to 800, the plan's gaps do not. Predicted: `schedule_D3` FINDING and no other; `§FAM D3 LCSSU_D3_800x2100 GENERATED`, every other tag LIBRARY; 2 D3 doors 800 wide, on their floor, 100 deep; W-D4 FINDING on those 2 (the drawing now contradicts itself; I wrote 50 mm here, measured 131 mm: the plan's clear radius is 669, not 750) as well as the 2 D2; `families_new.sql` holds only that family; `qualifies` on recorded acceptance; the same walls as the control. **Falsifier:** `PDF2BIM_NO_FILL=1` on the same PDF: dictionary sizes, D3 at 750, no generated family.
**Two defects mode D and mode H exposed (the first sweep's V9 said WRONG; measured, then fixed):**
- **D: 2 doors silently lost, verdict still `qualifies`.** `emit_dxf_p1.py` and `to_std.py` decided "is this arc a door?" against the schedule's widths, so at 800 the plan's 669 arcs became fixtures. Now a door arc is known by its own radius and pen (`ruler.door_arc`: leaves 669-900 mm in 0.72 pt; the fixture 559 mm in 0.12 pt). `to_std.py`'s second copy of the test is deleted.
- **H: REAR's beam misread, unnoticed since §35.** `infer_type.py` took the line triple nearest the TEMPLATE's spacing, so on a 3300 wall REAR took another long line, 131 mm low; `W-E1` logged WRONG in a line nothing judged. Its door head then read nothing, no `@HEADFRAME` was written, and the doors floated. Each view's beam is now re-read at the sheets' spacing once the blank is decided. **New W-T1c: any stage's own WRONG (stages 00-11) fails the template witness.**
**Stated limits:** only sizes are followed: a new tag (a D4) is outside the preset, and a window's panel count stays the template's. `to_std.py` chooses a window's class by nearest width, so two windows swapping widths are not told apart.
- **A third, in my own generator:** with `--only D3` the ordinals restarted at 1, the map's unique index (type, class, storey, ordinal) made `INSERT OR IGNORE` drop the mesh rows, and the family arrived with no geometry. The importer hard-failed and invented nothing: correct. Ordinals for a run's own family now come from its size; CL007 and CL008 stay byte-identical.
**RESULT (2026-11-05, final sweep on the committed code, every `§V ... RESULT fails 0`, Z B NOFILL H S T G FR D NOFILLD):** D: `schedule_D3` FINDING and only it; `§FAM D3 LCSSU_D3_800x2100 GENERATED`, the rest LIBRARY; doors [800, 800, 900 x 5] on their floor, 100 deep; W-D4 FINDING on 4 doors (D2 +80, D3 +131); `families_new.sql` holds that family only; 72 elements, qualifies, 61 accepted. Falsifier NOFILLD: doors [750, 750, 900 x 5], nothing generated. H now passes every roof and door witness, REAR's beam re-read 183.7 -> 180.1. W-T4, W-T5a/b, W-T6 PASS.
**NOT DONE, needs the owner: a user-facing PDF -> DXF step (RESUME item 4, ⛔).** The chain is ~1,500 lines of Python plus poppler. Two shapes, not one: **(A) in the Modeller page, recommended:** PDF opens like a DXF, offline, no install, no server, which fits the air gap; it means porting these rules to JS on pdf.js, one implementation retired. **(B) a command for a drafter:** days less work, keeps Python, needs an install. This changes what gets built, so it is asked, not assumed.

## 40. A SECOND DRAWING, AUTHORED FROM SCRATCH (owner 2026-11-05: *"u have to infer or recreate a sample 2nd low cost PDF just to test"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** §35's variants are warps of TB-LKTN, so they share its pens, symbols, fonts and layout. Only another hand tests the reader's rules of thumb.
**Status of this drawing, stated, always:** a test instrument, written by this lane, not a real drafter's set and never evidence about one. The author knows the reader, so a pass proves less than a real PDF would. **To limit that: the style table below is fixed BEFORE the first run, and the first run's failures are recorded here unfixed, before any change.**
**`make_second_drawing.py` writes `second_drawing.pdf` from its own table, no input from TB-LKTN. Same TYPE (§33's preset: one storey, main + porch gable, the 10 rooms by grid cell, 3 door + 3 window types, 7 + 7 openings). A different hand:**
| Feature | TB-LKTN | Second drawing |
|---|---|---|
| Paper, scale | A4, 1:100, fitted (35.87 mm/pt) | A3, 1:75, true (26.46 mm/pt) |
| Grid mm | 1300 1800 3700 3100; 1500 1600 3100 2300 | 1500 1800 3900 3300; 1700 1700 3400 2200 |
| Walls | 100, pen 0.36 pt, hatch | 150, pen 0.5 pt, no hatch |
| Bubbles | r 364 mm circle | r 500 mm circle, pen 0.25 |
| Windows on plan | 100 x 500 modules at 600 pitch | wall-thick frame + 2 glass lines, no modules |
| Windows on elevations | slats | frame + one mullion |
| Door swing | clear radius, sizes mixed | leaf radius = schedule width, gap = width |
| Schedule | 900/900/750 x 2100; 1800/1200/600 x 1000, 500 | 1000/900/750 x 2100; 1500/1200/600 x 1200, 600 |
| Heights | FFL 150, beam 3000, 25 deg, eaves 750 | FFL 300, beam 2800, 30 deg, eaves 600 |
| Room tags | hairline 2x2 box, stroked outline digits | no box, text layer: name, then `11.22 m2` |
| Column tags | W3 (the drafter's slip, §15) | C1, 200 x 200 |
| Roof plan | sheet ribs, valleys | closed outline, ridge, valleys, no ribs |
| Sections | chromatic membrane line | no membrane drawn |
| Kept the same (presumed by §33) | sheet order, view placement per sheet, grid names, tag names D1..W3 | same |
**Witness: `W-2D`, run by `witness_second.sh`, judging the IFC against the generator's own table, which the chain never reads:** every grid line, door and window size and position, wall thickness, FFL, beam, ridge within 10 mm; every value's status READ, FINDING or DEFAULT stated. **A DEFAULT on a value the sheet does state is a gap, listed, not a pass.** Falsifier: `PDF2BIM_NO_FILL=1` must not give this house.
**Ruling on what may change:** a failure is fixed by a rule of thumb that is true of the type and still holds on TB-LKTN (hash and sheets unchanged, or explained), or a marking stated in the template JSON (§13: editable for another drafter). Never by a branch keyed to this file, never by widening a tolerance to pass.
**FIRST RUN, UNFIXED (2026-11-05, recorded before any change):** `§FILL VACUOUS no window module pairs: modules 0 the scale cannot be read`, and with no scale everything downstream fails: `03b_infer_type` crashes finding no bubble row, `04_walls` reads 0 wall pairs, `05_openings` binds 2 of 14, `06_emit` crashes, no sheets, no IFC. It refused, invented nothing, and read none of the house. **So the reader as of §39 does not generalise past this drafter's window symbol: §35's weak-sighted reader is tied to TB-LKTN at its first step.**
**Errors in the drawing itself, found reading the reader, fixed in the generator, recorded so the first run stays honest:** side views against §33's presumed orientation; grid 1 missing on side views; sections A-A and B-B both headed by numbers; no porch floor line on B-B; the porch's eave not at the main eave and valleys not at 45 deg. None changes the style table.

**RESULT (2026-11-05, `witness_second.sh`, final rules, every run from a clean directory):** judged against the generator's table, which the chain never reads: wall envelope and 150 thickness, FFL 300, beam 2800, roof top within 1 mm, eaves 600, 7 doors and 7 windows each at its place, size, height, wall-deep, all within 10 mm; 10 rooms READ from the text layer; `qualifies`, 53 accepted, no size rows. **One DEFAULT, and it is right: the floor build-up, which this drawing does not draw.** Falsifier `PDF2BIM_NO_FILL=1`: no IFC. TB-LKTN after every change: 7 sheets byte-identical, hash unchanged, all variants `fails 0`.
**What it took (the rules that were TB-LKTN's, now the sheet's):**
| Was typed or tied to one hand | Now |
|---|---|
| scale only from window modules | the sheet's printed scale when no modules; span rounded to the module |
| wall pen, thickness, half-width 50, pair gap 2.0-3.6 pt | read: long paired lines centred on a grid axis (off-axis double lines on TB-LKTN gave 0.72 pen, 280 thick until that condition) |
| bubble and marker radius, door-arc pen | read per sheet |
| windows = 100 x 500 modules | modules, or one wall-thick frame; a frame's sill and head read from its edges on the elevation |
| column = 200 x 300 by a W3 tag | size band and tags in the template |
| pitch 25, eaves from ribs | pitch read from side views; eaves from ribs or the outline |
| floor to ground spacing within 20 percent; wall top nearest the template | wider search; **a wall corner drawn from the ground up settles the wall's top**; with none, nearest the template as before |
| section needs a membrane line; slab bottom z 0 | no membrane = build-up DEFAULT; bottom = FFL less the build-up (z 0 only because FFL = 150 = build-up) |
| printed areas stroked | a typed `n.nn m2` inside the cell is read first |
| family depth 100 | the wall's; id gains `d150` |
**My rules broke something four times, caught only by re-running both drawings:** the wall pen rule read TB-LKTN as 280 thick; the wall-top rule gave H FFL 120, then the second drawing FFL 200; the wider search gave H a triple with a ground spacing of 86. **A commit (803c5336a) said "all variants `fails 0`" while its own sweep said H `fails 5`: the text was written and pushed in the same command that printed the result. Corrected here; the rule is now: a triple settled by a ground-to-wall-top corner, else inside the template's +-20 percent, else the wider search. Read first, then write. A rule is not done until BOTH drawings and every variant have run.**
**An importer defect found, not fixed here:** non-linear `@Z` marks on a section crash `dxf2shell.js` l.270 (`map.zOf is not a function`) instead of refusing. Handed to §L39.
**Stated limits:** the author knows the reader; other sheets' witnesses (W-S1, W-S2, W-S7) hold this drafter's symbols and say VACUOUS on the second drawing, judged by nothing; panel counts stay the template's (its W1 says 2 panels, the mesh has 3); room names are still preset, not read; `R['roof']` notes remain the first drawing's prose. **Only a real PDF from another drafter tests a hand I did not write.**

## 41. THE PDF STEP IN THE MODELLER: "from PDF image" (owner 2026-11-05: *"Follow your recommend. So opening panel will have an added '.. from PDF image'"*; spec BEFORE code; importer side `bim-ootb prompts/DXF2BIM_MODELLER_LANE.md` §L39)
**Issue:** PDF -> DXF is a developer command (Python + poppler). A user holding a low-cost house PDF cannot reach the Modeller's Convert.
**What the user does:** Pill ▸ Open ▸ **"Open 2D drawings from PDF image (.pdf)…"**, picks the PDF. The sheets appear on the canvas exactly as if the 7 DXF files had been opened, and Convert behaves as it does today. **Paper stays the input: no form, no chooser of type or dimensions** (§33). Offline, no server, no install: it joins the air-gapped set (§L34).
**The word "image", stated:** the owner's label is used as given. **The reader needs a VECTOR PDF.** A scan has no lines to read and the ruling forbids OCR (§7), so a PDF whose plan page holds no vector lines gets a plain refusal naming that, not a guess. **To raise with the owner, once, in the report: most users will read "image" as "scan".**
**Design: the same rules, one implementation at a time:**
- `modeller/pdf2dxf/`: pure modules, UMD, usable from node and the page. One per Python stage, in stage order. Types JSONs copied from `scripts/pdf2bim/types/`.
- **Two seams, each with its own proof:**
| Seam | What | Oracle |
|---|---|---|
| Rules | sheet model -> the 7 typed DXF texts + logs | **BYTE-IDENTICAL to the Python chain**, fed the same model (dumped from the Python's own reader to JSON), on TB-LKTN and the second drawing, plus Z, B, H, S, T, G, D variants. Same `§` lines where a stage logs one |
| Reader | PDF -> sheet model, on pdf.js | the model's paths and words against Python's within stated tolerances; end to end `witness_sheets_same` tolerances + the same `geom_hash` geometry within 10 mm, on both drawings |
- **Library:** the page does not generate families. A drawing whose schedule sizes the published library lacks is refused by the existing `GAP:no_lod400_geometry` (§39); the Python stage stays the way to prepare a publish. **Therefore TB-LKTN's new door families need the owner's publish go before this entry can build it on the live site.** Witnessed against a local export until then, stated in the log.
- **Python stays as the oracle and the publish tool,** not a second product path: the page's rules are checked against it while both exist. Retiring it is a later decision.
**Witness claims:**
- W-PD1 (rules, node): byte-identical on every drawing named above; WRONG on the first differing byte, naming sheet and line; VACUOUS if a sheet is missing on either side.
- W-PD2 (reader, node): for each page: path count, pen and coordinates within 0.05 pt; words within 1.0 pt centre. Every difference counted and logged, not hidden. **Measured limit to be written here when known: pdf.js and pdftotext split and box words differently.**
- W-PD3 (page, CPU GL, real click): the new row is present; picking TB-LKTN's PDF yields 7 sheets, the same roles, `qualifies`; Convert builds 14 fills; 0 page errors; **with the network cut after one visit it still works.**
- W-PD4 (page): a raster-only PDF is refused in words and nothing is built; **a falsifier: a PDF of another type (a private building set, or the plumbing page alone) must refuse too, not build a bad house.**
- W-PD5: every `§INFER` READ / FINDING / DEFAULT is visible to the user on the panel, so a YELLOW is not silent.
**Perf budget (CLAUDE.md):** pdf.js is lazy-loaded with the entry, never on Modeller start. The plan page holds 37,907 paths: log `§PDF2DXF` wall time and heap at the end of the read.
**RESULT (2026-11-05, bim-ootb §L39):** W-PD1 `witness_pdf2dxf_rules` judged=215 fails=0: every sheet byte-identical to this chain on TB-LKTN, the second drawing and Z B H S T G D, every value and stage log line equal. W-PD2 `witness_pdf2dxf_reader` judged=28 fails=0: on every sheet the rules read, the same paths, pens and point counts, coordinates within 0.003 pt; 31 paths differ on p2, which no rule reads. **The measured limit promised above:** word centres differ by up to 2.1 pt in x and 1.2 pt in y on TB-LKTN (pdf.js shares a run's width out by characters; its font metrics are not Poppler's); some words re-split on p7 and p8 (24 on TB-LKTN's p8). The schedule, grid and tag words the rules read survive it: end to end, geometry within 0.1 mm, annotation within 23.6 mm. W-PD3/4/5 `witness_pdf2dxf_page` judged=22 fails=0. **To match pdftocairo the reader had to copy four cairo habits, each measured: a zero-width line is 0.12; a move after a move replaces it; a close with nothing drawn since the move does nothing; a line continuing the last line's direction extends it, in 1/256 fixed point.** Measured in the page: read 1.3 s, rules 2.9 s, heap 82-101 MB, under load.

## 42. THE ACCEPTANCE'S WORDING, AND THE ROW'S (owner 2026-11-05: *"go"*, on the recommendation; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** the owner's acceptance of 2026-11-04 was for TB-LKTN as a proof of concept (§30), but `to_std.py` writes it into the sheets of every PDF the chain reads, marked as stated by a person, so a stranger's PDF would list "Accepted by owner" on a decision the owner never made about it. Found while listing a film's caveats.
**Rule:** the acceptance is a rule of the TYPE, and says so: *who* = "the first-release rule of this building type (owner)", same date, *reason* = "a proof of concept, for this type's first release". The rows stay on screen. **Which codes may be accepted stays §L37's list**, in the importer: this changes the words and not what passes. Limit, stated: a drawing of this type from anyone qualifies on this rule; that is what the first release means, and the panel says it.
**The row:** "Open 2D drawings from PDF drawing (.pdf)…", replacing "from PDF image": the owner's first label, changed on his go because "image" reads as "scan" and scans are refused.
**Witness:** the same runs as §41; the 7 sheets change only on the `@ACCEPT` lines (W-T3-style diff, line count named); geometry hash unchanged; `witness_pdf2dxf_page` reads the new wording on screen.
**§42 RESULT (2026-11-05):** the sheets changed on the 6 `@ACCEPT` lines only, geometry hash unchanged; every variant `fails 0`, ruler `fails 0`, second drawing `fails 0`; port 215/0, reader 28/0, page 25/0 three runs running. Live: PRs #2655, #2656, #2658. ## 43. SCANNED DRAWINGS: A RASTER + OCR PATH, FIRST CASE A GERMAN WERKPLAN (owner 2026-11-22: *"Yes we can raster/OCR as it will be minimal for users in Germany, either they spend on that or give us CAD."*; spec BEFORE code)
**Ruling recorded:** the no-OCR ruling (§1, §7, §41) is lifted **for PDFs that hold only a scan**. A vector PDF still goes the vector path; OCR never overrides a text layer. The raster path is the fallback for users who cannot supply CAD; the user's alternative is always "give us the CAD export", and the refusal text says so.

**The case, measured (2026-11-22; `pdfinfo`, `pdfimages -list`, `pdffonts`, `pdftocairo -svg`):** file DE-1 (private client set, held outside every repo; sha256 `8f779bdd14a6…`; never committed, never named in public text, outputs stay in the session scratchpad). 4 A3 pages, each ONE 2338 x 3306 RGB JPEG at 200 dpi; 0 fonts, 0 text, 1 path (the page clip). Today `dxf_import_ui.js:1143` refuses it ("scanned image"), as designed.
- p1: a window sampling form (Bemusterung), handwritten: not a drawing; out of scope.
- p2: 4 elevations (Ansicht Süd/Nord/Ost/West) + Schnitt A-A, B-B; p3: Erdgeschoss plan, rotated 90 deg inside the sheet; p4: Obergeschoss plan. Title block: Werkplan 1:50, printed reduced to A3 (so the printed scale is FALSE on this paper; scale must come from the drawing).
- Client markups in BLUE ink over black-line drawings (window types, roller shutters). A "VORABZUG" stamp in grey.

**Is it a standard? (my reading, to be witnessed, not recalled as fact):** German building-drawing conventions (DIN 1356-1 style): dimension chains in metres with a raised last digit (3.38⁵ = 3.385 m); openings labelled width over height (1.51 / 1.31); sill height tagged BRH; room stamp (name, m²); material-hatch legend; view/section labels. **No structural grid bubbles** — the TB-LKTN type anchors on grid A–E/1–5 (§17); this type must anchor on dimension chains instead.

**THE KEY: a dimension chain is a CHECKSUM on OCR.** Each chain's segments sum to its overall (p4 top: 0.365+3.51+0.175+3.385+0.175+4.515+0.365 = 12.49, as read by eye from the 70 dpi view — to be confirmed by the probe). So an OCR number is never trusted alone: it is GREEN only when its chain closes (and, where a parallel chain spans the same extent, when both agree); otherwise YELLOW, never forced, never "corrected" to fit. This is the template-matcher (§12) for a scan: expected structure (chains that close) against what the reader makes out.

**Method (deterministic around a fixed, local OCR engine):**
1. **Route:** plan page has < PDF_MIN_PLAN_PATHS vector paths AND ≥1 page-size image → raster path (not refusal).
2. **Ink separation by colour:** black/grey = drawing; blue = markup layer (kept as markup, never geometry); thresholds from the page's own hue histogram, logged.
3. **Orientation + deskew:** from the long H/V line angle histogram (0/90/180/270 + small skew); p3's rotation is the test.
4. **Lines:** run-length on rows/columns of the binarised page (orthogonal plans): H/V segments with thickness; thick-pair poché = wall candidates (thickness histogram, not a constant).
5. **Text:** OCR runs LOCALLY ONLY (Tesseract CLI for the probe; tesseract.js vendored + lazy, like pdf.js, for the page). **No cloud OCR** (the January Google Vision route is not used: client drawings leave the machine only if the owner says so). Dimension text with a digit whitelist; the raised digit read as its own small box above-right.
6. **Chains:** a dimension line = long H/V line with tick marks; numbers bound to the segment between ticks; checksum per chain; scale px/m fitted from closed chains only, X and Y separately, must agree.
7. **Emit** the same STD-layer DXF sheets the vector path emits (`to_std`), so DXF2BIM / Convert is unchanged downstream.

**Language/type:** a new editable type template `german_werkplan_efh.json` beside `low_cost_single_storey_unit.json` (§13 pattern): chain notation, W/H label form, BRH, room-stamp form, view words (Ansicht, Schnitt, Erdgeschoss, Obergeschoss, Speicher, Legende). Room names need German OCR data (`deu`), not installed today (`tesseract --list-langs`: eng, osd) — a fetch of public data, not client data.

**Witness claims (each can say NO-OP / VACUOUS / WRONG):**
| ID | Claim | Issue it exposes |
|---|---|---|
| W-S1 | DE-1 routes to raster, a vector PDF (TB-LKTN) still routes vector, byte-identical to today | the new route steals vector PDFs |
| W-S2 | p3 orientation found = the one that makes its text upright; skew logged | rotated sheets read sideways |
| W-S3 | blue ink pixels removed from drawing layer, count logged; 0 blue on TB-LKTN render (falsifier) | client markup read as walls/dims |
| W-S4 | per chain: numbers read, sum vs overall, verdict GREEN/YELLOW; INCONCLUSIVE if 0 chains | OCR numbers trusted unchecked |
| W-S5 | scale from closed chains: X vs Y agree within 0.5 %; printed 1:50 shown FALSE on this A3 | scale taken from the title block |
| W-S6 | EG and OG overall extents agree (same building) | per-sheet drift |
| W-S7 | wall thickness histogram; exterior ring closes on the chain extents | pairing invents walls |
| W-S8 | openings: each W/H label bound to a wall gap of that width (±1 chain step) | label/gap mis-binding |

**Step 0 = probe only (no product code):** p4 (cleanest), Tesseract CLI, numbers + chains, W-S4/W-S5 measured, result appended here. Port to JS (`modeller/pdf2dxf/raster_*.js`) only after the probe shows chains close at a useful rate; the product row/refusal wording changes in `bim-ootb prompts/DXF2BIM_MODELLER_LANE.md` (§L40) then.
**Perf budget:** a 2338 x 3306 RGBA page = 31 MB per page in the browser; process one page at a time, release before the next; log heap per page.
**Positioning note (stated, not hidden):** Tesseract's recogniser is a fixed trained model running offline. It is a reader, never a decider: every value it gives passes a checksum or stays yellow.
**§43 step 0 RESULT so far (2026-11-22, `scripts/pdf2bim/raster_probe.py`, DE-1 p4, log `raster_probe_p4.log` in the session scratchpad): NOT YET USEFUL — 0 GREEN chains.**
- The reader is plain code: numpy + the local Tesseract CLI. No LLM call at run time; same page in, same log out. My eye-read of the chains (above) is NOT in the code and is used by nothing.
- W-S3 blue markup: 7,434 px removed (excess-blue > 40; tail>80 = 870). W-S2 skew: +0.22 deg (projection profile); before deskew a dimension line spread over 3 pixel rows and broke into pieces.
- Lines: 76 H + 44 V thin lines ≥ 300 px; ticks taken as a contiguous dark run crossing the line (text near a line is not a tick). The overall 12.49 chain is found (ticks 2, span 902 px).
- OCR, three readers tried, all measured on the same chains:
  (a) band OCR on the binarised page: garbage ("364" for 3.51); (b) band OCR on grey at 4x: main numbers mostly right (12.49, 3.51, 3.38, 4.51, 4.69, 3.26), raised digits mostly lost or merged ("115" = 11+raised 5), best chain read 6 of 7 segments; (c) glyph clustering + majority vote: 544 digit glyphs fall into 240 clusters at correlation 0.80 (too fragmented at 13 px glyph height), only 248 labelled; worse than (b).
- **Measured limit:** main digit height 13 px, raised digit 8 px, at the scan's 200 dpi. This is the edge of what Tesseract reads.
- **Next, in order:** combine (b) for main numbers with clustering for the raised digit only (its shapes are few); loosen clustering with a measured threshold (the within-label correlation histogram of the clusters that voted unanimously); merge collinear line fragments (row 2 still logs twice). Stop rule: if chains still do not close at a useful rate on p4, the answer to German users is "give us the CAD", or a 300-400 dpi rescan, stated as a measured limit and not hidden.
**§43 step 0c RESULT (2026-11-22, owner: *"go ahead with the next step"*; same probe, same log name): STILL 0 GREEN, but now ONE digit from closing on the best chain; the checksum caught every misread.**
Changes, each forced by a log line:
| Change | Evidence that forced it | Effect (log) |
|---|---|---|
| main numbers = grey band OCR; raised digit split by geometry (bottom above baseline by > 0.25 x main height) | reader (b) best for mains | `§RASTER_RAISED n=48 matched=45` |
| raised digit decided by TEMPLATE, the OCR fold read NOT a veto | `§RASTER_RAISED_DIAG`: templates say '5' for 43 of 48, Tesseract's fold read says '6' for 13 of them — OCR biased on 8 px glyphs | raised now read `^5` on every chain end |
| touching digits split at the min-ink column when wider than 1.3 x median digit width (7.0 px, measured) | pixel dump: 3 and 6 of "36⁵" are one component | `split_wide=109` |
| decimal separator = a widened gap holding grey < 215 at the baseline; gap threshold = valley of the gap histogram (measured 4 px) | pixel dumps: the dot is grey, not ink at Otsu 167; fixed gap 3 made "12,49" read "1,2,49" | `§RASTER_SEP_GAP valley_threshold>=4`, 51 separators |
| main-digit k-NN vote (K 7, share 0.6) over all labelled glyphs of the sheet | Tesseract confuses 3/4, 2/9 at 13 px | `§RASTER_KNN kept=198 changed=13 no_majority=29` |
| duplicate lines dropped (contained extent within 3 px) | row 2 logged twice | `dropped_dupes=9/3` |
**Checksum trail (the witness doing its job):** top chain row 2: d = +1010 mm (3,38⁵ read 4,39⁵) -> **+10 mm** after k-NN (read 3,39⁵: one 8/9 confusion left); row 3: +1700 -> +700 mm (3,26⁵ read 3,96⁵: one 2/9 left); vertical overall 11.99: +8480 -> +1480 mm (9,63⁵ corrected to 2,63⁵; "177" for 17⁵ left = a raised 5 not split on a vertical chain). Every one stays YELLOW; none was fitted.
**Open, in order:** (1) the vertical "17⁵ -> 177" raised split; (2) 8/9 and 2/9 at 13 px (few '8' samples: bank 6); (3) window chain (8/15 segments: the 1.51/1.31 width-over-height pairs need the below-line reading, spec'd as W-S8); (4) W-S5 scale stays INCONCLUSIVE until one chain closes. **Measured position:** the method works up to single-glyph confusions; whether those close at 200 dpi is still open. A 300-400 dpi rescan of the same sheet would answer it in one run.
**§43 step 0d RESULT (2026-11-22, owner: *"Proceed, and if able and well formed, load in Modeller and convert to DB"*): W-S5 PASS, W-S4 2 GREEN; NOT yet well formed for the Modeller (reasons below).**
- Fix 1 — raised 5 touching a 7 (`17⁵` read `177`): `§RASTER_WORD_DIAG` pixel dump showed one component = 7 + raised 5; after a width split each piece now takes its OWN height and is re-classified (`raised_from_split=17`).
- Fix 2 — a tick missed on the vertical chain (sum short by exactly one 11⁵ = -115 mm): pixel dump showed ticks are short DIAGONALS that fade to grey; tick test now uses a 3-column window on the grey-inclusive mask (< 215).
- **W-S4:** H row 3 `36⁵|3,51|17⁵|4,69⁵|11⁵|3,26⁵|36⁵` = 12.490 vs overall 12.49, **d = 0 mm GREEN**; V right `36⁵|4,01|17⁵|2,63⁵|11⁵|4,32⁵|36⁵` = 11.990 vs 11.99, **d = 0 mm GREEN**. H row 2 still +10 mm (one 8 read 9, 7 '8' samples on the sheet) — YELLOW, not fitted.

The raster path today yields only dimension chains + scale. Converting what exists would either be refused by the gate or need walls I would be inventing between chain ticks — Prime Rule.
- **What the closed chains already give (the bridge to walls):** a German inner-dimension chain IS a wall/room sequence along a cut: H = ext wall 36.5 | room 3.51 | wall 17.5 | room 4.695 | wall 11.5 | room 3.265 | ext wall 36.5 (tick px known); V likewise. Next steps, in order, each a witness:
  1. **W-S7 chain-anchored wall tracing:** at each GREEN chain's wall segment (thickness from the chain), follow the wall band perpendicular across the plan pixels while both faces stay present; ring closes on 12.49 x 11.99.

  4. Then emit STD-layer DXF sheets -> `dxf2bim_import` gate -> Modeller Convert -> DB, as TB-LKTN (§23-§26).

### §43.1 STEP 1 SPEC — CHAIN-ANCHORED WALL TRACING (W-S7) (owner 2026-11-22: *"go ahead with step 1"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Input (no re-derivation):** `raster_probe.py` writes `chains.json` (skew, Otsu, scale H/V px/m, every chain with tick px, segment values, verdict). `raster_walls.py` reads it plus the same page PNG. Only GREEN chains anchor walls; YELLOW chains are listed, not used.
**Rule (German inner-dimension chain, measured not assumed):** in a GREEN chain the segments alternate wall | room | wall … starting and ending with an exterior wall. A segment is a WALL candidate when its value is below the smallest room value of that chain (here walls 0.115-0.365, rooms ≥ 2.6 m) — derived per chain, logged. A H chain's walls are VERTICAL bands at [tick_i, tick_i+1]; a V chain's are HORIZONTAL bands.
**Trace:** for a band [a, b] (px), walk along the wall axis row by row across the plan body; a row is WALL when both faces show ink at the band edges (±2 px) AND the band interior is not paper (ink+grey fraction ≥ the value measured on the band's own first confirmed rows). Runs shorter than 2 x wall thickness are dropped as noise; gaps ≤ 1 px bridged; LONGER gaps are kept as gaps (= candidate openings, step 2), never filled.
**Witness W-S7 (can say NO-OP / VACUOUS / WRONG):**
| ID | Claim | Issue exposed |
|---|---|---|
| W-S7a | each wall band's measured thickness (face-to-face px / scale) equals its chain value ± 1 px | chain ticks are not the wall faces (extension-line offset) |
| W-S7b | the 4 exterior walls (first/last of the H and V GREEN chains) form a closed ring: each one's traced extent reaches the other two within 1 wall thickness; ring = 12.49 x 11.99 m | ring open / invented corners |
| W-S7c | interior walls: each traced run ends on another wall (T or L junction) or at a gap logged as an opening candidate | free-floating walls |
| W-S7d | a falsifier: the same trace on a band shifted half a room into a room interior finds NO wall (≤ 5 % of rows) | tracer reads hatch/furniture as wall |
**Output:** `walls.json` (mm, origin = exterior outer corner top-left, y down as on the sheet, flip stated when emitting DXF) + `§RASTER_WALL` lines. VACUOUS if 0 GREEN chains.
**§43.1 RESULT (2026-11-22; `raster_walls.py`, log `raster_walls_p4.log`, input `chains.json` + `page_clean.png` written by the probe — one owner for deskew/blue removal):**
- **W-S7a PASS 8/8** (face-line CENTRE to centre, ±1 px): 36⁵ walls 0.374 / 0.360 / 0.361 / 0.361 m, 17⁵ 0.167 / 0.173, 11⁵ 0.117 / 0.116. First attempt measured outer edges and read +32..+51 mm on every wall = the stroke width, a systematic bias, fixed by taking line centres (a dimension is to the drawn face line).
- **W-S7b PASS:** 4 exterior bands each reach both perpendicular exterior walls; body 12.486 x 11.985 m vs printed 12.49 x 11.99.
- **W-S7d PASS:** 6 falsifier bands (mid-room, same width) -> 0.0 % rows in wall runs. First attempt FAILED at 13 %: a hatch-FILL threshold cannot separate wall from window (fill histogram unimodal 0.3-0.5 on exterior walls). Replaced by a TEXTURE test: hatch is DIAGONAL (ink moves 1 px per row), window glass/frame lines are STRAIGHT (same columns row to row); thin walls (inner < 5 px) cannot show hatch -> faces alone, stated.
- **Openings fall out of the trace (input for step 2, not yet judged):** faces + straight texture (window-like) right wall 1.51 / 1.52 / 1.54 m, top 1.49 / 1.55 / 1.48 m, bottom 1.45 / 0.98 / 1.44 m — the drawing's labels say 1.51 and 1.01; the ±40 mm spread is the smoothing window, to be tightened in W-S8 against the labels. Left wall: one window-like 0.97 m and two FACE-ABSENT gaps 0.90 / 0.93 m (drawn differently; W-S8 classifies them).
- **Output:** `walls.json` 26 wall runs, 55.86 m, mm from the outer top-left corner.
- **W-S7c scope (stated, not hidden):** only the 8 wall bands crossed by the two GREEN chains are traced. Interior walls crossed only by YELLOW chains are not seen yet. **Two routes to the rest, next:** (a) close more chains (the 8/9 glyph confusion), (b) a general wall finder: the same diagonal-hatch texture test scanned over the whole plan body, its thickness vocabulary taken from the GREEN chains (0.115 / 0.175 / 0.365 m) — every found wall must still pass W-S7a and the falsifier.

### §43.2 STEP 1b SPEC — WHOLE-PLAN WALL SEARCH (W-S9) (owner 2026-11-22: *"go ahead with the whole-plan wall search"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Input:** `chains.json`, `page_clean.png`, `walls.json` (§43.1). Thickness VOCABULARY = the wall values of the GREEN chains (here 0.115 / 0.175 / 0.365 m), px via the W-S5 scale. Nothing else is a wall thickness.
**Detector (one rule for every band, vertical and horizontal):** a face = a stroke running along the wall axis; a band of width T (vocabulary) is WALL on a row when both faces are present AND (T thick: interior texture DIAGONAL > STRAIGHT, as §43.1) or (T thin, inner < 5 px: interior PAPER — nothing between the faces). Runs smoothed and split exactly as §43.1. **Minimum run length per thickness = the shortest run of that thickness CONFIRMED by §43.1** (measured, logged), not a constant. Overlapping candidates (same axis, x within T/2) keep the one whose faces are strongest.
**Witness claims:**
| ID | Claim | Issue exposed |
|---|---|---|
| W-S9a | recall: every §43.1 wall run is re-found (≥ 90 % of its length) | the scanner is blind to walls the anchored trace saw |
| W-S9b | every accepted wall's face-centre thickness = a vocabulary value ± 1 px; others REJECTED and counted | stray parallel lines taken as walls |
| W-S9c | connectivity falsifier: a wall is GREEN only if it is joined (end or face within one thickness) to the network grown from the exterior ring; isolated candidates (furniture, room-stamp boxes, stair lines) are YELLOW, listed, NOT emitted | furniture/text read as walls |
| W-S9d | total accepted length and per-thickness length printed; the ring of §43.1 is part of the network (else INCONCLUSIVE) | vacuous network |
**Output:** `walls_all.json` (GREEN only) + `§RASTER_SCAN_*` lines; the YELLOW list beside it.
**§43.2 RESULT (2026-11-22; `raster_wallscan.py`, shared rule `raster_texture.py`, logs `raster_wallscan_p4.log` + re-run `raster_walls_p4.log`):**
- **W-S9a recall 27/27 PASS** (every §43.1 run covered ≥ 90 % by the network; coverage counts walls of either axis, since a corner square belongs to the perpendicular wall).
- **W-S9b:** thickness measured on every raw candidate BEFORE suppression (vertical 204 of 450 verified, horizontal 288 of 733); the measurement decides the class, the template only proposes (`relabelled` 29 / 98).
- **W-S9c:** 46 walls in the network grown from 16 exterior seeds; **49 isolated candidates YELLOW, not emitted** — on the first runs these were exactly the 6 room-stamp tables. **Glazing rule:** a band thinner than the exterior wall inside the exterior strip is window glass/frame, not a wall: 23 removed, kept as opening evidence (`§RASTER_SCAN_GLAZING`).
- **W-S9d:** 81.37 m = 0.365: 32.90 m (exterior, windows as gaps), 0.175: 30.00 m, 0.115: 18.47 m.
- **Defects found and fixed on the way, each from a log line:** (1) suppression before the thickness check let thick false candidates evict true thin walls (recall 10 -> 21/26); (2) a minimum run = "shortest confirmed run" dropped short pieces between openings -> back to the §43.1 rule 2 x T; (3) thin bands required paper inside, stricter than §43.1's faces-alone rule -> aligned; (4) **the scan had RE-IMPLEMENTED the texture rule** (OR of the two diagonals) and bridged windows (top wall one run 194-5246 mm across a window) -> ONE owner `raster_texture.py` (max of the two diagonals), used by both scripts; §43.1 re-run still W-S7a 8/8, ring PASS, falsifier 0 %; (5) join tolerance was one exterior thickness (26 px) and pulled in the Bad stamp/furniture -> touching = 3 px.
- **NOT witnessed yet (stated):** PRECISION of the 46 network walls. Diagnostic overlay (not evidence) shows candidates to doubt: a thick piece at the Bad washbasin, the stair's centre wall, a thin double line in the Ankleide. **Next witness W-S10 (room areas):** flood the rooms bounded by the network (door gaps closed at the jambs) and compare each with the area printed in its room stamp (F = 12,55 m² … read by the same raster reader + checksum-style cross-check: Σ room areas vs body minus walls). Same idea as TB-LKTN's R1 (§17). Then W-S8 openings against the 1.51/1.31 labels.

### §43.3 STEP 1c SPEC — ROOM AREAS AS THE PRECISION WITNESS (W-S10) (owner 2026-11-22: *"Proceed check"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** §43.2 proves recall, not precision: a false wall (furniture, stair line) inside the network is invisible to W-S9. A room is bounded by walls on every side, so ONE wrong or missing wall changes that room's area; the drawing prints each room's area (F) and perimeter (U) in its stamp — an independent oracle per room.
**Method:** rasterise `walls_all.json` into a wall mask over the body; close DOOR gaps = a gap between two collinear wall pieces of the same thickness on the same band (a room is closed by definition; logged per gap, width printed); flood the free space; each component = a room candidate. Area = pixels / (KH·KV); perimeter = boundary length in m. The stamp: an isolated §43.2 YELLOW box (W-S9c) or the text block found inside the component; OCR'd locally (Tesseract), parse `F = n,nn m²` and `U = n,nn m`.
**Witness W-S10 (per room; can say NO-OP / VACUOUS / WRONG):** computed F vs printed F and computed U vs printed U. **No tolerance is assumed up front:** the first run prints every delta; if the deltas share one factor (a plaster deduction, e.g. DIN 277/WoFlV practice) that factor is REPORTED as a finding with its spread, never applied silently; a room outside the shared spread is a WALL defect candidate (named room, which side). VACUOUS if no stamp is read; a room whose stamp value fails to parse is YELLOW, not dropped.
**§43.3 RESULT (2026-11-22; `raster_rooms.py`, log `raster_rooms_p4.log`; oracle = `stamps_eyeread.json` in the session scratchpad, NOT committed (private client values)):**

### §43.5 SPEC — CLOSE MORE CHAINS (owner 2026-11-22: *"go ahead with A"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** only 2 chains GREEN; the wall-thickness vocabulary (W-S9) comes only from them, so a wall of another thickness (the Bad's "12⁵") is rejected. The best YELLOW chain fails on one 8 read as 9.
1. **Hole count as a deterministic digit constraint:** number of enclosed background regions of the glyph (ink mask; regions not touching the glyph box border). Digits 8 = 2; 0 4 6 9 = 1 (4 may read 0 when its loop is open); 1 2 3 5 7 = 0. Applied ONLY when the measured holes contradict the current label AND exactly one digit among the glyph's k-NN vote candidates fits the hole count; otherwise the label stands or becomes '?'. Logged per change (`§RASTER_HOLES`), with the histogram of holes per label over the bank (it must show the rule holds on this sheet before it is trusted: e.g. labelled '8' glyphs mostly 2 holes).
2. **Every GREEN chain feeds §43.1** (today only the first H and V); the vocabulary is the union of their wall values; a new value is logged `§RASTER_SCAN_VOCAB new=`.
**Witness:** W-S4 GREEN count before/after (must not drop a previously GREEN chain — regression); W-S5 still PASS; §43.1/§43.2/§43.3 re-run with numbers compared to the previous log.
**§43.5 RESULT (2026-11-22; `raster_probe.py`, log `raster_probe_p4.log`): A REACHES ITS MEASURED LIMIT at 200 dpi — no new GREEN chain without letting the checksum pick digits (forbidden).**
- **Run 1, hole COUNT rule — REGRESSION, disabled (`HOLE_RULE = False`, kept for the record):** it fixed row 2 (3,39⁵ -> 3,38⁵, GREEN) but broke both GREEN chains (4,69⁵ -> 4,68⁵; overall 11.99 -> 11.88) and 36⁵ -> 38⁵: at 200 dpi the tail of a 9 and the top of a 6 often close a SECOND small hole (`§RASTER_HOLES_DIAG`: '9' {1:11, 2:6}, '6' {1:22, 2:4}). My first reading of that histogram ("the 2-hole 9s are 8s") was wrong; the regression witness the spec required caught it.
- **Run 2, hole POSITION rule (`§RASTER_HOLES_GEO`), kept:** the LARGEST hole's height separates 9 (cy 0.27-0.36) from 0 (0.45-0.51) from {6, 8} (0.58-0.70) on every labelled glyph of the sheet; **6 vs 8 is NOT separable** (6s show the same small upper hole as 8s). The row-2 glyph (labelled 9, big hole low at 0.70) is therefore not a 9; its vote is 8:3 / 6:3 -> **'?'** (unread, not wrong). W-S4 still 2 GREEN, W-S5 PASS — no regression, no gain.
- **Consequence for the vocabulary (§43.5 item 2):** VACUOUS — no new GREEN chain to add a thickness, so the Bad's "12⁵" wall stays outside W-S9.
- **The two levers left (owner decision, not taken):** (a) a 300-400 dpi rescan of the same sheets (one run answers whether 6/8 and the stamps become readable); (b) a weaker verdict class **GREEN-RESOLVED**: when shape + votes narrow ONE glyph to exactly two digits and exactly one of them closes the chain, accept it, flagged as resolved-by-checksum (that chain then no longer verifies that glyph). Not done without the owner's word, because it uses the oracle to choose.

### §43.6 SPEC — READY FOR A RESCAN (owner 2026-11-22: *"(a)"* = 300-400 dpi rescan; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** pixel constants were set at 200 dpi (line ≥ 300 px, gap 3, text band 30, tick ≥ 4/≤ 12, face ±2, thin < 5, join 3, OCR upscale 4/6/8). A rescan must run UNCHANGED.
**Rule:** the probe takes the scan's DPI (`argv[3]`, default 200; for a PDF read it from `pdfimages -list` x-ppi), F = DPI/200; every pixel constant above × F (rounded, ≥ 1); OCR upscale ÷ F (≥ 1) so glyphs reach Tesseract at the same size. `chains.json` carries `dpi`; walls/scan/rooms read it. Everything already relative (main glyph height, hole geometry, histogram valleys, scale px/m) is untouched.
**Witness:** W-S11a regression — 200 dpi run: every `§` verdict and number identical to the last log. W-S11b mechanics — the SAME scan rendered at 400 dpi with `dpi=400`: the same chains GREEN, scale px/m × 2.000 ± 0.5 %, walls/rooms in metres within 1 % of the 200 run. (An interpolated render adds no information; this proves only that the scaling is right.)
**Client request (for the owner to send):** rescan the ORIGINAL-SIZE prints (A1/A2, not the A3 reduction) at 400 dpi, colour or greyscale, PNG/TIFF or PDF with lossless/high-quality images; or simply the CAD-exported PDF/DWG — then no OCR is needed at all.

An interpolation adds no information; OCR is input-size sensitive — a measured caution for the real rescan, not a defect of the scaling.

(2) a 2-segment GREEN chain (7,36⁵ | 4,62⁵) became the wall anchor and made 4.625 m a "wall" (rooms then merged into one 81 m² blob): **a chain anchors walls only if it begins and ends with a wall segment (≥ 3 segments)**; at 400 the walls stage now says VACUOUS (no wall-bounded V chain), and wallscan/rooms say VACUOUS instead of reading an older run's file (stale outputs are deleted at stage start).

**Thickness (W-S9 widened):** the search proposes EVERY thickness from the smallest to the largest READ chain value in 1 px steps (not only the READ values); the face-centre MEASUREMENT decides: within ±1 px of a READ value → READ; else consistent with its own template (±1 px) → MEASURED (a FINDING, logged with its value); nothing measurable → the registry DEFAULT `wall.thickness` (bim-ootb `modeller/element_defaults.json`, 0.15 m), marked DEFAULT. The connectivity and glazing falsifiers (§43.2) are unchanged; W-S9a recall and W-S10 rooms re-run and compared.
**To the importer (one mechanism, no second defaults engine):** the scan path writes the SAME STD sheet form as the vector path (`to_std.py`: A-WALL-FULL closed footprints, A-GLAZ wall-thick rectangles at exterior glazing gaps, door gaps left open in A-WALL-FULL, `@UNITS=mm`, `@SET=STD`, `@LEVEL`); every value it cannot read goes as `@DEFAULT=<registry key>,<value>` (storey/wall height, door height, window sill/height) so the importer shows it amber. Then the importer's own gate + convert (`gate_probe.js`, bim-ootb read-only) — its `§VERDICT` is the judge; nothing is accepted on its behalf.

### §43.9 SPEC — ISOLATE THE VIEW BLOCKS, THEN THE ROOF (owner 2026-11-23: *"There is roof on the PDF 2nd page. Perhaps isolating blocks of images should help to extract them?"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** p2 holds 4 Ansichten + Schnitt A-A + Schnitt B-B + legend + title block on one sheet; reading it whole mixes lines and numbers across views (§43.8: 0 GREEN).
**Blocks (deterministic):** ink mask (blue removed, deskewed — the probe's `page_clean.png`), downsampled by 8 (OR-pooling), dilated by a gap MEASURED from the page (the whitespace run-length histogram's valley between in-drawing gaps and between-view gaps, logged); connected components = blocks; blocks < 1 % of the page ink are text/clutter and attach to the nearest block. Each block is named by its CAPTION: the large text below/near it OCR'd locally (Ansicht Süd/Nord/Ost/West, Schnitt A-A/B-B); a block with no caption read is `UNNAMED`, never guessed.
**Roof from a gable view (geometry only):** upper envelope y_top(x) of the block's ink; the roof = the two longest straight runs of the envelope that rise and fall; slope by Theil-Sen (median of pairwise slopes, deterministic, robust to the chimney/trees); pitch = atan(slope · KH/KV). Eaves = where each slope run ends, ridge = their apex; heights above the block's ground line (its longest bottom horizontal) in metres via the sheet scale.
**Witness W-S13:** (b) each elevation's building width in px / the plan's 12.49 or 11.99 m = the sheet scale, and must equal p4's 72.24 / 72.34 px/m ± 0.5 % (same print) — a different value is a FINDING; (c) pitch left vs right slope agree ± 1° and Ost vs West agree ± 1°; the section's printed pitch text, if OCR reads it, is the third source; (d) VACUOUS if no block is a gable view.
**§43.9 RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_blocks.py`, `raster_roof.py`, logs `raster_blocks_p2.log`, `raster_roof_p2.log`):**
- **Whitespace blocks FAIL as a separator (measured):** the sheet frame joined everything into 1 block; with the frame removed (15,156 px of straight runs ≥ half the page) the count still falls 16 → 3 → 1 as the dilation grows (ground lines and dimension lines run from one view into the next). Kept as a log, not used.
- **CAPTIONS are the seeds — READ:** page OCR at 2x finds all six: ANSICHT SÜD (224,906) NORD (1172,904) OST (2254,904) WEST (1955,1407), SCHNITT A-A (370,1828) B-B (1964,1821), conf 89-92; also the storey labels SPEICHER / OBERGESCHOSS / ERDGESCHOSS beside Schnitt A-A (y 1366 / 1514 / 1676) and **"DACHNEIGUNG 22 GRAD" (the 22 at conf 96)** inside Schnitt A-A.
- **First geometric agreement:** ANSICHT OST's left roof slope (Theil-Sen on the upper envelope) = **22.6°** vs the printed 22°. One slope only — NOT yet a W-S13c pass (which needs both slopes of two gables).
- **Not right yet (named, from the log):** (1) nearest-caption assignment lets ANSICHT WEST swallow ink of Schnitt B-B and neighbours (bbox 1137..3191) — views need a BOUNDARY (e.g. the gap columns/rows between caption-anchored seed regions), not nearest-caption; (2) building columns come out 4.6-6.8 m wide vs 12.49 / 11.99 because **the elevations are NOT at the plan's scale** (visibly ~half size on the sheet), so 72.24 px/m is wrong for them — W-S13b reads FINDING, not PASS; the elevation scale must be MEASURED first (its own dimension text, or the section's height chain once it closes, or the building width = a known plan side as an explicit hypothesis to test across all four faces).
**▶ RESUME §43.9:** view boundaries → per-view scale → gable pitch both slopes (W-S13c vs the printed 22°) → eaves/ridge heights → STD elevation + roof sheets → importer gate. Open from §43.7/§43.8 as before.

### §43.10 SPEC — VIEW SEPARATION BY COMPETING GROWTH FROM THE CAPTIONS (owner 2026-11-23: *"Proceed with separation logic"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue (§43.9):** nearest-caption assignment let ANSICHT WEST swallow Schnitt B-B; whitespace blocks merge through ground/dimension lines.
**Rule:** seeds = ink fragments whose bottom lies within 60 px (× DPI/200) ABOVE a caption and overlap the caption's x-range (the view's ground line / base sits on its caption). All seeds grow AT ONCE (multi-source breadth-first) through the ink mask dilated by a small radius (the in-drawing gap); each pixel takes the label that reaches it first — a line joining two views is split at its geodesic midpoint. Ink no growth reaches (trees, people, loose text) joins the region whose pixels are Euclidean-nearest within one dilation step × 10, else stays UNASSIGNED (logged with its bbox; the legend and title block are expected there).
**Witness W-S14:** (a) disjoint: no view's bbox contains ANOTHER view's caption; (b) every caption's own seed ends in its own view; (c) assigned vs unassigned ink printed; (d) the §43.9 roof run repeated per view (pitch slopes, view widths in px) — values compared with §43.9, changes named.
**§43.10 RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_roof.py`, log `raster_roof_p2.log`, 7 s):**
- **W-S14a PASS:** 6 views grown from their caption seeds (SÜD 13 seed fragments, NORD 6, OST 4, WEST 8, B-B 3, A-A 1), disjoint — no view's bbox contains another view's caption; WEST no longer swallows Schnitt B-B (bbox 1795..2730 x 1128..1419; B-B 1793..2523 x 1544..1773).
- **W-S14c:** 44.8 % of the ink unassigned (bbox (17,413)-(3192,1931)); the legend and title block are expected there, but the unassigned box spans most of the sheet, so what else it holds is not yet measured.

The pitch value to build with is the PRINTED 22° (READ), corroborated by geometry.

Then eaves / ridge heights in metres, STD elevation + roof sheets (pitch 22° READ), importer gate.

**Measured first:** the elevation SCALE cannot be read yet — level-mark OCR is garbled (conf 0-46, "48.54", "42.43"); building corners per view are inconsistent (one view 315 px, others find one corner). **North:** no north arrow read → plan top = north is a DEFAULT, stated; faces S (+X), N (−X), E (+Y), W (−Y) follow from it.

### §43.12 SPEC — THE ROOF PROFILE + HEIGHTS FROM THE SECTION CHAIN (owner 2026-11-23: *"Proceed but the 2nd page top right i can see clearly is side elevation showing roof profile"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Roof in the importer's terms (read from its source, `dxf2shell.js` l.342/369/382, `dxf2bim_import.js` l.525):** roof sheet states `@PITCH` (the printed 22°, READ), one `A-ROOF-GABLE` closed rectangle = the top storey's wall envelope (taken from the EMITTED exterior walls, not the printed overall — the roof outline must equal the envelope), one `A-ROOF-RIDGE` 3D LINE through its centre along x (gables are OST/WEST, §43.10 → the ridge runs E-W) at z = ridge; every elevation draws a level line (A-ANNO-LEVL) at its `@ROOF` z.
**Heights from Schnitt A-A's height chain without reading separators:** the chain's tick px are exact; its digit strings are OCR. For each segment try the decimal placements (d/1000, d/100, d/10, d); a single px-per-m k must explain EVERY segment within ± 1 px (× DPI/200). If exactly one placement-set fits → the values are READ-by-proportion and k is the section's scale (logged with residuals); if none or several fit → INCONCLUSIVE, heights stay DEFAULT. With k: OST's eaves and ridge above its ground line (if OST shares the section's scale — tested: OST's eave-to-eave width / k must lie within the plan depth 11.99 m + 2 x a plausible overhang, else FINDING).
**Witness:** W-S15a proportion fit (residuals per segment); W-S15b the importer: `GAP:roof_top_not_drawn` and `GAP:roof_outline_mismatch` gone, roof planes built with pitch 22°; IFC/DB counts again.

### §43.13 SPEC — THE SHEET ARRANGEMENT AS A REFERENCE (owner 2026-11-23: *"Perhaps use the arrangement of drawing blocks as a standard reference to look for any other similar PDFs"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Idea (owner):** a German Werkplan set has a recognisable ARRANGEMENT — a views sheet (elevations in a row, sections below, legend + title block at the right) and plan sheets (one storey plan, legend, title block, the storey name as its caption). That arrangement is part of the TYPE TEMPLATE (§12/§13: the expected set the matcher looks for), next to the markings.
**Fingerprint (learned, then editable JSON `types/german_werkplan_layout.json`):** per sheet kind, the ANCHOR WORDS (ANSICHT, SCHNITT, LEGENDE, WERKPLAN, VORABZUG, the storey-name CLASS `…GESCHOSS`/SPEICHER) with their centres normalised to the page (0..1) after the page's best rotation (0/90/180/270, the one with most anchor hits).
**Match:** a page is scored against each sheet kind: anchors found of the expected ones, each within 0.12 of its normalised position; the kind with the best score ≥ 0.5 wins, else `UNKNOWN`. GREEN = expected anchor found in place; YELLOW = expected but missing/displaced, or present but unexpected — never forced.
**Witness W-S16 (generalisation, not self-match):** learn from p2 (views) and p4 (OG plan) ONLY; then (a) p3 — the EG plan, drawn ROTATED, never learned — must classify as a plan sheet; (b) p1 — the window form — must be UNKNOWN (falsifier); (c) p2, p4 self-match printed for completeness, not counted as evidence.
**§43.12 RESULT (2026-11-23; logs `raster_emit_p4.log`, `lib_probe_p4.log`, `ifc2db.log`):**
- **W-S15a INCONCLUSIVE (correct):** Schnitt A-A's height chain, px [13, 135, 11, 134, 10, 149] vs digits [2, 254, 22, 264, 20, 3794]: NO decimal placement fits one scale within ± 1 px. Three segments (0.22 / 2.64 / 0.20) agree on ~50.7 px/m, "254" and "379⁴" do not → at least two OCR readings are wrong. The agreeing subset is NOT picked (that would choose readings to fit). Heights stay DEFAULT.
- **W-S15b PASS — the roof is built:** roof sheet `@PITCH=22` (READ), `A-ROOF` + `A-ROOF-GABLE` = the emitted exterior envelope (0,0)-(12486,11985), `A-ROOF-RIDGE` along x at z 5421 (DERIVED: 3.0 m DEFAULT + 11.985/2·tan 22°); every elevation draws level lines at 3000 and 5421. Importer: `roofZ bottom 3, top 5.421, gaps []` — `GAP:roof_top_not_drawn` and `GAP:roof_outline_mismatch` gone; blocking 79 → **69**. **IFC: 76 IfcWall + 2 IfcRoof** (42,737 bytes) → **DB: 78 elements (IfcWall 76, IfcRoof 2), max z 5.421.**
- Still blocking (69): 12 `GAP:fill_unbound` windows, `GAP:slab_no_underside`, 56 QF dimension/grid/level rows (+ `QF:elev_level_mismatch` 4).
**§43.13 RESULT — W-S16 (owner's layout idea) PASS on the unseen page:** fingerprint learned from p2 + p4 ONLY (`scripts/pdf2bim/types/german_werkplan_layout.json`: generic anchor words + frame-normalised centres, editable); positions are normalised to the DRAWING'S FRAME (ink bbox), not the page, and the rotation is chosen by the best template score. **p3 (EG plan, drawn rotated, never learned) → `plan` at rotation 270°** (WERKPLAN + ERDGESCHOSS GREEN, LEGENDE YELLOW missing; score 0.67) — the rotation it finds is also p3's processing orientation (W-S2); **p1 (window form) → UNKNOWN** (falsifier PASS); p2 → views 1.00, p4 → plan 1.00 (self, not evidence). The first attempt FAILED on p3 for two named reasons (page-normalised positions; rotation tie broken by word count, 180° = SCHNITT marks) — both fixed. Limit, stated: the plan fingerprint has only 3 anchors and shares LEGENDE + WERKPLAN with the views sheet (a views sheet scores 0.67 as a plan in the page-normalised run) — more anchors (title-block fields, north arrow, scale text) would widen the margin.
**▶ RESUME (§43.11-§43.13):** EG via p3 at 270° (plan chain → walls → storey under the OG, clears `slab_no_underside`); window @SCHEDULE from the measured gaps; DIMENSIONs from the GREEN chains; more layout anchors; JS port for the Modeller page.

### §43.14 SPEC — THE GROUND FLOOR (EG) FROM p3 AS THE STOREY UNDER THE OG (2026-11-23, 'resume 2D' next item 1; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue:** the POC builds the OG as the ONLY storey; the set is two storeys (EG p3 + OG p4), so the OG floats with no storey below (`GAP:slab_no_underside`) and half the house is missing. p3 is drawn ROTATED inside its sheet; §43.13 found its rotation (270°) from the sheet layout, never by hand.

The probe then runs on that page UNCHANGED (W-S2 deskew logged as before).
**Plan chain on p3 (unchanged stages, same 200 dpi):** `raster_probe.py` → `raster_walls.py` → `raster_wallscan.py`; `raster_rooms.py` only if an EG stamp fixture exists (none: VACUOUS, stated — no new eye-read is made for the EG).

| W-S17a | p3 upright = the layout's rotation; ≥ 1 GREEN H and ≥ 1 GREEN V chain on p3 (else VACUOUS, stop) | the EG unreadable / read sideways |

### §43.15 SPEC — DIMENSIONS FROM THE READ CHAINS; READ VALUES USED WHERE THE SCAN MEASURES THEM (2026-11-23, 'resume 2D' next item 3; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue (measured, `gate` QF rows):** the importer's qualifier (`dxf2qualify.js` l.339-361, `tol.dim` 0.5 mm) wants a DIMENSION per plan side (overall + every wall end/opening point) that equals the geometry to 0.5 mm. Today the plan carries no DIMENSION, and its walls sit at pixel-measured positions (envelope 12.486 x 11.985) while the drawing's READ chains say 12.49 x 11.99 — so even a correct dimension would mismatch.
**Rule (the template rule of §33: READ = measured within tolerance, the drawn value used):** for each GREEN chain, its tick positions in mm (px / scale) are paired with the cumulative sums of its READ segment values. A wall coordinate on that chain's axis within 1 px (1000 / K mm) of a tick is set to the READ cumulative value; count and max shift logged (`§RASTER_EMIT_SNAP`). The grid marks (@GRIDX/@GRIDY) become those READ positions. Nothing farther than 1 px moves.
**Dimensions:** each GREEN chain is written as the drawing has it, on the side it is drawn (H chain above the plan = maxY, V chain right of it = maxX, from `line_y`/`line_x` vs the walls): one DIMENSION per segment + one overall, layer `A-ANNO-DIMS`, text = the READ value in mm (pattern: `to_std.py` `dim()`). Sides with no GREEN chain get NONE (their YELLOW chains are not stated; those QF rows stay, a true finding about the read).
**Elevations:** a level line at the plan level (z = 0, the stated DEFAULT) beside the two already drawn (H, ridge) — the qualifier's `elev_level_mismatch` asks every stated datum to be drawn; height DIMENSIONs are NOT emitted (every height is a DEFAULT; `elev_height_dim_missing` stays as the honest row).
**Witness W-S18:** (a) snap: every moved coordinate ≤ 1 px, count printed; envelope after = 12490 x 11990 exactly; (b) round trip W-S12a incl. DIMENSION counts; (c) importer QF before/after by code: `dim_missing` and `dim_point_missing` drop ONLY on maxY/maxX, `elev_level_mismatch` → 0, no NEW code appears (no `dim_text_mismatch`); (d) IfcWall count unchanged (76), DB bbox = 12.49 x 11.99.
**§43.14 RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_layout.py upright`, `raster_probe.py` unchanged; logs `layout_upright_p3.log`, `raster_probe_p3.log` in the session scratchpad): STOPPED at W-S17a, VACUOUS — the EG sheet is printed too small for the reader. ⛔ one owner question below.**
- **Orientation PASS:** `§LAYOUT_UPRIGHT page=p3 rot=270 kind=plan verdict=WRITTEN` (score 0.67, the §43.13 match); the probe's deskew reads +0.22° as on p2/p4 (same scan run).
- **W-S17a VACUOUS:** 31 chains, 15 judged, **0 GREEN** (`§RASTER_W-S4 ... green=0`), so W-S5 INCONCLUSIVE and the spec's stop rule applies; walls/wallscan not run (they need GREEN chains).
- **Cause, measured (not a reader defect):** `§RASTER_GLYPH_H main_h=10` on p3 vs **13** on p4. The EG sheet is printed at a smaller reduction: the one segment read alone, `6,77⁵` over 339 px, gives ≈ 50 px/m against p4's 72.24 (one unchecked read, NOT a scale: ≈ 0.69 x p4, about 1:158 on paper). Its dimension rows also sit ~35 px apart, and the tick finder mixes neighbouring rows (`ticks_x` lists cross rows). §43 step 0 measured 13 px as the edge of what Tesseract reads.
- **Seen by the developer, a diagnostic and not evidence:** the EG's top chains show the same house overall 12.49 plus an attached extension 6.77⁵ to the west, with a 2 cm joint. So once the EG is read, W-S17c would most likely be a FINDING (the EG footprint ≠ the OG footprint), not a pass.
- **⛔ BLOCKED, one question for the owner:** the EG sheet is printed about 30 % smaller than the OG sheet and its numbers can't be read at 200 dpi. Recommended: (b) let the EG take its scale from the OG sheet, by registering p3's tick positions to p4's READ chains (a pixel fit with a residual witness, digits never chosen); then the house part is READ through p4 and the extension is MEASURED only. The alternative is (a): a ≥ 300 dpi rescan of the EG sheet. Which one?

**§43.15 RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_emit.py`, logs `raster_emit_p4.log`, `gate_p4.log`, `lib_probe_p4.log`, `ifc2db.log`): W-S18 PASS — importer blocking 69 → 59, no new row code, the model is now the drawing's 12.49 x 11.99 exactly.**
- **Regression first:** before the change, the emitter run from this worktree reproduced the previous session's six sheets byte-identically and its `§` log line for line (baseline `gate`: 69 blocking).
- **W-S18a snap:** `§RASTER_EMIT_TICKS` worst tick vs its READ position = 17.2 mm (H) / 16.7 mm (V), 1 px = 13.8 mm; `§RASTER_EMIT_SNAP moved=55`, every moved coordinate was ≤ 1 px from a measured tick; the largest move to the READ value was 29.4 mm (tick offset + coordinate offset, ~2 px; stated). The envelope is now 0,0..12490,11990.
- **A bug the witness caught on the first run:** `max_shift_mm=318.1`. The V chain is read rotated, so its segments run in `ticks_rot` order, which is `ticks_y` REVERSED (sum constant). The pairing now detects the order from the two tick lists (`§RASTER_EMIT_SNAP_ORDER chain=V ... reversed`) and asserts every tick within 2 px of its READ position, so a wrong pairing fails loudly instead of moving walls.
- **W-S18b:** plan `A-ANNO-DIMS` 16 written = 16 read back (7 + overall per chain; H chain on maxY, V chain on maxX, both from `line_y`/`line_x`); elevations 3 level lines each; every sheet PASS.
- **W-S18c (importer, by code):** `QF:dim_missing` 4 → 2 (maxY + maxX overall present; minY/minX have no GREEN chain), `QF:dim_point_missing` 32 → 28 (the end points on maxY/maxX; window jambs are on the YELLOW window chains, not stated), `QF:elev_level_mismatch` 4 → 0; no `dim_text_mismatch`, no new code. Unchanged and honest: `elev_height_dim_missing` 12 (every height is a DEFAULT), `grid_missing` 4 (the drawing has no grid; its chain ticks sit on wall faces, not axes), 12 window rows, `slab_no_underside`.
- **W-S18d:** IFC 76 IfcWall + 2 IfcRoof (42,297 bytes); DB 78 elements, world bbox x 0..12.490, y -11.990..0, z 0..5.422 (ridge DERIVED from the READ depth 11.99, was 5.421 on 11.985).
- **Item 2 (window @SCHEDULE) ⛔ BLOCKED, one question:** the importer binds a window only to a library family within 5 mm (`dxf2shell_config.js` `FILL.size_tol` 0.005) and the library has no 1.4-1.5 m window. The measured gaps (N 1453/1495/1467, S 1426/955/1398, E 1479/1465/1465, W 940/899/940 mm) are 50-110 mm SHORT of the drawn labels (1.51 / 1.01), so authoring families at the measured sizes would invent sizes. The honest route needs the W/H labels READ first (W-S8, not done), then window families added to the PUBLIC library by migration, which is outward-facing (§34 needed the owner's go for TB-LKTN). Question: may this client's window sizes, once READ, go into the public library as new families?

**§43.13 follow-up (item 4, more layout anchors), MEASURED 2026-11-23, no code change:** local Tesseract (psm 11) on p2, p3 (at 270°), p4 and p1. The words read at conf ≥ 60 on BOTH plan sheets are WERKPLAN (already an anchor) plus four project-specific words (client name, street, town: private, and not a property of the type, so they never go into the public `german_werkplan_layout.json`). Generic title-block words searched at ANY confidence (MASSSTAB, DATUM, BAUHERR, BAUVORHABEN, PLANNUMMER, GEZEICHNET, INDEX, PROJEKT, NORDEN, ARCHITEKT, …): p2 LEGENDE 72 / WERKPLAN 91; p3 WERKPLAN 91; p4 LEGENDE 92 / WERKPLAN 93; p1 DATUM 62. **So no further WORD anchor is readable at 200 dpi.** The next anchor has to be geometric: the title-block box (a closed table rectangle in the frame's lower right) and the north arrow. That is a new method, spec first, and it is not done. The margin stays as §43.13 stated (a views sheet scores 0.67 as a plan).
**Item 5 (JS port into bim-ootb `modeller/pdf2dxf/`) not started, on purpose:** items 1 and 2 are waiting on owner answers that change the method (scale from another sheet; window families). Porting a chain that is still changing doubles the work.

### OWNER RULING 2026-11-23 (answers to §43.14 / §43.15): *"Agree with your 1(b), 2, openings and walls have to follow common German standard defaults, if the scans are unclear."*
- (1) EG: scale from the OG sheet by registering p3's ticks to p4's READ chains (§43.17).
- (2) READ window sizes may go into the public library as new families.
- **German standard defaults when the scan is unclear.** The SOURCE, so the code never relies on a recalled value: DIN 4172 *Maßordnung im Hochbau*, module 12.5 cm, with a 1 cm joint. Nominal sizes from the table on BauNetz Wissen, *Steinformat und Maßordnung* (https://www.baunetzwissen.de/mauerwerk/fachwissen/planungsgrundlagen/steinformat-und-massordnung-162694, table image `mw_masse_2`): **Außenmaß x·12.5 − 1 cm, Öffnungsmaß x·12.5 + 1 cm, Vorsprungsmaß x·12.5 cm.** The same page says plan elements (Planziegel) follow their own system, not DIN 4172. The 17⁵ READ on this drawing is not in the Außenmaß series, so a wall READ on the drawing always wins over the series. Single sash width: Dreh-Kipp windows usually 500-1250 mm wide (aroundhome.de, *Fenster Maße*, consumer source; used only for the sash count, stated). Window HEIGHT and sill: no norm value was found. They stay the registry DEFAULTs (`window.height` 1.2, `window.sill` 0.9; the sill lies inside the 0.80-1.00 m LBO band a forum source quotes, not relied on).

### §43.16 SPEC — OPENINGS MEASURED JAMB TO JAMB, DIN 4172 WHEN THE SCAN IS UNCLEAR, FAMILIES BY MIGRATION (item 2; spec BEFORE code)
**Issue (measured):** the exterior gaps (§43.1 runs) read 50-110 mm SHORT of the drawn labels. A diagnostic crop (not evidence) shows why: each jamb is a full-height stroke across the wall band, and the wall hatch (what the run tracer follows) stops short of it. The run end is not the opening edge.
**Measure (`raster_openings.py <work>`, writes `openings.json` + `§RASTER_OPEN_*`):** for each gap between two collinear exterior runs, search within ± 3 px × F of each run end, on the opening side, for the column (V walls: row) whose ink covers ≥ 90 % of the band's rows (the jamb stroke). Opening = between the two jamb stroke CENTRES (the face-line convention of W-S7a). A side with no stroke keeps the run end, logged `jamb=none`.
**Classify every width against the Öffnungsmaß series x·125 + 10 mm:**
- within 1 px (1000/K mm) of a series value → **STANDARD**: the measurement agrees, nominal used;
- otherwise → **STANDARD-DEFAULT**: the nearest series value, amber, measured kept in the log. This is the owner's rule for an unclear scan.
- **a TIE** (measured within 1 px of the MIDPOINT of two series values: the nearest value is a coin flip) → the one of the two that this sheet CONFIRMED as STANDARD (`STANDARD-TIE-SHEET`), else the nearest, flagged `STANDARD-TIE-UNRESOLVED`. Same idea as the wall-thickness vocabulary (§43.2): the drawing's own confirmed values first. Added after the first run: the south opening measured 1446.5 mm against a midpoint of 1447.5, and a diagnostic crop confirms the jambs were found correctly. The scan itself draws it about 3 px short.
A READ label would outrank all of these (W-S8). The labels are not read yet, so none is used. Search window: −2..+6 px from the run end toward the opening, because the hatch stops 2-4 px short of the jamb.
**Heights:** registry DEFAULT 1.2 m, sill 0.9 m (above). **Sashes:** ceil(width / 1250). **Depth:** the wall's READ thickness, so the family fills the plan's opening box (the §34 convention: depth from the plan).
**Families:** `gen_werkplan_window_families.py openings.json > migration/CL009_werkplan_window_families.sql`. Same mesh method and conventions as `gen_window_families.py` (§34, `family_mesh.py`), one X-running and one Y-running mesh per size, `provenance='PARAMETRIC'`. The source names DIN 4172 and the registry; no client name, only sizes. The migration is applied to a COPY of the library DB (as `run_p1.sh` does), then the local export is made. Publishing the live library is NOT part of this step: the page does not take scans yet (item 5).
**Witness W-S19:** (a) per opening: jamb found yes/no, measured width, class, nominal, |Δ| in mm; VACUOUS if 0 openings; (b) a falsifier: the same stroke test run at mid-wall positions (no opening) finds no jamb in ≥ 95 %; (c) importer: `GAP:fill_unbound` 12 → 0, and NO `fill_size_mismatch` / `lod400_*` row appears; (d) IFC: IfcWindow count = openings, each window's width = its nominal ± 1 mm, inside its wall gap; (e) W-W3-style: every existing family's `familyToRef` is unchanged with CL009 applied.
**§43.16 RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_openings.py`, `raster_emit.py`, `gen_werkplan_window_families.py` → `migration/CL009_werkplan_window_families.sql`, `types/german_werkplan_efh.json`; logs `raster_openings_p4.log`, `raster_emit_p4.log`, `lib_migrate.log`, `witness_s19e.log`, `lib_probe_p4b.log`, `witness_s19d.log`): W-S19 PASS, the windows are in. Importer blocking 59 → 47; IFC 76 IfcWall + 12 IfcWindow + 2 IfcRoof; DB 90 elements.**
- **Refactor first, regression:** the body frame (origin = the anchoring GREEN chains' outer ticks) now has ONE owner, `raster_body.py`, imported by `raster_walls.py` and `raster_openings.py`. Re-run: `raster_walls` log and `walls.json` byte-identical; `raster_wallscan` log and `walls_all.json` byte-identical. Without `openings.json` the emitter writes the §43.15 sheets byte-identically.
- **W-S19a (12 openings, both jambs found on 9; one jamb missing on 3, those keep the run end):** jamb-to-jamb vs DIN Öffnungsmaß: 3 **STANDARD** (E 1514 / 1500 / 1514 → 1510, |Δ| ≤ 10 mm); 7 **STANDARD-DEFAULT** (Δ 15-49 mm, nearest series value: N 1510 x 3, S 1510 / 1010, W 885 / 1010); 1 **TIE-SHEET** (S 1446.5, 1 mm from the 1385/1510 midpoint → 1510, the size this sheet confirmed); 1 **TIE-UNRESOLVED** (W 960.7 → 1010, nearest; no ~1 m size is confirmed on this sheet). Jamb to jamb is 20-55 mm closer to the series than the old run gaps.
- **W-S19b falsifier PASS:** the stroke test at 16 mid-wall positions found 0 jambs.
- **Doubt, stated, not hidden:** W 926 mm → 885 is STANDARD-DEFAULT (1.6 px from the midpoint, outside the tie band). My diagnostic reading of the drawing suggests 1.01 there. Only the W-S8 label read can settle it; the class is amber in `openings.json`.
- **W-S19e PASS:** CL009 applied to a COPY of `library/component_library.db` (idempotent: 3 families after one and after two applies, 6 meshes). The importer's own `familyToRef` gives the same result for all 68 existing families before and after.
