### §43.17 SPEC — THE EG SHEET REGISTERED TO THE OG SHEET (owner ruling 2026-11-23, item 1(b); spec BEFORE code)
**Issue (§43.14):** p3 (EG) is printed ~0.69 x p4's size; its digits (10 px) are below the reader, 0 GREEN chains. Its TICKS are still measured: tick positions are geometry and need no OCR.
**Step A — registration (`raster_register.py <p3 work> <p4 work>`, writes `<p3 work>/register.json`, `§RASTER_REG_*`):** a dimension chain drawn on both sheets has the same tick PATTERN at two print sizes: p3_px = s · p4_px + t, one s and t per axis. For every p4 chain with ≥ 6 ticks (GREEN or YELLOW: only its tick px are used) and every p3 dimension line (ticks pooled per `line_y`/`line_x` ± 3 px), candidate (s, t) come from mapping the p4 chain's first and last tick onto two p3 ticks, with s in [0.4, 1.0]. A candidate is kept when EVERY tick of the p4 chain lands within 1 px of a p3 tick. Per axis the scale is the median s of the kept matches, and the matches must agree within 0.5 % (else INCONCLUSIVE). **Nothing is read from p3's digits.**
**Revised after the first run (measured, `raster_register_p3.log` run 1):** per-chain full matching failed. H found 0 matches: p3's tick finder misses ticks at this print size (row 2 holds 653, 847, 855, 1024, 1031, 1258 and lacks the ticks near 672 and 1275). V matched only periodic tread fields (29 evenly spaced ticks, s 0.44 and 0.69, falsifier 3), and a periodic pattern matches at many scales. **Rule now: CONSENSUS per axis.** Both sheets show the same exterior, so one (s, t) maps EVERY common tick. Candidates (s, t) come from pairs of p4 ticks onto pairs of p3 ticks (s in [0.4, 1.0]); a candidate's score = the number of p4 ticks (all chains of that axis) landing within 1 px of any p3 tick. The best is refined by least squares on its inliers. Chains with near-uniform spacing (≥ 10 ticks, segment-length CV < 0.1) are tread or hatch fields: logged, excluded. **Falsifier:** the same search with the p4 ticks MIRRORED must score clearly lower (best mirrored ≤ half the true best).
**Revised again (run 2, measured):** whole-sheet consensus FAILS its own falsifier. H: s 0.421 with 31 inliers, mirrored also 31; V: s 0.683, 26 inliers, mirrored 24. With 64-121 ticks per axis, ±1 px coincidences are common (≈ 13 % hit rate), and legend/table lines join the pool. **Rule now: ONE p4 chain onto ONE p3 line.** The pool is the ticks of one p3 dimension line. A (chain, line) pair matches when ≥ 6 ticks AND ≥ 2/3 of the chain's ticks land within 1 px under one (s, t), refined by least squares. On one line the chance hit rate is ~1.5 % per tick, so 6 coincidences are practically impossible, while the 1-2 ticks p3's finder misses are tolerated. Per axis, the accepted matches must agree on s within 0.5 %. **Falsifier:** each p4 chain MIRRORED (segment order reversed) must reach no accepted match.
**Run 3 (measured, tolerance sweep, logs `reg_tol1.5.log` / `reg_tol2.0.log`):** at 1 and 1.5 px nothing matches. At **2 px**, p4's top house chain (line 489, 8 ticks) matches TWO separate p3 lines, the EG's top row (709) and its bottom row (1769), 6/8 ticks each: s 0.68986 / 0.68835 (spread 0.22 %), rms 0.77 / 0.75 px; mirrored 0. The tolerance is therefore 2 px, stated with its evidence: p3's ticks are thinner at 0.69 x print and their centres sit ~1-2 px off. The chance argument still holds: ~4 % per tick on one line, so 6 of 8 by coincidence is ~1e-3 over all candidates, and the mirrored falsifier stays the guard. V finds no chain: the EG's interior walls differ from the OG's, so its V chains share only their exterior ticks.
**Two-stage rule for V (one print: p4 measured KH/KV equal within 0.14 %, W-S5):** s_V := s_H; then ONLY the offset t_V is searched (1-D): a (p4 chain, p3 line) pair matches when ≥ 5 ticks land within 2 px. Every accepted V match must agree on t_V within 2 px; the mirrored falsifier as before. As a check, s_V is also fitted freely on the accepted V matches' inliers and reported against s_H; a disagreement above 0.5 % is INCONCLUSIVE.
**Run 4 (measured):** the two-stage V search at ≥ 5 ticks is VACUOUS. A fixed-scale diagnostic shows the best V match is p4's 29-tick field onto p3's 29-tick field, 29/29 at s = s_H: both are the LEGEND table's row lines, printed on both sheets at the same reduction. That CONFIRMS the scale independently of H (logged as the V scale witness), but it says nothing about where the house sits. **V offset rule (exterior pattern):** the anchoring GREEN V chain's exterior ticks (both faces of the first and last wall: the ring, the part the two storeys share, as the H match already shows for 12.49) are mapped with s_H. A p3 V line SUPPORTS an offset t_V when ≥ 3 of the 4 land within 2 px, including both ends. t_V is accepted when ≥ 2 separate p3 lines support it and agree within 2 px.
**Witness W-S17r:** (a) per axis ≥ 1 full-tick match (≥ 6 ticks) with max residual ≤ 1 px, else VACUOUS; (b) s_H vs s_V agree within 0.5 % (one print, KH = KV); (c) p3 scale = p4 READ scale × s; printed as px/m and paper scale; (d) the single unchecked OCR read on p3 (`6,77⁵` over 339 px, §43.14) is compared with the registered scale and reported. It is a cross-check only, never an input. (e) A falsifier: the same search against p4 chains SHIFTED by half a segment finds no full-tick match.
**Step B (after A passes; spec appended then):** the p3 lines matched to p4 GREEN chains inherit those READ values, because the same chain is proven by geometry. p3 then runs walls → wallscan → emit as a second storey under the OG, as §43.14 laid out (W-S17c-e).
**§43.17 step A RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_register.py`, final log `raster_register_p3.log`, sweep logs `reg_tol1.5.log` / `reg_tol2.0.log`): SCALE REGISTERED, VERTICAL POSITION NOT YET. Step B not started.**
- **H PASS:** p4's top house chain (8 ticks) matches the EG's top row AND its bottom row, 6/8 ticks each within 2 px, s 0.68986 / 0.68835 (spread 0.22 %), rms 0.76 px, mirrored 0. **s = 0.68910 → p3 = 49.78 px/m, paper ≈ 1:158** (p4: 72.24 px/m, 1:109).
- **Scale confirmed independently on V:** the legend table's 29 row lines (same table on both sheets) free-fit s = 0.68591, 0.46 % from s_H (inside 0.5 %). A periodic field fixes scale only, not position.
- **Cross-check (not an input):** p3's single unchecked read `6,77⁵` spans 339 px = 6.810 m at the registered scale, 35 mm (1.7 px) from the read value.
- **V offset VACUOUS by the rule:** one p3 line (1623) supports t_V = 415.0 with 3/4 exterior ticks (the top wall's two faces + the bottom outer face); the rule asks for 2 agreeing lines. Not relaxed: run 1-3 already moved the method once per measured failure, and a fourth loosening to get a pass would be tuning, not evidence.
- **Next (spec, then code):** the V offset from the WALLS, not the chains. At the registered scale the exterior wall is 0.365 m = 18.2 px. Search p3 for the two horizontal exterior wall bands (face pair 18 px apart + diagonal hatch, the `raster_texture.py` rule) spanning the registered house x-range (p3 x 653..1274). The top band gives t_V. The bottom band is then a MEASURED depth to compare with 11.99 (W-S17c), independent of the chain line's single support. If both agree with t_V 415 ± 2 px, step B (walls → wallscan → emit EG as Level 1 under the OG) can run.

### §43.18 SPEC — THE EG'S VERTICAL POSITION FROM ITS EXTERIOR WALL BANDS (owner: *"Proceed"*, 2026-11-23; spec BEFORE code)
**Input:** `register.json` scale (s 0.6891, p3 49.78 px/m) and the H map (t_H = median of the two H matches); p3 `page_clean.png`. The exterior wall thickness is the READ 36⁵ (p4 GREEN chains), which is T = 0.365 x 49.78 = 18.2 px on p3.
**Search (`raster_register.py`, stage C; one texture owner, `raster_texture.py`):** across the registered house x-range on p3, for every candidate top row a of a horizontal band [a, a + T], each column is WALL when both faces show ink (± 1 px) AND the band interior is diagonal hatch (diagonal > straight, the §43.1 rule). Score(a) = the share of WALL columns. The search runs over the WHOLE page height, so no prior position (the one supporting line, t_V 415) is assumed. Peaks are bands whose score is ≥ 3 x the page median score.
**Witness W-S17v:** (a) the two strongest peaks are the top and bottom exterior walls, their order and gap logged; t_V = top outer face − s · 751 (p4's Y0); (b) the measured depth (bottom outer face − top outer face) / K3 compared with 11.99, Δ in mm and px, reported, never forced (W-S17c); (c) the same test on VERTICAL bands at the registered x of the two exterior walls (X0_3, X1_3) must score as walls, a check of the H map (W-S17d); (d) agreement with the single chain support: |t_V − 415.0| reported; (e) falsifier: band starts shifted half a room into the interior score below the peak threshold.
**Then step B** (spec'd after (a)-(e) pass): the EG walls at this frame, its storey under the OG.
**§43.18 step B SPEC — EG walls through the registered frame (spec BEFORE code):** `raster_regwall.py` also writes a separate work dir `<p3>_reg/` whose `chains.json` holds p4's GREEN chains MAPPED onto p3: ticks and segment px through (s, t_H) / (s, t_V); V segment px kept in the probe's rotated convention (rot = page height − 1 − y); `scale_px_per_m` = K3; `clean`/`otsu`/`dpi` from the p3 probe. Each chain is tagged `registered_from=p4`. The values are READ on the OG sheet. The EG pixels then TEST each band: `raster_walls.py` → `raster_wallscan.py` → `raster_openings.py` run UNCHANGED on `<p3>_reg/`. A band the EG does not have finds no wall rows, the W-S7a/W-S7d logic, and that is expected where the two storeys differ. **Scope stated:** only the registered body (the house 12.49 x 11.99) is traced. The attached EXTENSION west of the house lies outside every READ chain (its one read, 6,77⁵, is unchecked). It is a named finding and is not built.
**Emit (W-S17e):** `raster_emit.py <og work> <roof.json> <out> <eg work>` writes `plan_eg.dxf` (@LEVEL=0) and `plan_og.dxf` (@LEVEL=3.0) through ONE plan writer; each elevation draws the EG band 0..2.75 and the OG band 3.0..6.0 with each storey's openings; roof bottom 6.0. All heights are DEFAULT (§43.14 values). Without the 4th argument the output is byte-identical to today.
**Witness:** W-S7a/b/d on the EG (the ring closes at 12.49 x 11.99, falsifier 0 %); W-S9a recall; W-S19 on the EG openings; importer: 2 storeys, `slab_no_underside` gone, IfcSlab between them, IfcWall = EG + OG emitted walls.
**§43.18 RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_regwall.py`, `raster_walls.py` / `raster_wallscan.py` / `raster_openings.py` unchanged on `<p3>_reg/`, `raster_emit.py` two-storey, `migration/CL010_werkplan_eg_window_families.sql`; logs `raster_regwall_p3.log`, `raster_walls_p3.log`, `raster_wallscan_p3.log`, `raster_openings_p3.log`, `raster_emit_2st.log`, `gate_2st.log`, `lib_probe_2st.log`, `ifc2db_2st.log`, `witness_s17e.log`): THE HOUSE HAS TWO STOREYS. IFC 119 IfcWall + 20 IfcWindow + 1 IfcSlab + 2 IfcRoof; DB 142 elements on Level 1 (z 0) and Level 2 (z 3.0).**
- **W-S17v PASS (vertical position from the walls):** the whole-page band search finds the top exterior wall at p3 row 932 (score 0.57) and the bottom at 1510 (0.32), with threshold 0.159 = 3 x the page median. t_V = 414.5, **0.5 px from the single chain support (415.0)**. Measured depth outer to outer: 596 px = **11.972 m vs the OG's READ 11.99 (−18 mm, −0.9 px)**. ### §43.19 SPEC — THE EG TERRACE CORNER: POST + GLAZED WALLS (spec BEFORE code)
**Issue (§43.18):** the EG's south-east corner is a covered terrace. The exterior ring stops (E wall ends at y 9019 mm, S wall at x 10024 mm), and the corner is closed by two glazed elements and a hatched post. A diagnostic crop (not evidence) shows sliding arrows.
**Rule (`raster_notch.py <work> [<type template>]`, writes `notch.json`):** a NOTCH exists when the last exterior run on a side stops > 1 m short of the corner, on BOTH adjacent sides (logged per corner; all 4 corners tested). Inside a notch:
- glazing H = rows within 1 m inside the E wall's end where ink covers ≥ 70 % of the span from the S wall's end to the E wall. The contiguous cluster nearest the wall end is the band, its thickness measured. Glazing V likewise.
- post = the connected diagonal-hatch region (`raster_texture.py`) around the two bands' crossing; its bbox is measured, then sized to the type's wall series (DIN 4172 Außenmaß x·12.5 − 1 cm): STANDARD within 1 px, else STANDARD-DEFAULT.
**Emit:** the glazed bands as `A-WALL-CURT` (the STD curtain-wall layer: IfcCurtainWall, §D45) from the post to the walls; the post as `A-COLS` + `@BODYZ=column,<level>,<level + wall_h>` (§L36 route, as TB-LKTN's columns). The sliding doors inside the glazing are not separated: stated.
**Revised after run 1 (measured, `raster_notch_p3.log`):** (i) NE was also flagged: both walls stop ~2.3 m short there because windows sit near that corner. A notch now also needs the ring line beyond both wall ends to be EMPTY: no row of the exterior band covered ≥ 70 % there. Glazing on the ring line = a window corner, not a notch. (ii) The post measured 864 x 804 with its centre 130 mm off the crossing, because the 2 px dilation joined neighbouring strokes. The post is now the region where the 7 x 7 diagonal-texture DENSITY is ≥ half the window's maximum, the component nearest the crossing. (iii) The V glazing showed 1 line: sliding panels are drawn offset, each covering ~half the span. A band is now the contiguous rows ≥ 45 % that contain at least one row ≥ 70 %.
**Run 2 (measured):** SE then failed the emptiness test on its east ring line, because the OG's outline above is drawn there DASHED (a dashed line covers ~50-70 %). Emptiness now counts only SOLID lines: a row whose longest continuous ink run is ≥ 30 % of the span (glazing is long runs between frames; a dash is a few px).
**Run 3 (measured, per-column dump):** the SE east strip is empty (≤ 2 % ink) except columns 1274-1275: one solid line on the band's OUTER edge, the outline of the storey above, solid at this print. Window glazing lies INSIDE the band. So the emptiness test ignores the outer 3 px of the band.
**Run 4 (measured, density sweep 0.5/0.65/0.8/0.9 of the max):** the hatch-region post size is UNSTABLE (583x763, 462x723, 0x20, 382x181 mm). That method does not isolate the post, and no threshold is picked to make it look right. **Post rule now:** the glazing lines END at the post's faces and run through its middle (the drawing's own construction). East face = the median start of the H glazing's lines that run to the E wall; south face = the median start of the V glazing's lines that run to the S wall; centre = the bands' crossing. Size = 2 x (face − centre), measured TWICE (along x from the H glazing, along y from the V glazing). The two must agree within 1 px (GREEN, then the wall series), else INCONCLUSIVE and the post is NOT built.
**Run 5 (measured):** from the glazing ends, the size along x = 3756 mm and along y = 5163 mm, disagreeing. The glazing lines run into the post's hatch as continuous ink, so a line's end is not separable from the hatch at this print. **Stop rule applied: the post is NOT built** (five methods, each logged). The two glazed runs ARE measured (bands of 4 and 5 lines, 121 and 141 mm), so they are built as curtain walls meeting at their measured crossing, which closes the ring. The missing post is a stated gap.
**Witness W-S20:** (a) notch corners found / tested; (b) per glazing band: coverage, thickness, span; (c) post size, class; (d) falsifier: the 3 corners without a notch produce no band; (e) importer: IfcCurtainWall 2 + IfcColumn 1 on Level 1, the EG ring closed (no new GAP).
**§43.19 RESULT (2026-11-23; `raster_notch.py`, `raster_emit.py`; logs `raster_notch_p3.log`, `raster_notch_p4.log`, `raster_emit_4.log`, `gate_4.log`, `lib_probe_4.log`, `witness_s20e.log`): the terrace corner is closed by its two glazed runs. The post is NOT built (stop rule).**
- **W-S20a/d:** 4 corners tested per plan. EG: SE is a notch (S wall 2466 mm short, E wall 2971 mm short, the ring line beyond both EMPTY); NE is not (its walls stop short too, but window glazing lies inside the band). The OG sheet (falsifier) has 0 notches.
- **W-S20b:** glazing H = 4 lines, cover 0.88, band 121 mm, x 9763..12125 at y 8798..8919; glazing V = 5 lines, cover 0.65, band 141 mm, y 8919..11625. Both are built as `A-WALL-CURT` → **2 IfcCurtainWall on Level 1, z 0..2.75** (wall top = slab underside, the registry rule `wall.top_when_unseen`). The sliding doors inside them are not separated (stated).
- **W-S20c post: NOT BUILT.** Five methods, each logged (spec runs 1-5); none measures the post stably at 0.69 x print. It stays a named gap. Without a post, the V run starts where the H run ends (no overlapping footprints).

### §43.20 SPEC — HEIGHTS FROM SCHNITT A-A, CORRELATED WITH THE PLANS (owner 2026-11-23: door/level dimensions on page 2, *"if corelated with the floor plans"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Measured first:** the section's level marks (+2,86, +6,04, +8,46⁵ …) are NOT machine-readable: OCR at 3x returns 2 of ~15, conf 39/85, the same limit as the elevations (§43.11). So the heights come from the section's GEOMETRY, with two independent checks.

The measured EG FFL → OG FFL distance must equal it within 2 px. Nothing is emitted until (2) and (4) pass.

- **Rule:** a SLAB = two neighbouring line groups (run ≥ 25 % of the span) with every row strictly between them at hatch density ≥ 0.30. The plans give the storey count N (READ: EG + OG, N = 2), so the top N + 1 slabs top-down are: the top storey's CEILING, the floor slabs of storeys N..2, and the BASE slab of storey 1; any hatched band below the base slab is logged as a sub-base layer and not used. Fewer than N + 1 slabs = VACUOUS (heights stay DEFAULT). The ROOF BOTTOM = the strongest roof line where it meets the faces (`§SECT_ROOF_Z`, mean of the two faces).
- **Heights (datum = the base slab's TOP = EG level 0; only differences, the absolute datum is not needed):** OG level = base top − OG slab top; slab thickness = OG slab bottom − top; EG walls = level 0 .. OG slab bottom; OG walls = OG level .. roof bottom; each in mm at k (1 px = 20 mm, rounded to 5 mm, the drawing's notation unit). Written to `section_heights.json`, each value with its rows.
- **Emitter:** `raster_emit.py` takes `section_heights.json` as an optional 5th argument; given, it replaces `wall.height`, `synth.slab.thickness` and ROOF0 with these READ-by-geometry values and logs them as `READ-GEOM`; absent, the DEFAULTs stand as before (the vector chain and a single-storey scan are untouched). The ridge stays DERIVED from the READ pitch over the envelope depth; the measured apex is logged beside it as a check (`|derived − measured| ≤ 2 px` = 40 mm, else FINDING).

### §43.21 SPEC — DOORS ON THE SCAN: WALL GAP + SWING ARC, SIZED TO DIN 18101 (owner 2026-11-23: *"Proceed"*, the next item after heights; spec BEFORE code)
**Source cited first (read 2026-11-23, BauNetz Wissen):** "Wandöffnungen und Türgrößen nach DIN 18101" (https://www.baunetzwissen.de/fenster-und-tueren/fachwissen/konstruktion-funktion/wandoeffnungen-und-tuergroessen-nach-din-18101-155227): Nennmaß = Baurichtmaß + 10 mm (width), + 5 mm (height); Vorzugsgrößen Baurichtmaß 625 / 750 / 875 / 1000 / 1125 x 2000 (Nennmaß 635 / 760 / 885 / 1010 / 1135 x 2005); "200 cm … kann … gegen die nächstgrößere Höhe von 212,5 cm ausgetauscht werden". "Türblattgrößen nach DIN 18101" (…/tuerblattgroessen-nach-din-18101-155263): one leaf Nennmaß 635 / 760 / 885 / 1010 x **2010**, 1135 x 2130; two leaves 1260 / 1510 / 1760 / 2010 x 2010, 1260 x 2130. **The two pages disagree on the height Nennmaß (2005 vs 2010): recorded, not resolved.** The widths are the DIN 4172 series the windows already use (x·125 + 10), so the type template gains a `door_series` with these lists and the source; no new width rule.
**Measured before writing (OG `walls_all.json`, collinear runs of one band with no run between):** interior gaps hatch-end to hatch-end 857, 872 (17⁵, H), 857, 857, 843 (17⁵, V), 843 (11⁵, V); EG 884, 864 (17⁵, V). All near 885. A diagnostic look at the plan (not evidence) shows a quarter-circle swing arc at each, and arcs at some EXTERIOR gaps (French doors, the EG entrance) but none at the windows.
**Rule (`raster_doors.py <plan work> [template]`, writes `doors.json`):**
1. Candidates: (a) every exterior opening in `openings.json` (§43.16); (b) every gap > 300 mm between two collinear interior runs of `walls_all.json` (same axis, band edges within 1 px, the nearest neighbour run).
2. Width (interior): jamb to jamb with the §43.16 jamb stroke rule (one owner: import the function, do not copy it), classed against the template's `door_series` widths: STANDARD within 1 px, else STANDARD-DEFAULT (nearest), as §43.16.
3. **Swing arc (the door witness):** for each of the 2 jambs as hinge × the 2 sides of the wall, the hinge = the jamb centre on that side's face; for radius r from 0.80 to 1.05 × width (1 px steps), sample the quarter circle at angles 10°..80° from the wall line (1° steps) and count samples on ink (g < 215, the lane's grey threshold, ± 1 px radially). Coverage = the best r's share. **DOOR** when the best of the 4 placements has coverage ≥ 0.60; it gives the hinge jamb, the swing side and the drawn radius. Otherwise **OPENING** (a passage or a window), not a door.
4. Exterior openings: an arc ≥ 0.60 reclassifies the window as a DOOR (entrance / French door); its width class stays the §43.16 one.
**Witnesses:** W-S21a (counts, classes per storey, both jambs found); W-S21b falsifier: the arc test at a SOLID mid-wall point (each interior run ≥ 1.0 m, the gap set to 885) must find no arc (rate ≤ 5 %, else FAIL); W-S21c every exterior opening that stays a window has coverage < 0.60 (logged per opening); W-S21d VACUOUS when no candidate at all. Log `raster_doors_p4.log`, `raster_doors_p3.log`.
**Not in this step (stated):** emission (A-DOOR arcs + `@SCHEDULE` door families by migration, the §34/§43.16 pattern) is step 2; the door HEIGHT is step 3 (section-measured, else DIN 2005/2010 above the finished floor, which needs the screed line named). Until then the model is unchanged.
**§43.21 run 1 RESULT (logs `raster_doors_p4.log` / `raster_doors_p3.log`, first rule): W-S21b FAIL on both sheets.** OG: 7 DOOR (6 interior 885, 1 exterior), the falsifier found an "arc" at 4 / 18 solid mid-wall points (max coverage 0.77); EG: 4 / 8 (max 0.89). Coverage counts ink anywhere on the circle, and a furnished, lettered plan puts ink on any circle. The first rule is retired.
**Measured on the way (diagnostic script, all three populations, both sheets):** (1) longest CONTIGUOUS arc run: OG doors 0.69-1.00, falsifiers ≤ 0.48, but the EG doors 0.17 / 0.20, because the EG prints its arcs DASHED (0.69 x reduction) and a dimension line crosses them, so contiguity is refused; (2) **radial CONTRAST** = coverage at the best r minus the mean coverage at r ± 4..6 px (a drawn arc is a thin circle; clutter is not): OG doors 0.82-1.00, EG doors 0.50 / 0.55, falsifiers max 0.36 (OG) / 0.30 (EG). A diagnostic look (not evidence) at the borderline exterior openings: EG W 6830 (contrast 0.44) is a T30 door to the boiler room, dashed arc; OG W 6006 / 10368 (0.31) are French doors whose arc hinges on the WINDOW FRAME inside the wall band, not on the face.
**Rule, revised (run 2):** (3') the hinge is tried at every across-band offset from the face inward to the far face (1 px steps), both jambs, both sides; score = radial contrast at the best r. (3'') **the threshold comes from the sheet's own null population:** DOOR when contrast ≥ (max falsifier contrast on that sheet) + 0.10. The falsifier uses the identical search (offsets included). W-S21b then reads: the threshold is ≤ 0.60 (a falsifier near 0.5 would mean no separation: INCONCLUSIVE, no doors emitted from that sheet), and the logged margin between the weakest DOOR and the strongest falsifier.
**§43.21 run 2 RESULT (logs `raster_doors_p4_r2.log`, `raster_doors_p3_r2.log`; `raster_jamb.py` now owns the jamb rule, `raster_openings.py` imports it: `openings.json` and its logs byte-identical on both storeys):**
- **OG (p4): W-S21b PASS, 8 DOORs.** 6 interior (17⁵ x 5, 11⁵ x 1), all jambs found, jamb to jamb 857-886 → DIN 18101 885 (1 STANDARD, 5 STANDARD-DEFAULT at −14..−28 mm = 1-2 px); contrast 0.84-0.99. 2 exterior reclassified window → DOOR: W 6006 (0.86) and W 10368 (1.00), hinge 124 / 138 mm inside the band (on the frame): the French doors. Null (18 solid mid-wall probes, same search) max 0.50 → threshold 0.60; **margin weakest door over null 0.33**. W-S21c: the 10 windows max 0.39. Not doors: the 4.61 m and 2.05 m gaps (the Luftraum edge / stair), the 1.38 m gap with one jamb.
- **EG (p3, 0.69 x print, dashed arcs): PASS by the rule but WEAK.** 1 DOOR (885 STANDARD, contrast 0.60) over a null max 0.49 (margin 0.105). The second interior door (0.52) and the T30 boiler-room door (0.47) are below the threshold: missed. The EG door recall is limited by the print (a 400 dpi rescan would answer it, as §43.6).

A `@TAG=door,<family_id>` at the swing's bbox centre; one `@SCHEDULE=<family_id>,door,<w>,<h>` per family on the sheet; `@HEADFRAME=0` (the schedule states the OPENING, the DIN Nennmaß, frame included). Interior runs whose end is within 1 px + 30 mm of a door's nominal edge, in the door's band, end AT that edge (the §43.16 window rule, logged `moved`). An exterior opening that `doors.json` calls a DOOR is written as the ARC, not as `A-GLAZ`, on the plan AND on its elevation.

### §43.22 SPEC — THE DOUBLE GARAGE (owner 2026-11-23: *"continue"*, the item after doors; spec BEFORE code; NOT built yet)
**What the drawing holds (diagnostic look, not evidence):** p3 (EG plan) draws a "DOPPELGARAGE" west of the house, against the house's west wall, in the house's southern part: two bays (STPL. 1 / 2), the garage door across its NORTH wall (a dashed X = the door's ceiling track), a window in its south wall, a T30 door into the house (the boiler room). p2 Schnitt B-B cuts it: a flat concrete roof (printed +2.43 underside, +2.65 top), floor −0.07, base slab to −0.38, clear 2.50.
**Measured (p3 at the registered scale K3 49.782 px/m, §43.17; diagonal-hatch density, the `raster_texture.py` rule, 5 px mean ≥ 0.25, runs ≥ 1.0 m, region x < the house's west face X0 653, y = the house ± 3 m):** H bands rows 1161-1178 (cols 375-654) and 1515-1531 (cols 313-654); V band cols 313-329 (rows 1161-1531); the house's west wall cols 639-654. **Footprint ≈ 6.9 m (E-W, to the house's west face) x 7.4 m (N-S), MEASURED only: no READ chain covers it** (its one OCR read, `6,77⁵`, is unchecked, §43.14).
**Two findings that block a naive trace:** (1) band widths from a smoothed density are inflated / eroded by up to 4 px: thickness must come from the FACE strokes (the §43.18 face-pair rule), then be classed to the template's `wall_series` (DIN 4172 x·125 − 10: 115 / 240 / 365); (2) the north band reads continuous over 5.6 m although the drawing shows the garage-door opening there: the dashed X of the door track is DIAGONAL ink and the hatch test counts it. The opening must be found by the §43.16 jamb rule (jamb strokes across the band) and by a straight-vs-diagonal split along the band (`band_scores`), not by hatch density alone.
**Steps, in order (each its own spec + witness before code):**
1. **Footprint (`raster_annex.py`, p3):** the garage ring west of X0: face pairs → thickness class; the ring closes on the house's west wall (shared or separate wall: measured, stated); openings: the north garage door (jamb to jamb, the DIN 4172 / door series), the south window, the T30 door (the §43.21 arc rule on this sheet is WEAK: stated). Witnesses: ring closes (W-S7b form), falsifier bands inside the garage score below threshold, dims MEASURED with ± 1 px = 20 mm.
2. **Heights (`raster_section.py` on SCHNITT B-B):** k from A-A (same sheet; the assumption stated) CHECKED by B-B's own face span against the step-1 footprint depth or width, within 1 px (the check also identifies the cut direction); then the step-3 slab rule (base slab, roof slab) → garage floor, wall top, roof slab top, relative to the EG level.
3. **Emission:** the garage as its own body on the EG plan (walls, openings, the garage door as a wide opening: the importer's door/rect rule to be read first), its flat roof as its own roof sheet. **Read the importer first** for: walls outside the storey envelope on elevations (`dxf2elev.js` faces), a second roof outline (today `GAP:roof_outline_mismatch` when the roof ≠ the top storey's envelope, `dxf2shell.js` l.366), a lower wall height on the same storey (`@WALLTOP` per-wall rule, `applyWallTops`). If the importer cannot take a second volume, that is a finding for the bim-ootb DXF2BIM lane (diagnose here, fix there), not a patch from this lane.

(2) k = A-A's `section_heights.json` k (same sheet; ASSUMED, stated) and CHECKED: span / k must equal one of step 1's outer sizes within 1 px; that size names the cut direction, and the other size must be off by > 5 px (unambiguous). It is ONE function `slab_pairs()` in `raster_texture.py`, called by `raster_section.py` (25 %, its A-A output must stay byte-identical) and by this script. Exactly 2 hatched pairs are required (roof slab above, base slab below), else VACUOUS. (4) Heights over the base slab top (the garage's own datum): wall clear = base slab top → roof slab bottom, roof slab thickness, base slab thickness, parapet top = the topmost line group touching both face columns above the roof slab. ### §44 SPEC — ONE COMMAND: SCAN PDF → STD SHEETS (item (5) step 1; owner decision 2026-11-23 above; spec BEFORE code)
**Measured first:** the hand-run chain carries 7 values between steps: the plan scale for `raster_roof.py` (72.242 = the reference plan's `chains.json` scale H), and for `raster_section.py` the plan depth (11.99 = the reference plan's READ V overall), the pitch (22 = `roof.json` pitch_text), the outer wall (0.365 = the reference `walls.json` max chain thickness), the plan's probe log, the storey count (2 plan pages) and the view name; plus the page roles and the storey order. The storey order was a human read: the layout matcher's STOREY anchor only checks the CLASS of word (`(ERD|OBER|DACH|KELLER|UNTER)GESCHOSS`) at its place. The word itself is read: Tesseract psm 11 on the upright pages gives p3 `ERDGESCHOSS` (conf 91), p4 `OBERGESCHOSS` (93).
**`scan2dxf.py <scan.pdf> <out_dir>`** runs the existing scripts UNCHANGED, in this order, and carries every value from an earlier step's file (logged `§SCAN_CARRY name=value from=<file>:<key>`); the command line takes nothing else:
1. render every page `pdftoppm -r 200`;
2. classify with `raster_layout.py match types/german_werkplan_layout.json` (kind + rotation per page, UNKNOWN pages skipped and logged; a page at rot ≠ 0 made upright the way `upright` does); each plan's STOREY word (the anchor OCR word, conf ≥ 60) orders the plans by the German sequence KELLER / UNTER < ERD < OBER < DACH; an unread or repeated storey word → INCONCLUSIVE, nothing written;
3. each plan: `raster_probe` → `raster_walls` → `raster_wallscan`; the REFERENCE plan = the one whose chains anchor both ways (`raster_body.anchor_chains`); every other plan `raster_register` → `raster_regwall` → its `_reg` work, then `raster_walls` → `raster_wallscan` there; `raster_openings` + `raster_doors` on the reference and the registered works (as the hand chain);
4. the views page: `raster_probe` → `raster_roof <reference scale H>`;
5. each `SCHNITT` view of `roof.json` in order: `raster_section` with the carried depth / pitch / outer wall / reference probe log / plan count; the first PASS (`section_heights.json` written) is the main section;
6. each registered plan: `raster_annex`; if its ring closes, `raster_annex_section` on the remaining SCHNITT views, first PASS;
7. `raster_emit.py <upper plan work> roof.json <out> <lower plan _reg work> section_heights.json [annex_heights.json]`. Two plan storeys only (the emitter's limit); more → stated, stop.
**Witnesses:** W-S44a the one command's sheets are byte-identical to the hand-run sheets of §43.22 step 3 RESULT 2 on DE-1; W-S44b every carried value is logged with its source file and equals the hand-typed value; W-S44c falsifier: the same run with the storey words made unreadable (the title blocks blanked) stops INCONCLUSIVE with nothing written. `raster_layout.py` gets one additive log line `§LAYOUT_STOREY page=… word=… conf=…`, its other output byte-identical.
**§44 RESULT (2026-11-23; `scan2dxf.py`, run logs `scan2dxf_run1.log` + `out/logs/01..21_*.log`; falsifier logs `scan2dxf_falsifier.log`, `scan2dxf_falsifier2.log`): ONE COMMAND TURNS THE SCAN INTO THE SHEETS.**
- 21 steps, the existing scripts unchanged except one additive log line in `raster_layout.py` (`§LAYOUT_STOREY`; its other output on p3 byte-identical to the §43.13 log). Pages: p1 UNKNOWN (skipped), p2 views, p3 plan @270 (`ERDGESCHOSS`), p4 plan @0 (`OBERGESCHOSS`); reference plan p4 (anchors both ways), p3 registered to it; main section A-A (B-B tried first, no PASS), annex ring closed, annex heights from B-B.
- **W-S44b PASS:** every carried value logged with its source and equal to the hand-typed one: storey order ERD < OBER (layout log), plan scale 72.2417 (p4 `chains.json`), depth 11.99 (p4 GREEN V chain), pitch 22.0 (`roof.json`), outer wall 0.365 (p4 `walls.json`), plan probe log, storeys 2.
- **W-S44a PASS:** all 7 sheets (`plan_eg`, `plan_og`, `elev_N/E/S/W`, `roof`) **byte-identical** to the hand-run sheets of §43.22 step 3 RESULT 2 (garage included).
- **W-S44c PASS (two falsifiers):** p3's storey words whited out → p3 no longer classifies as a plan (the storey word is one of its three anchors) → INCONCLUSIVE, no sheets; p4 twice (both plans `OBERGESCHOSS`) → INCONCLUSIVE "both plans read the same storey level", no sheets.
- Stated limits: two plan storeys (the emitter's), one views page, the German Werkplan type files; Tesseract CLI (the browser port uses tesseract.js, owner decision).
- **▶ RESUME item (5) step 2 (next):** the JS port into bim-ootb `modeller/pdf2dxf/` beside the vector reader, loaded only for a scan; oracle = this command: the port's sheets byte-identical to `scan2dxf.py`'s on DE-1. Read `modeller/pdf2dxf/index.js` + `pdf_reader.js` first (how the vector path refuses a scan today, §41) and measure the scripts' size before choosing the port order (spec first).

### §45 MEASURED BEFORE THE PORT — THE PIXEL SEAM: THE BROWSER CANNOT GET THE ORACLE'S PIXELS, AND THE READER ONLY WORKS ON THOSE (2026-11-23, 'resume 2D', item (5) step 2; spec BEFORE code; nothing in bim-ootb changed)
**Why measured first:** §44's port oracle is "the port's sheets byte-identical to `scan2dxf.py`'s on DE-1". That needs the browser to start from the SAME pixels as the Python chain. Step 1 of `scan2dxf.py` is `pdftoppm -r 200` (poppler). Measured whether a browser can reproduce that.
**Port size (origin/master 29d4f20cc):** 20 `raster_*.py` + `scan2dxf.py` = 2,559 lines, ~210 KB (dense numpy; largest `raster_emit.py` 31 KB, `raster_probe.py` 29 KB). Beyond numpy, the chain calls three things a browser does not have: poppler's render (step 1), PIL resampling (`resize` LANCZOS / BILINEAR, `rotate` BICUBIC for the deskew in `raster_probe.py` l.46, `raster_layout.py` `rotate(expand=True)`), and the Tesseract CLI (5.3.4, `eng.traineddata` 4.1 MB = the fast model; 4 call sites: `raster_probe.py` l.116, `raster_layout.py` l.27, `raster_roof.py` l.33, `raster_blocks.py` l.86). The vector port (bim-ootb `modeller/pdf2dxf/`, 14 files, 1,411 lines) had none of these.
**DE-1's pixels (`pdfimages -list`):** each of the 4 pages is ONE embedded JPEG, 2338 x 3306 RGB at exactly 200 ppi, page `/Rotate 270`. So 200 dpi is the scan's own resolution; no resampling is needed to get the pixels.
**Three decoders of the same JPEG, grey level (L), per page:**
| pair | max | pixels identical | pixels differing > 20 |
|---|---|---|---|
| pdf.js 3.11.174 (the Modeller's vendored copy, `scan_pixels_pdfjs.js`) vs libjpeg (PIL) | 9-12 | 99.05-99.32 % | 0.000 % |
| pdf.js vs poppler `pdftoppm -r 200` (the oracle) | 126-128 | 89.28-95.30 % | 0.76-2.89 % |
**Poppler is the outlier.** Its render is aligned (best row/column offset 0) but smoothed along the image's x (least-squares 5 x 5 fit from pdf.js pixels: centre row 0.17 / 0.66 / 0.17), and NOT a fixed filter: residual rms 7.45 grey levels after the best fit, max 89. A browser cannot reproduce it short of porting poppler's Splash image path (or shipping poppler as WASM) — both against the no-install / minimal-browser rule.
**The reader on the browser's pixels (`scan2dxf.py` unchanged, step 1 fed (a) the stored JPEG decoded by libjpeg, (b) by pdf.js, each turned by `/Rotate`):** both stop at step 4, `§SCAN verdict=INCONCLUSIVE 0 plans anchor both ways`. Poppler run (same code, same day): `§SCAN verdict=WRITTEN` 7 sheets.
- Why: on poppler pixels p4 has **2 GREEN chains of 49 judged** (`§RASTER_W-S4 ... green=2`: H pos 540 `36^5 | 3,51 | 17^5 | 4,69^5 | 11^5 | 3,26^5 | 36^5` = 12.490, V pos 2350 = 11.990) and the scale `§RASTER_W-S5` 72.242 / 72.340 PASS. On pdf.js pixels: **0 GREEN of 51** (H 540 reads `... | 34,917^5 | 3^5~`, V 2350 reads `365^1 | 401~ | ...`; the overall `12,49` line at pos 438 is not found at all), W-S5 INCONCLUSIVE. Everything after (scale, plan body, walls, registration, emit) hangs on those two chains.
- **Not the threshold:** Otsu forced to 157 / 162 / 167 (poppler's) / 172 on pdf.js pixels: green 0 every time.
- **Not curable by a stated pre-filter:** a [1,2,1] or [1,4,1] blur in x, y or both, applied to the grey before everything: green 0 on pdf.js pixels AND **0 on poppler's own pixels** (8 runs). The reader is on a knife edge: any change of the pixels, even a smoothing of the pixels it works on, loses both chains.
**What this means (stated, not softened):** the scan POC reads DE-1 from one exact rendering. It is not yet a reader of the drawing; a port that is byte-identical to it is impossible in a browser, and a port that is not byte-identical has no oracle. The §44 W-S44a "byte-identical" PASS stays true, but it proves determinism of one pixel set, not robustness.
**Spec for the next step (proposed, owner's word wanted on the ORDER only, see the question below):**
- **§45 step A (Python, before any port): the reader must be DECODER-INVARIANT on DE-1.** Gate W-S45a: on poppler, libjpeg and pdf.js pixels the chain reads the SAME READ values (both anchoring chains GREEN, scale within 0.5 %, plan 12.49 x 11.99, and the sheets' READ numbers equal; bytes may differ only where a value is MEASURED, each listed). W-S45b falsifier: a chain with one segment value altered must not turn GREEN (no checksum shopping). Where the fault is: the dimension-number reading (Tesseract on 4x LANCZOS crops of the grey band + the glyph-count / template-bank cross-check, `raster_probe.py` l.111-l.440), not the line / tick finding (chains, ticks and the 15-tick H 591 line are found on all three).
- **§45 step B: canonical pixels = the stored image as pdf.js decodes it,** turned by `/Rotate` (`scan_pixels_pdfjs.js`, the browser's own code; Node and browser run the same JS, so the oracle's input and the port's input are equal by construction). `scan2dxf.py` step 1 switches to it; poppler drops out of the chain.
- **§45 step C: then the port,** stage by stage, each stage's output files byte-equal to the Python stage's on the same input files (so a difference is caught at its stage, not at the sheets). PIL's resampling is ported as a fixed-point equal of PIL's code, or replaced on both sides by a stated simpler resampling; OCR = tesseract.js with the SAME `eng.traineddata` file vendored (not tesseract.js's default CDN model), lazy-loaded; its own seam witness W-S45c: the same words and confidences as the CLI on every crop the chain OCRs, or the differences listed.
**⛔ ONE QUESTION FOR THE OWNER:** go with step A first (make the reader survive the browser's pixels, then the port), instead of porting now? The alternative is to keep scans as a Python-only command (the Modeller page keeps refusing them) until a second scan is in hand.
**How to re-measure (session scratchpad paths are gone in a new session):** export `scripts/pdf2bim` from origin/master; run `scan2dxf.py <scan> <out>` once as is, and once with a stand-in `pdftoppm` first on PATH that writes the pages from `scan_pixels_pdfjs.js` output (rotate the raw RGB by `np.rot90(a, 1)` for `/Rotate 270`); compare `logs/04_probe_p-4.log` `§RASTER_W-S4` / `§RASTER_CHECKSUM` lines.

### §47 SPEC — THE ONE COMMAND RUNS THE CORNER STEP; THE EG's NE CORNER IS A READING GAP (owner 2026-11-24, on the live Modeller: *"The house seems correct shape just missing two corners"*; spec BEFORE code)
**Measured (the owner's Viewer DB, `element_world_bbox`, wall cover of each 365 mm outer corner square):** OG all four corners 36 / 36 covered; **EG NE and SE 0 / 36**, NW and SW 36 / 36. EG: the N wall ends at x 10.225, the S wall at 10.024, the E wall spans y -9.019..-2.31 only (both east corners open, as the owner saw: the house's east side reads as "left" from the usual view).
**Cause 1 — the one command never runs the corner step.** `scan2dxf.py` (§44) has no `raster_notch.py` call, so `notch.json` never exists and `raster_emit.py` draws no `A-WALL-CURT` (the sheet has 0). Run on the EG registered work: `§RASTER_NOTCH corner=SE … ring_beyond_empty=True/True -> notch=True`, glazing H (9763,8798)-(12125,8919) 121 mm and V (9763,8919)-(9903,11625) 141 mm, post NOT BUILT (§43.19 stop rule); `§RASTER_W-S20 corners_tested=4 notches_found=1` PASS. On the OG: 0 notches (VACUOUS, no corner short).
**Cause 2 — the EG NE corner is NOT a notch:** `corner=NE N-wall stops 2265 mm short, E-wall 2310 mm short ring_beyond_empty=False/False -> notch=False`: ink continues beyond both traced ends, so wall is drawn there and the tracing missed it. A reading gap, open (§47 step 2, below).
**Step 1 (now):** `scan2dxf.py` runs `raster_notch.py` on every plan work (reference and registered) after the walls and before the emit, logged as steps; nothing else changes. Witnesses: W-S47a the emitted `plan_eg.dxf` carries the 2 `A-WALL-CURT` runs of the notch log (to 1 mm); W-S47b through the importer (live library) the set still qualifies and the EG SE corner square is covered by the curtain walls in the model; W-S47c the OG sheet byte-identical to before (no notch there).
**Step 2 (open, spec to follow):** the EG NE corner: why `raster_walls` / `raster_wallscan` stop 2.3 m short where ink continues (a door / window gap at the corner, hatch break, or the registration's edge); measured first, never closed by drawing a wall the scan does not show.
**§47 step 1 RESULT (2026-11-24):** `scan2dxf.py` logs `§SCAN_NOTCH work=p-4 notch.json=yes` (0 notches) and `work=p-3_reg notch.json=yes`; **W-S47a PASS** `plan_eg.dxf` `A-WALL-CURT: 2` written = read back; **W-S47c PASS** the other 6 sheets byte-identical to the run without the step. **W-S47b PASS on the LIVE library** (`witness_dxf_scan_set_page.js`, sha 5510b96bbf80): qualifies (82 accepted), Convert 93 inserts (+2 curtain walls) / 30 / 30, Viewer DB 123 rows, the Viewer "DONE — STD 93 elements", 0 page errors; in the DB the glazing closes the EG ring: S wall (to x 10.024) → IfcCurtainWall x 9.763..9.903, y -11.625..-8.919 → IfcCurtainWall y -8.919..-8.798, x 9.763..12.125 → E wall (from y -9.019). The outer SE corner square stays open BY THE DRAWING (the terrace, outside the glass); measuring "corner square covered" was the wrong test for a notch, restated here. **Open: step 2, the EG NE corner (a reading gap, walls 2.3 m short with ink beyond).**
**OWNER FACTS 2026-11-24 (on the live model, before §47 step 1 reached him):** *"Back corner, yes it is an open corner, but it should have then enveloped walls. Front have no front door, sides are full window doors."* Read against the measurements:
- **Back corner = the EG SE terrace notch:** enveloping walls are now there (§47 step 1: 2 curtain walls close the ring; the terrace stays outside). Whether they are glazing or solid wall is what `raster_notch.py` measured (long straight lines = glazing, §43.19); stated, not re-judged here.
- **§47 step 2 — the front door (EG NE corner):** the reading gap there (N wall 2265 mm short, E wall 2310 mm short, ink beyond both ends, `notch=False`) is where the owner says the front door is missing: first suspect a recessed entrance (walls set back + the door), the door undetected by `raster_doors.py` (EG recall already weak, §43.21). Measure the ink in that corner first (lines, arcs, hatch), then trace; nothing drawn that the scan does not show.

Needs the opening heights READ from the views page (the elevations or Schnitt A-A's k 51.001 px/m, already measured) per side, then families of that size (migration + library publish, as CL009-CL014).

**§47 CORRELATED WITH THE SOURCE (2026-11-24, owner: *"Do corelate with the original source rather than invent"*): the EG scan page (p3 upright, 200 dpi) read at the three places, against the reader's logs:**
- **Front door = the N-wall opening at centre 5730 mm, 1677 mm wide** (`14_doors_p-3_reg.log`: `exterior face=N centre_mm=5730 width=1677 … contrast=0.20 cov=0.35 … -> WINDOW`). The sheet draws a door there: the swing arc is DASHED, under the canopy box labelled `EINGANG` (page px ≈ 900..985 at k ≈ 51 px/m from the house's west face ≈ 4.8..6.5 m). The door test needs a mostly solid arc (cov 0.35 fails), so a dashed swing reads as a window. **Fix to spec: a dashed arc (gaps at a regular period along the circle) counts as a swing, measured on this door; falsifier: a window's sash lines must still read WINDOW.**

The traced gaps (N 2265, E 2310 mm) are exactly these glazing runs. **Fix to spec: the corner step treats a corner whose short runs are drawn as thin parallel glazing lines (not hatch) as a glazed corner: curtain wall / window runs along the traced gap, its door swing as a door; the sill / heights stay DEFAULT until the BRH digits are read (a 400 dpi rescan, §43.6).**

The `BRH` labels beside them are where the sheet states the sill; not readable at 200 dpi. Not changed until READ (rescan, or the elevations' heights).
- **No stairs (owner 2026-11-24: *"And no stairs. You may check from the saved DB also"*):** the owner's Viewer DB has 0 IfcStair (classes: wall 55, curtain wall 2, slab 4, door 10, window 20, roof 2, opening 30). **The source draws one, legibly:** EG plan, a half-turn stair with winders round a central hatched spine, labelled `16 STG 17,9/27,3` (16 risers, rise 17.9 cm, going 27.3 cm), run dimension `2,58`. **Correlation:** 16 x 0.179 = 2.864 m vs the OG level READ-GEOM from Schnitt A-A 2.855 m: 9 mm, i.e. the label and the section agree. **Neither side reads stairs today:** the scan chain has no stair step; the importer EXCLUDES `A-FLOR-STRS` ("the reader has no stair or column shape yet", bim-ootb `dxf2shell_config.js` l.41); the IFC writer can already write `IfcStairFlight` (§D109). **§47 step 4 (spec to follow, three parts):** (a) scan: the stair outline + the `STG` label read (count, rise, going; the label's digits checked against the storey height, as above); (b) emitter: `A-FLOR-STRS` outline + a stair mark carrying count / rise / going; (c) importer (bim-ootb lane): a stair body from that mark (flights + winders, or one stated simplification), `IfcStair` / `IfcStairFlight`.

### §48 SPEC — A DOOR WITH A SIDE LIGHT: THE ENTRANCE (owner 2026-11-24: *"Yes"* to the front door first, *"Ok"* to (a) door + side light as drawn; spec BEFORE code)
**Measured (probe on the EG registered work, the door step's own frame, every hinge position along the opening, r 0.30..1.05 x width):** the N opening at 5730 mm (1677 mm measured, page px 896.5..980.0, K 49.78 px/m) holds an arc of contrast **0.634** (cov 0.634) at r **54 px = 1085 mm**, hinge 40 mm from the opening's west jamb, swing inward (side +1); the five best fits all hinge at the west jamb with r 1024..1085 mm. The arc is DASHED (ink along 10..80°: `################.###..##......###.....##...######........######.#######`). The sheet's door threshold is 0.59 (`§RASTER_W-S21b` null max 0.49 + 0.10): **the swing clears it; it was missed only because `arc()` searches r 0.80..1.05 x the OPENING width (1342..1761 mm)**. The pixel view of the opening (source, 6x): the leaf from the west jamb to ≈ page px 956 with its handle mark, then a fixed glazed panel (two thin lines) to the east jamb ≈ 987: a door + a side light.
**Rule (raster_doors.py, generic, data-driven):** an EXTERIOR candidate that is not a door by the existing test is tried once more with the leaf narrower than the opening: r from 0.80 x the smallest DIN 18101 single leaf (`door_series.single_leaf_width_mm`) up to 0.80 x the opening width (exclusive), hinge at either jamb as today; its own null population = the same solid-wall probes searched over the same absolute r range, threshold = their max + 0.10 (≤ 0.60, as §43.21). If it clears: `kind = DOOR`, `leaf_mm` = the DIN single leaf nearest the arc's r, `sidelight_mm` = the opening's emitted width − `leaf_mm`, on the side away from the hinge. Every other candidate is judged exactly as today.
**Emitter (raster_emit.py):** a door with `leaf_mm`: the arc radius = `leaf_mm` at its hinge jamb (family by `fid_of` as every door); the side light = one `A-GLAZ` box over the rest of the opening in the wall band + `@TAG=window,<family>` + `@SCHEDULE=<family>,window,<sidelight_mm>,<door height>,0` (sill 0 and the door's height: DEFAULT, the side light shares the door frame; stated in the log). Families of the new sizes by migration (CL015), as CL009-CL014.
**Witnesses:** W-S48a the entrance is DOOR with `leaf_mm` + `sidelight_mm` logged; **falsifier W-S48b: every other exterior opening keeps its kind** (the 8 EG windows stay WINDOW, no new DOOR on the OG), and the narrow-range null threshold ≤ 0.60 (else VACUOUS, nothing reclassified); W-S48c through the importer the door and the side light are two fills in ONE void (the Modeller groups openings per wall gap), the set still qualifies; W-S48d the house elements otherwise unchanged (class + box to 1 mm).
**§48 RESULT (2026-11-24; logs `/tmp/claude-1000/s48/`: `doors_p-3_reg.log`, `doors_p-4.log`, `run3/logs/*`, `lib_probe2.log`, `w_libpub.log`, `pub_verify.log`): THE ENTRANCE IS A DOOR + A SIDE LIGHT IN THE MODEL.**

### §49 PROPOSED DESIGN — THE EXPECTATION CHECKLIST: WHAT A BUILDING TYPE IMPLIES, LOOKED FOR, NEVER INVENTED (owner 2026-11-24: *"When a house is multi storey we can then note there must be a staircase. Our markings should look out and get the staircase shape dimensions. This should be part of some standard template checklist concept. Workout what's a good design as such."*; spec only, nothing built)
**What exists (build on it, no fourth mechanism):** (1) the TYPE TEMPLATE (§33: preset + blanks, every value READ / FINDING / DEFAULT; `types/low_cost_single_storey_unit.json`, `types/german_werkplan_efh.json`); (2) `type_checklist.py` (§12: the sheet's own schedule = EXPECTED counts vs the tags FOUND); (3) the layout fingerprint (§43.13: what a sheet of this type looks like). Missing: rules of the form "**if** the set shows X, **then** it must also contain Y". Today a missing stair, front door or terrace door is noticed only by the owner looking at the model.
**The unit: one expectation = data, not code.**
```
{ "id": "stair_per_storey_pair",
  "when":   "storeys >= 2",                          // over READ facts only (storeys, garage, terrace notch, roof form ...)
  "expect": { "class": "IfcStair", "count": "storeys - 1", "per": "adjacent storey pair" },
  "look":   { "lower_plan": "tread lines inside a closed outline", "upper_plan": "a void at the same registered place",
              "label": "<n> STG <rise>/<going>" },   // where and what the reader looks for (German Werkplan wording)
  "measure":["outline box", "run direction", "flights / winders", "n risers", "rise", "going"],
  "check":  [ "n x rise = storey height (READ-GEOM, §43.20) within 1 unit of the label",
              "2 x rise + going in 590..650 mm (DIN 18065 Schrittmaßregel)",
              "drawn tread lines = n - 1 per flight" ],
  "source": "DIN 18065; owner 2026-11-24" }
```
**Verdict per expectation, one line each (`§EXPECT id=… verdict=…`), the witness grammar of this lane:** `NOT-EXPECTED` (the `when` is false: a single-storey set has no stair rule), `READ` (found and measured, every check PASS), `READ-FINDING` (found, a check fails: stated, the sheet wins), `FOUND-UNREAD` (the evidence is there, e.g. tread lines, but a value is not legible: the gap is named), `MISSING` (expected, nothing found where it was looked for: a stated finding, NEVER filled with a default body). A checklist where nothing was judged prints `INCONCLUSIVE`.
**Where it lives (recommendation):** ONE rules file per building type beside its template (`types/<type>_expect.json`, inheriting a common base `types/expect_common.json`: storeys ⇒ stairs, ground storey ⇒ an entrance door, a garage ⇒ an overhead door, a covered terrace ⇒ a door to it, each room ⇒ a door). The SAME file is read twice: by the scan reader (to know where to look and what to measure) and by the Modeller's qualifier (bim-ootb, as advisory rows `QF:expected_missing` / `QF:expected_unread` on the panel), so a CAD set from any source gets the same list. The panel shows "Expected by the building type: found / unread / missing" under the accepted rows.
**First rule to build: the stair (the owner's example), on DE-1, measured from the source already (§47):** lower plan = a half-turn stair with winders round a central spine, label `16 STG 17,9/27,3`, run `2,58`; checks: 16 x 0.179 = 2.864 vs 2.855 (READ-GEOM) 9 mm = within one label unit; 2 x 179 + 273 = 631 mm in 590..650: PASS. Chain: `raster_stair.py` (scan: the outline, tread lines, the label by OCR, the upper plan's void) → emitter `A-FLOR-STRS` + `@STAIR=<n>,<rise>,<going>,<form>` → bim-ootb importer: a stair body (IfcStair / IfcStairFlight; the writer already has IfcStairFlight, §D109) instead of excluding `A-FLOR-STRS` (`dxf2shell_config.js` l.41).
**Witnesses (when built):** W-S49a DE-1: `stair_per_storey_pair` READ with the three checks PASS; W-S49b falsifier: a single-storey set (TB-LKTN) → NOT-EXPECTED, never MISSING; W-S49c the label blanked → FOUND-UNREAD (tread lines still found), never READ; W-S49d the same rules file run by the Modeller's qualifier on the emitted sheets gives the same verdict.

Importer side: bim-ootb §L43.
**Witnesses:** **W-S49a PASS** `§EXPECT id=stair_per_storey_pair pair=ERDGESCHOSS->OBERGESCHOSS verdict=READ` with `risers_times_rise_vs_storey` 16 x 179 = 2864 vs FFL-to-FFL 2875 (levels 0 → 2855, build-ups 145 / 165) d −11 tol 27.6 (n x 0.5 + one section px), `step_rule_din18065` 631 in 590..650, `walk_line_length_vs_treads` 4116 vs 4095 d +21 tol 35.1; **W-S49b PASS** one storey → NOT-EXPECTED, summary INCONCLUSIVE; **W-S49c PASS** no dictionary → MISSING (nothing drawn); a dictionary for another sha → refused → MISSING. Sheets: only `plan_eg` changes (+3 `A-FLOR-STRS`, +1 mark), W-S12a PASS.
**In the model (bim-ootb §L43, local code, LIVE library 175e0d52cffd):** one `IfcStairFlight` (`.WINDER.`) on Level 1, box x 5246..7447, y −7432..−5108, z 0..2677 (15 treads; the 16th riser lands on the OG level 2855; model rise 178.4 = 2855 / 16, the label's 179 checked above), 0 validation statements about it; the page: qualifies, Convert 95 / 31 / 31, Viewer database 126 rows with the stair, the Viewer "DONE — STD 95 elements", 0 page errors.

### §51 SPEC — WALLS FROM THE SHEETS' CORRELATION: THE UPPER PLAN SAYS WHERE TO LOOK, THE LOWER SCAN'S OWN INK DECIDES (owner 2026-11-24: *"can't a floor be inferred from those sheets corelations?"* … *"yes, build both"*; spec BEFORE code; the stairwell is bim-ootb §L44)
**Measured (`/tmp/claude-1000/s51/`):** 53 OG READ walls; 24 are traced on the EG too, 29 not. Two evidence measures on the EG page (its registered frame), each inside the OG wall's band: ink share, and **face cover** = the share of the wall's length where BOTH long faces carry ink (±2 px). Joint score `J = min(face cover, ink share)`. Its null = the same bands shifted 0.5 / 0.75 / 1.0 m across into the house where neither plan has a wall (≥ 150 mm clear). **Short pieces poison the null** (the two highest nulls, 0.32 m and 0.25 m bands, land on real untraced EG walls), so the rule judges walls ≥ 0.6 m only: null 28 probes, max 0.43 → **threshold 0.53 = null max + 0.10** (the door rule's margin, §43.21), the same at ≥ 0.9 m. **Calibration on the 21 walls traced on both:** 15 clear it (the reader is conservative, not loose).
**Rule (`raster_stack.py <upper work> <lower registered work>`):** candidates = upper READ walls ≥ 0.6 m not overlapping a lower traced wall; READ = `J ≥` threshold (the sheet's own null, ≤ 0.60 else VACUOUS, nothing added); the wall takes the UPPER wall's rectangle (position, thickness: stated, `src: STACKED`), the lower scan only says it is there. Writes `<lower>/walls_stacked.json`; the emitter adds them to the lower plan's walls.
**On DE-1 (predicted from the measurement):** READ 3: the stair spine (V x 6264..6437, y 5446..6525, J 0.74), the stair's right wall (V x 7447..7613, y 5446..7465, 0.66), H x 1190..2312, y 5163..5266 (0.67); out: 18 with J ≤ 0.11, and the middle band 0.25..0.39 (stated per wall). Stated limit: the spine taken from the OG plan runs to y 6525, ~100 mm past the EG stair's turn (6428).
**Witnesses:** W-S51a the 3 READ with their J; W-S51b the calibration line (traced walls clearing the threshold, of N); **W-S51c falsifier**: every candidate shifted by +0.75 m → 0 READ; W-S51d through the importer: the set still qualifies, the 3 walls on Level 1, the house elements otherwise unchanged.
**§51 RESULT (2026-11-24; logs `/tmp/claude-1000/s51/`: `stack.log`, `run/logs/*`, `lp.log`):** as specified and predicted. `§RASTER_STACK null probes=28 … max=0.43 p95=0.38 threshold=0.53 verdict=PASS`; **W-S51b** calibration 15 of 21; **W-S51a** READ = 3 of 17 candidates (the spine J 0.739, H x 1190..2312 J 0.667, the stair's right wall J 0.657); **W-S51c PASS** shifted +750 mm → READ = 0. `scan2dxf.py` step 6d runs it; `raster_emit.py` writes them (`§RASTER_EMIT_STACKED`, position + thickness from the OG plan, presence from the EG ink): only `plan_eg` changes (A-WALL-FULL 33 → 36, W-S12a PASS). **W-S51d PASS** (with bim-ootb §L44): `§LIB blocking 0`, 177 elements = the 174 before (each class + box to 1 mm) + exactly the 3 walls (z 0..2.630). Stated: the spine wall (from the OG plan, to y 6525) overlaps the EG stair's winders by ~0.1 m in plan (the EG stair turns at 6428); the 14 candidates out stay out, listed in `stack.log`.

**§51 note — the Pages publish (2026-11-24, owner: *"yes, split the workflow"*):** bim-ootb `deploy-pages.yml` failed 6 times (2026-11-23 14:19 / 15:58, 2026-11-24 02:40 / 02:58 / 03:09 / 03:17 UTC) with `No artifacts named "github-pages" were found` seconds after a finalized 270 MB upload in the SAME job (GitHub status operational; other sessions' publishes failed alike). ### §52 SPEC — THE OWNER'S WALK OF THE LIVE MODEL, 2026-11-24 (five items; spec BEFORE code; logs `/tmp/claude-1000/s52/`)
**Owner, 2026-11-24:** *"There are columns in middle of some rooms on top level which should not be. Thus check what causes them and if it is a pattern of something else. There are also markings of toilet/sink perhaps can insert mesh from library. Try some walking. The corner to the left of front facade still uncovered where the kitchen is. The other openings around ground floor should default to full length door as in German custom"* … *"openings should be glass material to see thru"* … *"only that one missing lower ground corner of kitchen. Its back is accurate, not missing, a cut out, open area under the 2nd level as cover"* (= the SE terrace notch of §47 is right; only the EG NE kitchen corner is open).
**Order of work:** §52.1 the stubs → §52.2 EG openings as full-height window doors → §52.3 glass → §52.4 the kitchen corner → §52.5 sanitary fixtures. "Try some walking" is read as a walk of the model by code, room by room (PRIMAL LAW: no eyes): W-S52a below is that walk.

#### §52.1 THE "COLUMNS" ARE ANNOTATION INK READ AS WALLS — measured, and the pattern
**Measured (owner's DB `DE-1_scan_viewer.db`, `element_world_bbox`):** 14 OG `IfcWall` bodies are < 0.8 m on both plan axes, full storey height (z 2.86..6.12). Mapped back onto the OG scan (`walls_all.json`, 68 walls: 53 READ + 15 MEASURED), the short and the odd ones sit on: the dimension figures `31⁵ 20 67 11⁵` and their ticks in the Bad; the rules of the `BAD` room-stamp box; the door-stop marks at the Flur and WA doors; the sink counter outlines in the right bath; the stair-void edges (`LUFTRAUM` edge, `BRÜSTUNG NACH ANGABE`, the spine through the void); the open door leaves at the two Flur doors (drawn as thin rectangles).
**Cause:** `raster_wallscan.py` judges a band of ≤ 9 px (≤ 0.125 m at this print, i.e. every 115 mm partition) by its two faces alone (§43.1 rule "thin bands cannot show hatch"), and accepts any run ≥ 2 x thickness (0.23 m); connectivity then joins annotation to annotation once one piece touches a wall. **The §43.1 premise is wrong on this sheet:** the 115 mm partitions DO show the hatch.
**The pattern: every cut wall on this sheet is hatched at ONE period.** Along a wall's interior, ink runs start every **8 px** (median, every one of the 27 chain-anchored walls, sd ≤ 1.4 px) and the starts move by ±2 px between lines 2 px apart (45° hatch). Score = (share of the line covered by starts at that period, ±2 px) × (share of starts continued diagonally on the line 2 px over), best line of the band. The 27 chain walls score **≥ 0.62**; the annotation pieces score 0.00..0.48 (text 0.24..0.44, stamp rules 0.00..0.36, door stops 0.00..0.40, void edges 0.00).
**Rule (`raster_wallscan.py`, generic, every plan):** the period P = median start spacing on the chain walls' centrelines (the sheet's own hatch); a whole-plan candidate is a wall only if its hatch score ≥ (min score of the chain walls) − 0.10 (the lane's margin, §43.21); the test runs BEFORE connectivity, so annotation cannot bridge. Fewer than 5 chain walls, or a calibration min < 0.40 → VACUOUS: the old rule is kept and stated.
**Witnesses:** W-S52a **the room walk**: per storey, bodies < 0.8 m on both plan axes that do not touch a wall ≥ 0.8 m long, listed with position (expected 0 on the OG after the rule); W-S52b falsifier: W-S9a recall of the chain walls stays 27/27 (OG) and as before on the EG; W-S52c every removed candidate is logged (`§RASTER_SCAN_NOHATCH`) with its score; W-S52d through the importer the set still qualifies, and the only element changes are walls removed.
**Stated loss (measured before code):** real hatched pieces that are short or broken by a symbol may score below the threshold (the Bad's installation wall left box 0.48; the chimney square by the stair 0.38/0.44: its flue circle breaks the hatch). They are listed by W-S52c, not re-added by hand.

#### §52.2 EG OPENINGS: FULL-HEIGHT WINDOW DOORS (Fenstertüren) BY DEFAULT
**Owner ruling 2026-11-24 (German custom):** the ground-floor exterior openings default to full-length window doors. Today they are sill 0.9 / height 1.2 (registry DEFAULT, §43 READ vs DEFAULT; the `BRH` sill digits are not readable at 200 dpi). **Rule:** an EG (lowest storey) exterior window whose sill is not READ takes sill 0 and the storey's door height (MEASURED, §43.21 step 3: EG 2.275), source `DEFAULT (owner 2026-11-24: Fenstertür, German custom)`; OG windows unchanged (the OG `BRH 1.02` labels are readable). The garage (an annex, not the house's ground storey) unchanged. Families of the new sizes by migration (as CL009-CL016). **Witnesses:** W-S52e every EG house window sill 0 / height 2275 in the sheets and the model, each logged; W-S52f OG + garage openings byte-identical; the set qualifies.

#### §52.3 GLASS
**Measured:** every element of the owner's DB carries `material_rgba 0.920,0.900,0.850,1.000` (the extractor's fallback for an unstyled body: `ifc_extract_core.js` l.553), windows and curtain walls included, so they render solid in the Viewer and the Modeller (both already draw a body see-through when its alpha < 1: `viewer/streaming.js` A._getMaterial, `modeller/bonsai_kernel.js` §MAT-PARITY). The library holds glass extracted from a real IFC: `surface_styles` `Glass - Clear, Grey` rgb (0.988, 1.0, 1.0) transparency 0.64, `EXTRACTED:Ifc2x3_Duplex_Federated.ifc`. **Rule (bim-ootb DXF2BIM lane):** IfcWindow and IfcCurtainWall bodies of the DXF import are written with that glass style (IfcSurfaceStyleRendering with Transparency), so the extractor reads alpha 0.36. **Witnesses:** W-S52g in the exported Viewer DB every IfcWindow / IfcCurtainWall row has alpha < 1 and every other class alpha 1; falsifier: a set with no window → 0 glass rows, nothing else changes.

#### §52.4 THE KITCHEN CORNER (EG NE) — §47 step 2
**Measured (§47):** the N wall stops 2265 mm and the E wall 2310 mm short of the NE corner, ink beyond both; the source draws both runs as thin double lines (glazing) with a door swing in the N run, `RS` tag boxes and `BRH` labels: a glazed corner. **Rule (`raster_notch.py`, generic):** a corner whose two short runs carry straight parallel lines (the §43.19 glazing test) but no hatch is a GLAZED CORNER: one curtain-wall run along each traced gap, as the §43.19 notch (thickness = the measured line pair), the door swing in it read by the door step. **Witnesses:** W-S52h the NE corner square covered by the two glazing runs in the model; falsifier: OG 0 glazed corners, the SE notch unchanged.

#### §52.5 SANITARY FIXTURES FROM THE DRAWING'S OWN SYMBOLS, LIBRARY MESHES
**Measured:** both plans draw WC bowls, wash basins, a bath tub, a shower; the library holds real meshes (`component_definitions` type IfcFlowTerminal FIXTURE: `WC Toilet` 0.512 x 0.799 x 0.787, `MRV basic round sink` 0.61 x 0.505 x 0.545, `Bath_Tub_1525x759`, `Shower_Rectangular_865x814`; all extracted). The Modeller's DXF importer places no fixture today (`A-FURN` ignored, `dxf2shell_config.js` l.16). **Plan:** (a) scan: a reader of the closed symbol outlines (position, size, facing from the wall it backs onto), every value a pixel measurement; (b) emitter: a `P-SANR-FIXT` layer with one mark per fixture `@FIXTURE=<kind>,<w>,<d>`; (c) importer (bim-ootb lane): the library mesh nearest in size per kind, IfcSanitaryTerminal, on its storey. Spec of (a) written when measured; nothing placed where no symbol is drawn (the disc walker places by room rules, which would invent positions the drawing already states).
